# Fight for Peace Campaign Story Line

This document is the central continuity reference for the *Fight for Peace* campaign. It consolidates the completed story design for Stages 1 and 2, the approved connections between them, and the current concepts for Stage 3 and the longer campaign. It exists to keep future stages consistent, preserve earlier consequences, and prevent a later twist from invalidating facts already shown to the player.

## 1. Story status and authority

The following labels apply throughout this document:

- **Canon:** Approved story content that Stages 1 and 2 must preserve.
- **Planned:** A direction approved for the future campaign, but not yet converted into a full stage specification.
- **Concept:** A promising possibility that still requires refinement before it becomes binding.
- **Unresolved:** A question that must not be answered implicitly during production.
- **Production detail:** Dialogue, shot composition, localized wording, exact timing, final artwork, casting, or another presentation value that may change without changing the story.

When sources disagree, the latest approved stage specification controls completed-stage events. This campaign document controls cross-stage continuity. New stage specifications should update this file whenever they add a lasting fact, character state, mystery clue, time jump, or world change.

## 2. Campaign identity

### 2.1 Central theme

**Canon:** The heroes fight to restore or protect peace, not to conquer territory, seek revenge, or execute defeated opponents. Strength is justified by protection, rescue, restraint, accountability, and the defense of shared prosperity.

Every stage should therefore contain:

1. A self-contained local threat and a specific reason to intervene.
2. People, resources, knowledge, or community systems worth protecting.
3. A complete local resolution rather than a cliffhanger that makes the stage feel unfinished.
4. A consequence that changes the village or wider region.
5. A small reliable clue or development that advances the longer campaign.

### 2.2 Canonical story spine and hero modules

**Canon:** Each stage has one authoritative threat, objective, boss conflict, and outcome. Selected heroes may add personal motives, dialogue, reactions, and co-op exchanges, but hero selection does not create contradictory versions of the main plot. In co-op, both heroes have equal narrative standing.

Heroes are unique people from the village. Duplicate versions of one hero cannot appear on the same team. New stages may add heroes, but do not replace or retroactively diminish the founding protectors.

### 2.3 The elder

**Canon:** The wise elder is the village chief and the campaign's principal analyst, planner, and moral guide. He studies threats, attempts peaceful or accountable solutions where possible, establishes the heroes' objectives, and explains why a fight has become necessary. His visual identity is based on the creator's supplied personal reference.

The elder is perceptive but not infallible. Stage 2 demonstrates this balance: offering care to an injured stranger is morally correct, while allowing access without sufficient verification creates risk. His later decisions must continue to combine compassion with evidence, preparation, and responsibility.

### 2.4 Presentation and localization

**Canon:** English is the default language. Simplified Chinese and German are required. Story artwork and generated video contain no embedded language-dependent text. Titles, dialogue, narration, subtitles, captions, warnings, and transcripts are rendered as localized interface layers.

Stage introductions and major transitions may use skippable generated video, but every required story beat also has a still-art fallback, localized recap, and transcript. No player must watch a video or hear speech to understand the story.

### 2.5 Approved localized stage titles

| Stage | English | Simplified Chinese | German |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The Broken Water Pact | 破裂的水源盟约 | Der gebrochene Wasserpakt |
| 2 | Prosperity Under Siege | 繁荣危局 | Wohlstand unter Belagerung |
| 3 | Shadows over the Herb Mountain | 药山阴影 | Schatten über dem Kräuterberg |
| 4 | The Weight of Inheritance | 传承之重 | Die Last des Erbes |
| 5 | The Bell Before Dawn | 黎明前的钟声 | Die Glocke vor der Morgendämmerung |
| 6 | The Price of Leverage | 筹码的代价 | Der Preis des Druckmittels |
| 7 | The Hidden Ledger | 隐秘账簿 | Das verborgene Hauptbuch |

Stage 7's visible title deliberately avoids “Meridian” because the Compact's name is not proven until that stage concludes. Stage 8 carries the game's title because it resolves the campaign's central philosophy rather than merely naming the final enemy. Stage 2 shares its title with its approved musical identity, linking the chapter's theme and score without changing its locked story.

## 3. Campaign chronology at a glance

| Period | Status | Principal development |
|---|---|---|
| Before Stage 1 | Canon foundation | The river village depends heavily on shared water infrastructure. The founding heroes train to protect their community. An unknown sponsor begins operating through intermediaries. |
| Stage 1 | Canon | An armed faction occupies the river gate, diverts the water, and coerces workers. The heroes free the workers, defeat the Sluice Commander, restore the river, and recover the first sponsor evidence. |
| Three-year interval | Canon | Water security becomes resilient prosperity. The village builds reserves, greenhouses, aquaponics, education, healthcare, civic infrastructure, and a civil-protection program. |
| Stage 2 | Canon | Tomas Rell enters through a staged rescue and enables Marek Vane's raid on the greenhouse complex. The raid fails, Marek is captured, Tomas is expelled, and his genuine formula enters controlled research. A second sponsor clue proves a shared supply network. |
| First 18 months after Stage 2 | Approved | Researchers authenticate the records, refine the fictional preparation, conduct preliminary mouse studies, receive external scientific review, and secure a substantial research grant. Early modernization continues without transforming the whole village at once. |
| Stage 3, approximately 18 months after Stage 2 | Approved specification | A required rare mountain herb draws experienced villagers into territory infiltrated by bandits. Several villagers are kidnapped. Tomas has joined the bandits and helps prepare revenge and future raids. The patrol enters the mountains to rescue the captives. |
| Middle campaign | Planned concepts | Across the next two years, Tomas's former wife and son expose how love, prejudice, fraud, custody manipulation, and inherited revenge shaped the family. The current elder confronts his incomplete resistance to the previous generation's collective punishment. Character Chronicles and a historical main-story stage reveal the past while the sponsor mystery advances. Village infrastructure, transport, clothing, energy, healthcare, and protective technology evolve visibly between stages. |
| Final campaign, approximately four years after Stage 2 | Planned concept | The substantially transformed village uses advanced but humane infrastructure, smart homes, renewable energy, robots, wearable assistance, protective materials, and structured modern transport. The Compact is exposed and confronted without implying that technology alone created peace. |
| Final campaign state | Planned direction | The conflicts ultimately end in peace. The conclusion should resolve the human cycle of betrayal and revenge as well as the external sponsor mystery. |

## 4. Stage 1 The Broken Water Pact

### 4.1 Situation before the attack

**Canon:** The village and surrounding communities depend on a shared river gate, mill, canals, and irrigation system. Water is not merely scenery; it sustains homes, fields, work, and the village's future.

An armed faction seizes the shared river gate, diverts the village's water, and coerces local workers. Drying fields and interrupted irrigation place both the captives and the wider village in immediate danger.

### 4.2 The elder's decision

**Canon:** The elder first analyzes the occupation and attempts negotiation. Only after negotiation fails does he ask the two founding heroes, Mountain Guardian and River Scout, to intervene.

Their objective is explicit:

- Free the coerced workers.
- Restore the water supply.
- Prevent further harm to civilians and infrastructure.
- End the occupation without turning the mission into revenge or territorial conquest.

### 4.3 Founding heroes

**Canon:** Mountain Guardian and River Scout are working archetype titles; their final personal names remain unresolved.

- **Mountain Guardian** is a calm, grounded protector associated with village craft, waterworks, close control, stable defense, and deliberate power.
- **River Scout** is a warm, alert messenger and river-path guide associated with mobility, aerial pursuit, repositioning, and rapid response.

Both are complete solo heroes. Their relationship demonstrates the campaign's cooperative ideal: different strengths can protect the same peace without one hero becoming subordinate to the other.

### 4.4 Stage progression

**Canon:** The playable journey follows eight story and combat beats:

1. **Dry fields:** The heroes see the direct cost of the diverted water and confront the first occupiers.
2. **Occupied bridge:** They break through lane-control formations and advance toward the captured workers.
3. **Worker rescue:** The first civilians are freed and the scale of the occupation becomes clearer.
4. **Working mill:** The heroes cross active waterworks and machinery while avoiding unnecessary damage to systems the village will still need.
5. **Investigation and preparation:** A brief safe period allows the heroes to assess the route and prepare for the final approach.
6. **Canal route:** Mixed enemy roles, ranged pressure, and coordinated attacks defend the route to the gate.
7. **Gate courtyard:** Elite defenders make a final attempt to preserve the occupation.
8. **Sluice Commander:** The commander uses the game's complete standing, aerial, ground, defense, weapon, environmental, and boss-Resolve vocabulary.

Coerced workers remain noncombatants. Enemies who surrender or flee are no longer legal targets.

### 4.5 Resolution

**Canon:** The heroes defeat the Sluice Commander, who may surrender or be safely captured according to the final encounter presentation. The workers are secured, the river gate is restored, and water returns to the village. Stage 1 resolves its complete local conflict before introducing the campaign mystery.

### 4.6 First sponsor clue

**Canon:** Evidence recovered at the gate shows that an unknown outside power financed, equipped, or supplied the occupation. The reliable forensic clue consists of:

- A damaged but recognizable shipment seal found on the Black-Iron Enforcer's equipment.
- An incomplete order code associated with the supplied equipment.

This proves outside support but does not reveal whether the sponsor is an individual, government, corporation, criminal organization, or another kind of power. The clue is automatically recorded in the Sponsor Dossier after Stage 1.

## 5. The three-year Prosperity Interlude

### 5.1 Lasting consequence of Stage 1

**Canon:** Approximately three years pass between the restoration of the river and the beginning of Stage 2. The victory creates lasting development rather than a brief celebration.

The village works collectively to become less dependent on a single river source. It builds reservoirs, rainwater storage, expanded irrigation, and related reserves. The community reinvests a significant portion of its new wealth in education, healthcare, engineering, agriculture, workshops, civic services, and future protectors.

### 5.2 Agriculture and research

**Canon:** The village develops scientifically plausible hydroponic and aquaponic greenhouses. Commercial systems use distinct fish or freshwater-prawn tanks, filtration, biological treatment, pumps, aeration, and connected plant beds or channels. A smaller demonstration system may show plants above oxygenated water with fish and protected freshwater prawns below.

The village produces vegetables, crops, fish, freshwater prawns, and selected medicinal or aromatic plants. The clinic tests medicinal plants rather than assuming that natural substances are automatically safe or effective.

### 5.3 Social and physical development

**Canon:** New homes, a school, a hospital or expanded clinic, workshops, greenhouses, and a civic headquarters transform the settlement without erasing its river-and-mountain identity. Clothing becomes cleaner, better tailored, more colorful, more durable, and better suited to professional and protective work.

Prosperity also leads to a civilian civil-protection and peacekeeping program. It trains selected young adults in rescue, first aid, evacuation, de-escalation, nonlethal restraint, modern martial arts, vehicle handling, and accountable equipment safety. It is not an army of conquest.

### 5.4 Interlude presentation

**Canon:** A skippable **Prosperity Interlude** presents this transformation as a Hybrid Village Chronicle. Recurring villagers, future heroes, and recognizable locations carry the viewer through water security, construction, seasonal change, greenhouses, aquaponics, harvests, education, healthcare, clothing improvements, and the formation of civil protection.

The interlude must communicate that prosperity was earned through hard work, intelligent investment, science, and cooperation. Technology supports people rather than replacing the community.

## 6. Stage 2 Prosperity Under Siege

### 6.1 New heroes

**Canon:** Stage 2 introduces 18-year-old fraternal twins from the village civil-protection program.

- **Wind Sentinel, 风神:** The sister. She is observant, compassionate, fast, and quietly confident. Her karate-derived style emphasizes movement, redirection, route creation, and rapid response.
- **Fire Warden, 火神:** The brother. He is direct, earnest, disciplined, and fierce without being cruel. His style combines Sanda or Sanshou, streamlined modern kickboxing, and short Military Qinna-derived joint control.

Their modern protective clothing and patrol boots express the village's development. Their sibling cooperation introduces reciprocal combat handoffs, but each remains a complete solo hero. Mountain Guardian and River Scout remain selectable and narratively relevant.

### 6.2 The staged rescue

**Canon:** While patrolling an outer agricultural route, Wind Sentinel and Fire Warden see apparent villains pursuing an injured elderly man. They intervene, drive the pursuers away, and take the man to the village clinic.

The rescue is staged. The injured man, **Tomas Rell**, is allied with the people pretending to pursue him. His injuries are real but arranged as part of the deception. Tomas remains a noncombatant infiltrator throughout Stage 2; he is not the stage boss and does not suddenly reveal martial-arts abilities.

### 6.3 Tomas's offer

**Canon:** Tomas claims to possess a plant-derived pharmaceutical technique that may improve recovery while reducing harmful effects. He offers to share it in gratitude for being rescued.

The elder provisionally accepts the offer and allows Tomas to recover under supervision. The formula is not an empty invention: Tomas has genuine specialist knowledge concerning medicinal-plant cultivation, extraction, preparation, and dosage control. However, it is not a universal cure, and no claim is accepted without independent testing.

### 6.4 Recovery and suspicion

**Canon:** Tomas spends about two weeks recovering. He then requests a three-day tour of the village and greenhouse complex, supposedly to prepare his work.

His questions increasingly concern roads, structures, schedules, access routes, and logistics rather than agriculture or medicine. The elder notices the mismatch. Instead of publicly accusing a recovering guest without proof, he quietly orders surveillance and places the guard on alert.

### 6.5 Night signal and partial breach

**Canon:** On the arranged night, Tomas attempts to contact his accomplices and trigger Marek Vane's raid. Waiting guards reveal themselves immediately. The elder's preparation prevents complete surprise, but a secondary access, act of sabotage, or prearranged signal allows the raiders to endanger the greenhouse complex.

The partial breach affects the greenhouses and their connected systems only. Residential, educational, medical, and civic districts are not overrun. Researchers and civilians evacuate or reach protected positions according to the civil-protection plan.

### 6.6 Playable route

**Canon:** The stage flow is:

`Prosperity Interlude -> Roadside Deception -> elder briefing and loadout -> Guided Pursuit Corridor -> greenhouse attack -> optional protection branches -> filtration crisis -> production hall -> Marek intercept -> dispatch-arena boss -> layered epilogue`

The major locations are:

1. **Perimeter and Loading Yard:** A guided motorbike pursuit introduces the modern response system before combat reaches the loading area.
2. **Aquaculture and Crop Systems:** The heroes protect workers, fish, shrimp, plant beds, seed reserves, and aeration.
3. **Medicinal and Filtration Systems:** The heroes defend the research wing, prevent sabotage, and contain a reversible control breach.
4. **Production and Dispatch:** The remaining raiders consolidate around Marek, leading to a short mounted intercept and the final arena battle.

Three optional branches deepen the story without gating completion:

- **Emergency Aeration Annex:** Rescue a worker and stabilize aquatic life.
- **Seed Reserve Bay:** Protect the village's future planting stock.
- **Medicinal Preparation Room:** Rescue a researcher and recover a provenance ledger connected to the formula's history.

The Preservation Ledger records what the heroes save, what is damaged, and what must be stabilized. Performance changes ending inserts and recognition, not the canonical survival of the village or the defeat of the raid.

### 6.7 Marek Vane The Gilded Jackal

**Canon:** The Stage 2 boss is **Marek Vane, The Gilded Jackal**, an outside raider commander. He is a charismatic but ruthless predator who seeks the village's wealth, greenhouse technology, research, and movable resources. He believes prosperity belongs to whoever is strong enough to seize it.

Marek leads an independent raider band. An unknown intermediary supplies selected equipment and intelligence, but Marek may not know the sponsor's identity. His personal motive remains greed; he does not become a loyal ideological representative of the hidden sponsor.

His encounter moves through a short mounted interception, a finite-ammunition armed-command phase, and a close-combat counter-brawler phase. At low health he may threaten optional greenhouse controls, revealing that he would rather damage what he cannot possess. Whether he surrenders cleanly after a Resolve break or is safely incapacitated after a final attempt, the canonical result is the same: he is captured alive.

### 6.8 Resolution and justice

**Canon:** The raid fails. The greenhouse's living systems survive in a recoverable state, although optional systems may require repair according to the run's Preservation result.

Marek and eligible captured raiders receive medical assessment. The village documents identities, testimony, weapons, equipment, and evidence. Marek is then transferred through a recorded chain of custody to the **Regional Civil Protection Council**, a civilian mutual-aid institution for serious cross-community crime, emergency coordination, neutral hearings, and lawful prisoner transfer.

Tomas is captured alive. Fearing the consequences, he hands over:

- The genuine formula.
- Supporting source notes.
- The provenance information known to him.

The clinic seals and quarantines the material for independent validation. The elder accepts custody of useful knowledge but makes clear that it cannot purchase innocence or erase betrayal.

Tomas receives necessary medical care and basic travel provisions. He is disarmed, documented, permanently excluded from the village, escorted beyond its boundary at dawn, and reported to the Regional Civil Protection Council so nearby communities are warned. He is neither forgiven nor executed, and he is not abandoned in immediate mortal danger.

### 6.9 Formula status at the end of Stage 2

**Canon:** Stage 2 completes only **Quarantine and Provenance**, the first research milestone. The clinic must still perform independent replication and safety review before considering an approved preparation.

Tomas contributed to earlier research but did not create the formula alone. He previously worked with a regional agricultural-research cooperative and retained material without proper authorization. The true inventorship and contributions of other researchers remain open to later investigation.

No story, item description, cinematic, or archive entry provides real ingredients, extraction steps, dosages, or actionable medical instructions. The formula is fictional story knowledge.

### 6.10 Second sponsor clue

**Canon:** Stage 2 reveals a reliable two-part connection to Stage 1:

- Marek's supplied weapon crate carries the same recognizable supply seal seen on Stage 1 equipment.
- Its order code follows the same incomplete structure and adds a route marker.

Wind Sentinel and Fire Warden recover the crate. A returning founding hero recognizes the seal. The elder compares both stages' codes and identifies an intermediary route. Scouts follow that route to a depot, but the site has been deliberately abandoned.

The evidence proves that one supply network supported both attacks and that someone is actively covering its tracks. It does not identify the sponsor.

### 6.11 Layered epilogue

**Canon:** Stage 2 closes through a layered sequence rather than one long unbroken movie:

1. Marek's surrender or safe incapacitation and restraint.
2. Immediate hazard control and the final Preservation summary.
3. Tomas's handover of the formula, source notes, and provenance.
4. Marek's lawful transfer and Tomas's escorted expulsion.
5. Community recovery, worker reunions, repairs, and stabilization of living systems.
6. Discovery of the abandoned intermediary depot.
7. Results, research progress, Museum discoveries, and Sponsor Dossier updates.

There is no hidden bad ending. Performance variations affect presentation and rewards but not the required story facts.

## 7. Stage 3 Shadows over the Herb Mountain

Stage 3 has completed refinement and is packaged in `stage3_feature_story_line.md` and `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-01-fight-for-peace-stage-3-specification.md`. Exact frame data, final numerical tuning, dialogue, artwork, animation, audio assets, and map dimensions remain production work; the approved foundation below is authoritative for continuity.

### 7.1 Scientific catalyst

**Approved:** After Stage 2, village researchers continue controlled study of Tomas's formula and source notes. Through replication and refinement, they produce a fictional candidate preparation that is remarkably effective at controlling cancer-cell growth in laboratory mice.

This is a preclinical result, not proof of a human cure. International medical institutions recognize the significance of the research and the village receives a substantial grant for further investigation. The village's reputation and resources increase, but so do the risks surrounding its knowledge.

The improved candidate requires a wholly fictional mountain plant called the **Silvervein Moonbell / 银脉月铃草 / Silberader-Mondglocke**. It is a small blue-violet plant with silver-veined leaves that grows in cool mist pockets on shaded high-altitude ledges. Its restricted habitat makes it difficult to cultivate, but it is neither magical nor inherently curative.

The plant contains a completely invented research cofactor designated **MV-3**. MV-3 does not treat cancer by itself. Within the fictional research, it helps stabilize the village's candidate preparation sufficiently to produce more consistent control of selected tumor-cell growth in laboratory mice. No human efficacy has been demonstrated, and the story never provides safety values, dosage, extraction, preparation, or an actionable biological mechanism.

The international grant funds replication, toxicity research, conservation, provenance review, and possible controlled cultivation rather than commercial production. Researchers require only limited responsibly gathered samples and do not strip the habitat. The bandits target the gatherers for ransom and strategic leverage; the raw plant is not portrayed as a miraculous black-market cure. Stage 3 recovers enough documented material for continued preclinical work but unlocks neither an approved medicine nor permanent combat power.

#### 7.1.1 Research governance and institutional scope

Stage 3 does not add another hostile government, corporation, ownership lawsuit, or medical institution. The kidnapping remains its complete immediate conflict. The grant nevertheless imposes responsible noncombat conditions: Silvervein Moonbell remains part of the Silvermist ecosystem rather than automatic village property; Qinglan becomes a credited conservation and field-research partner; and collection requires documented community consent, limited sampling, habitat monitoring, and complete provenance.

Grant funders receive no automatic ownership of the formula, Moonbell habitat, village data, or future treatment rights. Tomas's earlier contribution remains documented but grants him neither sole ownership nor permission to seize later independent work. After the kidnapping, field collection pauses until the route is secured and Qinglan agrees that it may resume. Legitimate institutional backups mean that kidnapping Ren Xia cannot transfer or erase research ownership.

International partners support medical care, conservation, and independent verification after the rescue without solving the conflict or obstructing the patrol through artificial bureaucracy. Human trials, licensing, access, and benefit-sharing remain questions for later peaceful development rather than secondary Stage 3 antagonist plots.

### 7.2 Kidnapping in the mountains

**Approved:** The village sends experienced residents into the mountains to gather the herb. The mountains were previously considered peaceful, but bandits have established a hidden presence.

The bandits capture several villagers and send the remaining gatherers back with a ransom demand. Their immediate leverage is ransom; their larger objective is to weaken, exploit, and eventually raid the prosperous village.

The patrol team prepares a mountain expedition to:

- Locate and rescue the captives.
- Prevent the bandits from using hostages to extort the village.
- Secure the herb-gathering routes without treating the mountain as territory to conquer.
- Discover why the bandits have become organized enough to threaten the village.

#### 7.2.1 Silvermist Highlands route and culture

Stage 3 takes place in the **Silvermist Highlands / 银雾岭 / Silbernebel-Hochland**, which rise above the river valley and are reached through one maintained switchback road and several older walking trails. Moist air from mountain waterfalls creates the cool shaded mist pockets where Silvervein Moonbell grows.

The approved route is:

`village briefing -> Lower Switchback -> Qinglan Hamlet -> Moonbell Basin -> Echo Quarry -> Broken Ridge Camp -> High Winch Terrace`

- **Lower Switchback:** A safe mountain-horse introduction becomes a pursuit after the patrol finds abandoned gathering equipment and evidence of an ambush.
- **Qinglan Hamlet / 青岚村 / Dorf Qinglan:** A small cliffside community supporting gatherers, trail maintenance, conservation, and horse handling. Bandits have damaged its communications without occupying the entire settlement. The optional signal-hut rescue occurs here.
- **Moonbell Basin / 月铃谷 / Mondglockental:** A protected north-facing valley containing the documented Moonbell habitat. The heroes secure abandoned samples and rescue isolated gatherers without harvesting the environment indiscriminately.
- **Echo Quarry / 回声石场 / Echosteinbruch:** A disused stone quarry with stable service tunnels, timber scaffolds, mine carts, and an old herbalist shelter. It provides the concealed route toward the camp and supports the second optional branch.
- **Broken Ridge Camp / 断脊营地 / Lager am Gebrochenen Grat:** The bandits occupy a former quarry logistics site controlling both major passes. Captives are held in separated but reachable work and storage enclosures rather than one convenient cage.
- **High Winch Terrace / 高索台 / Hochseil-Terrasse:** The boss arena is a broad former loading platform with retaining walls, cargo rails, winches, breakable timber cover, and safe boundaries. Environmental attacks remain tactical without requiring lethal cliff falls.

Qinglan's residents are modern mountain workers rather than an isolated mystical community. They maintain trail ledgers, conservation zones, sample provenance, weather signals, rescue bells, radios, and cooperative horse shelters. Their clothing combines contemporary weather protection with locally produced woven details. Their knowledge is ecological and practical; they do not possess secret cures or supernatural herbal wisdom. Village researchers remain responsible for controlled scientific study.

An old quarry service passage provides Tomas with a believable prepared escape route while the heroes prioritize captives and the Mountain Bandit Chief.

#### 7.2.2 Hostage roster and rescue structure

Two expedition members return to raise the alarm:

- **Luo Fen / 罗芬**, age 44, is an experienced gathering coordinator whom the bandits send back with the ransom demand because they expect the village to trust her account.
- **Deng Hao / 邓浩**, age 32, is a clinic field medic who returns with Luo Fen after suffering a non-life-threatening injury and provides the first documented description of the ambush.

They cannot provide a complete camp map because the captives are moved after the attack. Four people remain captive:

- **Gao Wen / 高文**, age 52, is Qinglan's senior trail and conservation steward. He suffers an injured ankle but keeps the younger captive calm.
- **Tao Min / 陶敏**, age 22, is Gao Wen's apprentice gatherer. She carefully observes bandit movements and later recalls the route toward the camp.
- **An Bo / 安博**, age 35, is a Qinglan horse handler and rescue volunteer. The bandits force him to maintain captured horses and quarry transport equipment.
- **Ren Xia / 任霞**, age 41, is the village research liaison responsible for sample provenance. The bandits hold her at the main camp because Tomas believes she can authenticate the research's value.

All four rescues belong to the mandatory route. Gao Wen and Tao Min are freed from a guarded field shelter in Moonbell Basin; An Bo is rescued from the Echo Quarry transport enclosure and helps calm the recovered horses; Ren Xia is rescued at Broken Ridge Camp before the boss approach and secures the surviving samples and documentation.

Every captive survives canonically, and there is no hidden execution timer. Each rescue group receives a visible **Secure**, **Strained**, or **Critical** condition grade based on delay, environmental damage, and protection performance. The grade changes medical presentation, score, recognition, and epilogue details but never causes death or blocks the story. Freed captives follow short authored routes to protected shelters rather than becoming long escort objectives. Their knowledge provides contextual assistance without turning them into combatants.

#### 7.2.3 Garran Voss — The Ridge Wolf

The Mountain Bandit Chief is **Garran Voss — The Ridge Wolf / 加兰·沃斯 — 岭狼 / Garran Voss — Der Gratwolf**, age 46. A former quarry transport foreman, he becomes a mountain raider after years of using intimidation, stolen cargo, and illegal tolls to control remote routes. He leads a smaller band before Tomas arrives; Tomas's money, intelligence, radios, and logistical planning allow him to expand the camp and attempt larger kidnappings.

Garran seeks ransom, route control, and wealth rather than revenge against the village. He regards Tomas as a profitable but untrustworthy planner, while Tomas regards Garran as disposable physical force.

Garran is broad, weathered, and powerful without becoming a giant armored brute. His layered charcoal and ochre mountain work clothing includes a dull-red reinforced vest, quarry gloves, rope harness, heavy trail boots, and a compact modern radio supplied through Tomas. He has no military uniform, wolf costume, sponsor emblem, or futuristic armor.

His combat identity is **Ridge Hook Wrestling**, centered on a blunt **Quarry Hookstaff** adapted from a long quarry-handling tool:

1. **Hookstaff Control:** Long sweeps, guarded thrusts, hooked pulls, low-line attacks, and short descending strikes control space.
2. **Clinch and Cast:** After the staff is damaged or disarmed, Garran uses collar ties, shoulder throws, trips, body blows, and bounded ground follow-ups.
3. **Winch Pressure:** At low Resolve, he activates quarry winches to reposition cover and cargo. Every environmental attack has a clear warning and can be avoided, interrupted, or redirected.

The Hookstaff follows normal durability and disarm rules. Garran has a Defense Bar, Exact Blocks, Counters, a defense-consuming Combo Breaker, scaling, anti-infinite protection, and punishable recovery. In co-op, hooked grabs can be interrupted by the second hero, and no sequence traps one player without a rescue opportunity. His canonical defeat ends through disarm, Resolve break, restraint, medical assessment, and live capture.

### 7.3 Tomas's return

**Approved:** After his expulsion, Tomas grows more resentful. He joins the mountain bandits, helps establish or improve their camp, and uses his intelligence, money, contacts, knowledge of the village, and business skills to prepare future raids.

Tomas remains a non-martial strategist. He should not become a conventional fighting boss merely because he returns as an antagonist. His danger comes from planning, manipulation, finance, intelligence, legal opportunism, and his ability to recruit or influence people who can fight.

The bandits target medicinal-herb gatherers partly for ransom and partly to obstruct or control the village's valuable research. Their ultimate ambition is to plunder the village and seize its wealth, technology, and knowledge.

Tomas reaches brokers connected to the same hidden supply network that supported the Stage 1 occupation and Marek's Stage 2 raid. He does not know the sponsor's identity. He believes he is purchasing equipment, intelligence, transport, and mercenary support for his own revenge. The sponsor, acting only through intermediaries, recognizes Tomas's wealth, resentment, local knowledge, and bandit connections and treats him as a deniable instrument for pressuring and studying the village.

Both sides believe they are exploiting the other. Tomas remains the campaign's central emotional antagonist, while the sponsor remains the larger strategic mystery. Fighters, brokers, and captured lieutenants know only their immediate contact and cannot expose the sponsor directly.

#### 7.3.1 Tomas's physical presence and prepared escape

The heroes initially encounter only signs of unusually organized planning: accurate village schedules, modern radios, disciplined supply placement, and maps based on Tomas's knowledge. At Moonbell Basin, an intercepted open-radio exchange confirms Tomas's recognizable voice as he instructs Garran to move Ren Xia and continue observing the patrol's response.

At Broken Ridge Camp, the heroes briefly see Tomas in an elevated planning cabin questioning Ren Xia about the research's value. She refuses to authenticate exaggerated claims or describe any preparation process. When the heroes breach the camp, Tomas activates a prearranged quarry signal, takes only portable records, enters the old service passage, uses a quarry transport cart through its narrow first section, and transfers to a waiting horse beyond the ridge.

Tomas begins withdrawing before the heroes can reach his level. Garran moves Ren Xia toward the boss approach and orders fighters to delay the patrol. The accessible route leads to Ren, while an ordinary quarry gate and damaged rail section close the service passage. The player receives no false chase prompt and loses no reward for fulfilling the rescue priority. Garran refuses to abandon his camp and confronts the heroes at High Winch Terrace.

The mandatory sponsor clue comes from Garran rather than Tomas's carelessness. Garran secretly retains a damaged payment invoice in his private supply case as leverage against Tomas. After his capture, his key and testimony lead to the case. Its readable batch reference links the bandits to the regional procurement cutout. The epilogue briefly shows Tomas reaching a lowland shelter and contacting his former commercial broker, creating the transition toward Stage 4.

### 7.4 Authored Stage 3 content baseline

Stage 3 targets approximately 40–50 minutes for a first Story clear and 25–35 minutes for an experienced replay. Its authored route contains twelve mandatory combat encounters plus Garran and two optional encounters: two at Lower Switchback; two at Qinglan plus the Signal Hut branch; two at Moonbell Basin; three at Echo Quarry plus the Herbalist Shelter branch; and three at Broken Ridge Camp before High Winch Terrace.

Five checkpoint payloads cover stage departure, arrival at Qinglan after the mounted introduction, the Gao Wen and Tao Min rescue, the An Bo rescue, and the Ren Xia rescue immediately before Garran. Restoration preserves captive state, optional branches, consumed supplies, weapon state, horse state, and persistent environmental damage.

One mountain horse per active hero is available at Lower Switchback, and one replacement per hero becomes available after rescuing An Bo. Horses retreat safely at zero Stability and never die. Mounted play appears at the opening and one quarry-to-camp pursuit rather than dominating the stage.

The Medium weapon baseline is two Hiking Staffs, one main-route Wood Axe, two Quarry Hammers, and one bandit revolver with five remaining rounds. Garran's Quarry Hookstaff becomes interactable only through its authored boss disarm. Easy adds one Staff and one Hammer; Extreme and Ultimate remove one duplicate Staff without removing an entire weapon class.

The Medium recovery baseline is three Life items, two Defense items, two Momentum items, and one rare Mountain Camp Meal in the Herbalist Shelter branch. Easy adds three common items, Hard removes one, Extreme removes two, and Ultimate removes three; the optional rare meal remains available at every difficulty.

All difficulties retain the twelve mandatory encounter locations. Initial solo population targets are approximately 22 ordinary enemies on Easy, 30 on Medium, 34 on Hard with stronger role combinations, 36 on Extreme including Ridge Stalkers, and 40 on Ultimate including Ridge Stalkers and Pack Captains. Co-op targets roughly 35 percent more total threat rather than double counts and remains subject to simultaneous-pressure caps.

Garran's private case appears only after his capture. Its damaged invoice and procurement batch reference are mandatory, checkpoint-safe, and impossible to destroy or miss. Every count above is an authored playtest baseline rather than immutable final balance; validated tuning may adjust counts without changing the route, content classes, rescue structure, or clue continuity.

## 8. Tomas Rell long-term character arc

### 8.1 Established public identity

**Canon:** In Stage 2, Tomas appears to be a vulnerable elderly medicinal-crop and extraction specialist. He is intelligent, technically credible, educated, observant, and capable of using genuine knowledge to make a deception persuasive.

He participated in the earlier research behind the formula, but he was never its sole inventor. Stage 2's provenance records may establish the former cooperative and missing contributors without revealing his complete personal history.

### 8.2 Planned hidden history

**Planned:** Tomas is highly intelligent, capable, and commercially astute. Over many years he accumulates a substantial fortune by exploiting legal gray areas, disputed ownership, asymmetric contracts, and personal connections. His acquisition or control of formula-related material involved a dubious legal dispute layered on top of his genuine earlier contribution and unauthorized retention of source material.

This preserves three facts simultaneously:

1. Tomas understands the science and contributed to it.
2. Other people also created the knowledge and may deserve credit.
3. His later possession or claimed control was ethically and legally questionable.

### 8.3 Marriage, initial fortune, and surviving wife

**Planned:** After exile, Tomas genuinely falls in love with a young woman from a wealthy family. Their marriage is not fraudulent from the beginning, and their son is born from a relationship that once contained real affection.

Tomas's former wife is **Xu Lian** in English and German and **徐莲** in Simplified Chinese. **Xu** is the family name and **Lian** the given name. She is approximately two years younger than Tomas and is **50 during Stage 2**. She retains Xu Lian as her personal and professional name rather than being defined only through the Rell family name.

Xu Lian serves as **Director of Agricultural Systems and Cooperative Partnerships** in her family's Yunzhou agricultural logistics and investment group. Her expertise includes refrigerated agricultural transport, medicinal-crop supply chains, greenhouse equipment, ethical cooperative contracts, and research-partnership logistics. She is neither the family's principal fraud investigator nor an inventor of the secret formula.

She meets Tomas while overseeing a medicinal-crop distribution project. She admires his intelligence, technical credibility, and work ethic, supports his early proposals, and defends him against family prejudice. Her operational role later allows her to preserve correspondence, project approvals, transport records, and partnership documents from direct professional experience without reducing her to a source of money or evidence.

The woman's influential relatives never fully accept Tomas. His intelligence and hard work earn him partial access to the family's commercial world, but his ostracized lineage subjects him to continuing prejudice, distrust, and unequal treatment. His wife defends him, and the family must not be written as one uniformly malicious group. Some relatives act from unfair social prejudice; others only later acquire legitimate reasons to distrust his financial conduct.

The family is internally divided:

- Tomas's wife loves him, defends the marriage, and argues that he should be judged by his conduct rather than his lineage.
- One or two younger relatives respect his intelligence and support some of his early business ideas.
- A dominant parent or senior family council distrusts him from the beginning because of his exiled background and denies him the status and authority granted automatically to insiders.
- A financially responsible relative begins from a comparatively neutral position, evaluates Tomas by his work, and later identifies genuine irregularities.

Two confirmed relatives preserve this division. **Xu Tao / 徐涛**, age 46 during Stage 2, is Xu Lian's younger cousin and a cold-chain operations engineer. He respects Tomas's intelligence, supports several early projects, and defends the marriage, but later refuses to conceal Tomas's fraud. His testimony confirms both Tomas's genuine early effort and the unfair treatment he experienced.

**Xu Rong / 徐蓉**, age 62 during Stage 2, is Xu Lian's aunt and the family group's former financial custodian. She begins from a comparatively neutral position and judges Tomas through his work. When she discovers the diverted investment funds, she secures audit copies and insists on a documented investigation. Her evidence establishes that later opposition to Tomas was based on proven misconduct rather than only on lineage prejudice.

Neither relative is Xu Lian's unconditional partisan. Together they help authenticate her evidence without controlling her decisions, her son's response, or the campaign outcome.

The dominant senior figure is **Xu Decheng** in English and German and **徐德成** in Simplified Chinese. **Xu** is the family name and **Decheng** the given name. He is Xu Lian's father, age **76 during Stage 2**, and the founder and retired chair of the family agricultural logistics group.

Xu Decheng built the enterprise from regional storage and transport operations and strongly values stability, reputation, and family continuity. He distrusts Tomas because of the Rell family's exile, denies him a permanent leadership stake despite strong performance, and declares that Tomas's son will not be accepted as a future successor. After Tomas's fraud is exposed, Decheng initially uses it to justify all of his earlier suspicion.

In the later investigation, Xu Decheng gives a recorded statement admitting that he treated Tomas unfairly before any misconduct occurred. He does not excuse Tomas's fraud or demand forgiveness for himself. His admission establishes that the family's prejudice and Tomas's deliberate betrayal were both real. He remains a noncombatant with no connection to the sponsor network.

The family owns a diversified regional agricultural logistics and investment group. Its legitimate activities include agricultural storage and distribution, refrigerated transport, seed and medicinal-herb trading, irrigation and greenhouse equipment, investment in farming cooperatives and processing facilities, and financing for selected agricultural or medical-research partnerships.

This enterprise gives Tomas a credible commercial education and network. He proves his value through a profitable medicinal-crop distribution or processing project, learns contracts and regional supply routes, and builds relationships with brokers, lawyers, financiers, researchers, carriers, and warehouse operators. These contacts later support both his legitimate rise and his illicit network.

The family's business does not originate or invent the secret formula. Tomas's scientific contribution remains connected to the separate regional research cooperative. The family enterprise may later finance, transport, process, or dispute commercial rights connected to cooperative work, creating the overlap Tomas exploits in a dubious legal case without merging the two institutions.

Tomas therefore experiences real discrimination while also receiving real loyalty and opportunity. When legitimate financial concern later becomes mixed with the family's older prejudice, he treats every objection as proof that nobody ever accepted him. Some early supporters withdraw because of his conduct, not his ancestry, but he refuses to recognize the difference.

This divided testimony later prevents either side from owning the full history. A supportive relative can confirm Tomas's honest early effort, a prejudiced senior figure can admit the family's unfairness, a financial guardian can document the fraud, and his former wife can describe how love and trust gradually collapsed.

Repeated exclusion reawakens Tomas's childhood humiliation. He gradually stops distinguishing between the relatives who mistreat him, the wife who loves him, and the distant village responsible for his family's exile. Instead of rejecting inherited vengeance, he uses it to justify deliberate adult choices.

The decisive catalyst follows years of gradual erosion. Tomas develops or rescues a profitable family venture and expects a formal leadership role, ownership share, or durable position. His wife and younger supporters endorse him, but the senior council refuses because it will not permit someone from an exiled lineage to control the enterprise. A senior figure also makes clear that Tomas's son will not be treated as a legitimate future successor.

Instead of accepting his wife's offer to leave the family enterprise and build an independent life together, Tomas decides that the family owes him what it refused to grant. He secretly redirects funds from an upcoming investment into structures he controls. At first he describes the act to himself as taking his rightful share. After the first deception succeeds, restitution becomes enrichment and then revenge.

When the financial guardian discovers the irregularities, Tomas interprets the investigation as more persecution. His wife's refusal to defend actual fraud feels to him like betrayal, although she defended him against prejudice for years. The council's discrimination explains why the decision wounds him; it does not cause or excuse the choices that follow.

Tomas exploits the family's trust, access, contracts, investments, and business relationships to divert or swindle substantial assets. This becomes his initial capital. He later expands the fortune through deceptive investments, legal gray areas, disputed ownership, illicit dealings, and a network of useful connections, while revenge against the village remains his ultimate purpose.

During the collapse of the marriage, their son is approximately **nine years old**. Tomas persuades Xu Lian to sign limited temporary travel and education consent, presenting relocation as protection from family conflict and continuity of schooling. After relocating, he files for provisional custody in another jurisdiction using selective evidence of the Xu family's hostility.

Tomas deliberately routes hearing notices through outdated or controlled addresses so that Xu Lian cannot respond in time. He obtains an interim order by default, changes the child's school, residence, and contact channels, and prolongs later challenges through lawyers, commercial connections, and cross-regional delay. The institutions are deceived and slow rather than universally corrupt. What begins with limited lawful consent becomes deliberate custodial isolation.

Tomas teaches the boy that Xu Lian willingly signed him away and never appeared to reclaim him. He intercepts or conceals attempts at contact. Xu Lian preserves the original limited consent, misdirected notices, petitions, returned letters, and related records, which later demonstrate both her efforts and Tomas's deception.

**Planned:** Tomas's former wife remains alive. She preserves correspondence, financial records, and her own account of the marriage. After her son has separated from Tomas, she offers him the choice of a personal meeting without demanding acceptance. Her evidence gives him a source independent of both Tomas and the village elder and helps him understand that Tomas manipulated them both. She remains a consequential character with agency rather than existing only as the source of Tomas's wealth.

After the marriage and financial scandal, Xu Lian remains in Yunzhou but leaves daily executive management of the family enterprise. She retains a lawful minority interest and defined access to archived partnership and project records, but she does not control the family fortune. She rebuilds her professional life as an independent agricultural adviser and mediator for farming cooperatives, greenhouse projects, ethical supply agreements, and agricultural research partnerships.

Xu Lian is respected, financially stable, and active in her own community. She has not spent the intervening years waiting for Tomas or her son to return. Her present work demonstrates that the agricultural knowledge, partnerships, and opportunities Tomas encountered could have been used constructively. Her continuing professional registration provides a credible route through which the Regional Civil Protection Council can later locate and verify her.

Xu Lian re-enters the narrative through a **verified documentary chain in the later campaign**, not through a retroactive addition to Stage 1 or Stage 2. A later investigation into Tomas uncovers old Yunzhou partnership references. The Regional Civil Protection Council traces those references to the Xu family archive. Xu Rong authenticates the financial records, while Xu Tao confirms Tomas's early legitimate work and the project history. The Council then locates and verifies Xu Lian through her current professional registration.

Xu Lian provides a sealed evidence package and recorded statement before requesting any personal contact with her son. The material includes intercepted or returned letters where available, custody and relocation records, investment documents, evidence of diverted funds, her own account, and corroborating statements from Xu Tao, Xu Rong, and Xu Decheng. The protected package may be prepared before the son's capture, but it is disclosed to him only when his separation from Tomas makes access relevant and safe.

The son receives the records without being forced into immediate personal contact. This preserves his agency and allows him to evaluate the material alongside the village case file, the elder's written objection, and his own observations.

**Confirmed (2026-11-03):** Xu Lian's documentary disclosure arrives in Stage 4, her lawful notice in Stage 7, and her invitation in the Stage 8 epilogue.

**Unresolved:** The final visual identities of Xu Lian, Xu Tao, Xu Rong, and Xu Decheng (designed with the Release 2 art).

### 8.4 His father's betrayal

Tomas's father is **Oren Rell**, age 42 during the historical crisis. Tomas is approximately nine years old. Oren is intelligent, competent, commercially ambitious, and fully responsible for his own conspiracy; his family's later collective punishment does not make his betrayal false.

**Confirmed office:** Oren served as the village's **Communal Supply and Trade Steward**, a trusted civilian official responsible for reserve and purchasing ledgers, external purchases of grain, medicine, seed, and tools, pack-convoy and delivery schedules, temporary gate access for legitimate traders, transport routes, and reports to the previous elder about reserve levels.

He was not a military or patrol commander and did not possess complete defense plans. His legitimate work nevertheless exposed him to convoy-escort availability, delivery windows, gate procedures, and enough recurring security patterns to observe or infer exploitable gaps.

Oren believes that the village benefits from his ability without granting him the wealth, influence, or recognition he deserves. He increasingly resents Zhao Heng's oversight and converts wounded pride into entitlement. For personal gain, he forms a commercial conspiracy with a marauding band. He targets the village's **Seasonal Emergency and Trade Reserve**, which contains winter grain and preserved food, dried medicinal supplies, seed for the next planting season, spare iron tools and repair materials, and a modest fund for urgent external purchases.

He gradually diverts small portions and disguises them as spoilage, transport losses, emergency purchases, and accounting corrections. The stolen goods accumulate at a concealed mountain depot. The planned raid is intended to seize the much larger reserve still inside the village, including the trade strongbox. Its loss would threaten food security, treatment of injuries, the next harvest, infrastructure repair, and the village's ability to buy emergency supplies.

The final operation uses a **false emergency convoy with a concealed assault**. Tomas's father fabricates an urgent external-purchase order and supplies authentic procedural knowledge. A small bandit infiltration group approaches as merchants and carriers with some legitimate goods visible while weapons are concealed in the wagons. A larger force waits outside along a prepared mountain route.

Once admitted, the infiltrators intend to disable the warning system, secure access to the storehouse, and signal the outside force. The main band will then enter during the confusion, seize the reserve and trade strongbox, and retreat through the prepared route. In return, Oren expects payment, safe passage, a share of the stolen wealth, and access to Rovan's dishonest commercial connections so that he can leave as a prosperous merchant rather than an exposed thief.

Oren tells himself that the attackers will rely on intimidation and that his procedural knowledge will keep the theft controlled. This is self-deception rather than innocence: he knowingly admits armed raiders, helps conceal their weapons, and accepts the foreseeable risk to defenders and civilians.

The young future elder detects inconsistencies among the purchase order, reserve ledger, expected merchant seal, and wagon weight. He alerts the previous elder before the plan fully succeeds. Realizing that discovery is imminent, the infiltrators attack early and the outside force advances without completing its preparations.

The conspiracy is proven through mutually reinforcing evidence: falsified records, concealed wagon weapons, captured attackers, recovered goods at the hidden depot, and testimony concerning the agreed signal and route. The case therefore does not depend on the elder's accusation alone.

Two independent supporting witnesses make the distinction between evidence and authority especially clear. **Su Yan / 苏妍**, age 24, is Oren's junior ledger assistant. Suspicious corrections lead her to preserve duplicate receipts separately from the main reserve book. Because she worked under Oren, she is initially investigated, but her records, timing, and conduct show that she did not join the conspiracy. She is cleared as an individual rather than treated as guilty through association.

**Brann Holt**, age 33, is Rovan's captured convoy quartermaster. He confirms the concealed weapons, agreed signal, mountain depot, escape route, and payment promised to Oren. His testimony matches Su Yan's receipts, the recovered supplies, and the physical convoy evidence. Brann receives an individual sentence for his own participation and no complete immunity; he knows the operation's logistics but not every external broker or the later sponsor network.

After the failure, Oren admits no meaningful moral responsibility. He presents himself to Tomas as a gifted man whose rightful future was stolen by a fearful village, turning a real injustice against the family into a false claim that his own betrayal was justified.

The historical bandit commander is **Rovan Kest**, age 40 and known regionally as the **Road-Cutter**. He leads a mobile caravan-raider band that uses stolen merchant papers and wagon markings, bribes informants for cargo intelligence, attacks caravans and remote storehouses, resells stolen necessities through dishonest brokers, and relocates rather than attempting to rule territory. He accepts the steward's proposal because authentic village procedures and delivery knowledge reduce the risk of striking a defended settlement.

Rovan seeks supplies, wealth, and criminal reputation rather than revenge or political control. He serves as the historical Chronicle's combat boss, while Tomas's father remains the civilian conspirator whose access and records enabled the assault.

Rovan's defeat does not create the modern sponsor through a preplanned multigenerational conspiracy. Instead, surviving raiders, corrupt brokers, stolen-goods merchants, forged identities, concealed warehouses, and mountain routes persist as an **institutional criminal legacy**. Across several decades, new leaders absorb, reorganize, and modernize parts of this infrastructure until it becomes one historical root of the network later used by the unknown sponsor.

The modern sponsor is not required to be Rovan's biological descendant. Its contemporary leadership and motive remain its own. The connection is revealed late through route records, broker archives, transaction patterns, and organizational history rather than an obvious unchanged emblem. Tomas therefore unknowingly relies on institutional descendants of criminals who helped cause his family's downfall, while remaining fully responsible for choosing to use that network.

Rovan is defeated and captured alive after attempting to withdraw with selected records and part of the loot. The heroes disarm rather than execute him, and the young future elder ensures basic treatment for Rovan and the other captives. Rovan's testimony confirms the agreement with Tomas's father and identifies the concealed depot, but he knows or reveals only the participants and logistics of this operation rather than every broker in the wider raiding economy.

Because the village lacks a secure long-term prison, Rovan is transferred under guard to a distant regional authority. A regional court sentences him to 30 years in secure custody for armed conspiracy, organized robbery, forged trade identities, and responsibility for the fatal assault. His testimony is considered as evidence but does not excuse his crimes or produce an informal pardon.

Rovan dies from illness in regional custody 17 years later, aged 57. He never escapes, resumes command, or directs the later network from prison. His testimony, trial record, and confiscated route documents remain in the regional archive. Several logisticians, brokers, lesser raiders, and external contacts were never present or escaped dispersal, allowing the institutional legacy to survive and evolve independently of him.

The young elder's warning prevents a massacre but cannot prevent all harm. No civilian is killed. **Qiao Lan / 乔岚**, age 36, is the single defender killed. Qiao is the family name and Lan the given name; English and German retain **Qiao Lan**. She is a skilled carpenter who ordinarily maintains gates, platforms, and other public structures and serves voluntarily in the village's civic reserve rather than as a professional warrior.

Qiao recognizes sabotage at the secondary entrance, sounds the manual warning, and holds the damaged gate mechanism long enough for other defenders to secure it. Her death is conveyed without graphic imagery. Her action prevents Rovan's larger outside force from gaining rapid entry and directly explains why the assault causes serious but contained harm. Liang Zhen is nearly killed and receives his permanent injury while protecting others; several defenders are wounded; and a small number of civilians receive nonfatal evacuation injuries. One supply wagon is stolen or destroyed, and the warning platform, storehouse entrance, and a nearby workshop are visibly damaged. Most homes, the trade fund, and the emergency reserve are saved.

The defender's death is communicated without graphic imagery through reactions, the empty post, respectful aftermath, and a later memorial. The sabotage and physical destruction of the platform, storehouse, wagon, workshop, and surrounding objects may be shown directly and clearly in the Chronicle, consistent with the game's established environmental-damage presentation.

Qiao Lan is survived by her husband **Chen Wei / 陈维**, age 38, an irrigation and stonework specialist, and their daughter **Chen An / 陈安**, age 11. Chen Wei initially demands severe punishment for Oren Rell. After the evidence proves no participation by Tomas or the other relatives, he supports Sheng Guo's individual-trial and restitution proposal and refuses mob violence or inherited blame. His signed statement becomes part of the village case file. Zhao Heng therefore cannot claim that Qiao's immediate family demanded collective exile; his ruling arises from wider fear, political pressure, and his own unwillingness to reconsider his authority.

The damaged warning bell is repaired and installed at the rebuilt secondary gate as Qiao's civic memorial. It honors protection, preparedness, and public service rather than revenge or warfare. A fragment of the original gate mechanism and one of Qiao's carpenter's tools later enter the Village Museum and Archive, allowing the Chronicle, Museum, and modern storyline to preserve the same evidence and meaning.

The young future elder notices inconsistencies between inventories, transport movements, and patrol information. He warns his predecessor and helps organize the defense. The bandits still attempt the raid, producing the historical battle in which the young elder leads villagers to defeat them and protect the community.

Recovered documents, the concealed supplies, and testimony from captured participants prove the father's involvement. His guilt is therefore genuine and well documented. The historical raiders were not acting for a future sponsor or following a long-term master plan, but remnants of their criminal logistics system later become one institutional ancestor of the modern sponsor network.

The father was responsible for a real betrayal, but the response extended beyond him: his entire family was expelled. Tomas was young, remembers the contempt directed at the family, and grew up carrying the humiliation. His father's dying wish called for revenge against the village.

The story must preserve the moral complexity:

- The previous elder was right to stop the betrayal.
- Punishing and exiling the whole family was collective punishment and remains controversial.
- Tomas suffered as a child for an act he did not commit.
- That injustice helps explain his resentment but does not excuse his adult deception, raids, kidnapping, or revenge.

The family-wide exile unfolds over approximately one month. On the night of the attack, Tomas's father is detained and the household is placed under protective supervision because angry villagers gather outside. During the first three days, the injured receive treatment, Qiao Lan is mourned, the depot is found, and the ledgers, concealed goods, and captive testimony confirm the father's guilt.

On the fourth day, the previous elder orders the household temporarily relocated to a guarded village outpost. He presents the measure as protection from retaliation and containment while unidentified contacts are investigated. During the next two weeks, the evidence proves the father's agreement with Rovan but produces no proof that young Tomas or the other relatives participated.

The young future elder proposes transferring the father for individual regional judgment, returning the innocent relatives to their home, and protecting them from retaliation. During the third week, grief, rumor, fear of surviving raiders, and demands for unity intensify public pressure. The previous elder rejects individual review, removes the father from office, confiscates assets directly connected to the crime, and converts the temporary relocation into permanent family exile.

By the end of the month, the household departs with basic provisions and safe passage, but no innocent relative receives an individual hearing. The previous elder prioritizes immediate security, public unity, and political closure over distinguishing proven guilt from family association. His emergency responsibilities make the decision understandable but not just.

The previous-generation elder is **58 years old** during the historical crisis. He is an experienced and generally capable administrator who has led the village through floods, crop failures, and smaller security emergencies and serves as Sheng Guo's respected governance mentor. During the attack he coordinates evacuation, reserve protection, and defensive assignments while Sheng Guo and Liang Zhen operate in the field.

His confirmed personal name is **Zhao Heng** in English and German and **赵衡** in Simplified Chinese. **Zhao** is the family name and **Heng** is the given name. Dialogue uses **Elder Zhao** or its localized equivalent, while formal records identify him as **Zhao Heng, Previous Village Elder**. The approved personal name remains 赵衡 in Simplified Chinese.

His moral failure occurs after the immediate danger. Even after the evidence implicates only Tomas's father, he rejects Sheng Guo's individual-judgment proposal and converts temporary containment into permanent collective exile. He privately recognizes that the evidence against the relatives is insufficient but refuses to reopen the case because doing so would expose his earlier decision and, in his view, weaken his authority during an unstable period.

He remains the village elder for approximately eight further years and continues to be respected for stopping the raid and managing reconstruction. At age 66, declining health leads him to retire through the village's normal succession process. Sheng Guo, then approximately 34 and now experienced in administration, becomes elder with community support. The predecessor dies several years later without issuing a complete public apology or reversing the exile.

After succession, Sheng Guo introduces individual evidence review, written objections and appeals, protection for unaccused relatives, documented custody and transfer, and outside review for serious sentences. He nevertheless fails to repair the original case fully. Oren intercepts and rejects Sheng Guo's initial contact attempt rather than allowing Tomas to hear another account, the household has begun to divide and move, and Sheng Guo does not persist strongly enough or publicly clear the uninvolved relatives. He therefore improves the system while retaining a second source of regret toward the people already harmed.

Lin Mei escorts the household along the **Changling Trade Road** to **Qingshi Market Town**, where they settle in a modest outer caravan quarter. The father's conviction records and damaged reputation prevent him from obtaining another trusted office. The household survives through irregular accounting, loading, repair, and small trading work, and some relatives eventually separate from the father to escape his continuing grievance.

Young Tomas grows up around warehouses, freight pricing, caravan contracts, merchant disputes, and people who alternately judge him through his family name or his own conduct. During approximately ten years of irregular accounting and trading work, Oren teaches Tomas useful commercial skills while repeatedly presenting himself as a gifted man robbed of his rightful future.

Oren dies at age 52 from an ordinary chronic illness aggravated by declining health and delayed treatment. He is not assassinated by the village, Rovan's remnants, or a hidden sponsor. Before dying, he asks 19-year-old Tomas to restore the family's status and avenge its humiliation. Tomas then leaves Qingshi for **Yunzhou Commercial City** carrying both practical commercial education and Oren's poisoned interpretation of history. This two-stage path later helps him enter the agricultural logistics world and meet his future wife.

The three location names use stable proper-name roots with localized functional descriptors:

- **Changling Trade Road / 长岭商道 / Changling-Handelsstraße** connects the village, mountain routes, Qingshi, and the wider regional market system.
- **Qingshi Market Town / 青石镇 / Marktstadt Qingshi** is the caravan settlement where the exiled household begins its new life.
- **Yunzhou Commercial City / 云州城 / Handelsstadt Yunzhou** is the diverse regional center where Tomas builds his independent fortune, enters agricultural logistics, and meets his future wife.

All three are fictional locations rather than representations of a real-world map. Exact road geometry and district layouts remain production details for the later campaign-map pass.

The predecessor's fear and emergency responsibilities make the decision understandable but not just. Investigating possible accomplices was legitimate; assuming guilt through family association was not. The modern village's evidence procedures, medical treatment of captives, documented custody, and Regional Civil Protection Council later demonstrate institutional learning from this failure.

**Unresolved:** The final visual identities of Su Yan, Brann Holt, Qiao Lan, her family, and Rovan.

### 8.5 The current elder's historical connection

**Confirmed historical age and status:** The current wise elder is **26 years old** during the historical battle, approximately forty years before Stage 1. He is a governance apprentice under the previous-generation elder, assists with records, public notices, and emergency coordination, studies literature, and is an experienced martial artist without yet being the village's strongest fighter or senior decision-maker. This makes him capable of detecting the documentary inconsistencies and coordinating defenders while still lacking authority to overrule his predecessor after the crisis.

His canonical personal-name mapping is **Sheng Guo** in English and German and **盛国** in Simplified Chinese. **Sheng** is the family name and **Guo** is the given name. Historical selection presents him as **Sheng Guo — Civic Apprentice** or the localized equivalent with **盛国** in Chinese; modern dialogue primarily uses **Wise Elder** or **Elder Sheng**, while formal Museum records use **Sheng Guo, Village Elder**. Role titles are localized independently and must never replace or retransliterate the approved Simplified Chinese name **盛国**.

He is therefore approximately 66 during Stage 1, approximately 69 during Stage 2, and in his early seventies during the later technological transformation. The approved local facial reference for both his young and elderly visual continuity is **`GuoPhoto.jpg`** in the project root. The historical model sheet will translate that reference into the game's cel-shaded style as a recognizable 26-year-old version of the elder while preserving shared facial structure across ages. No image transformation is performed merely by recording this decision.

His confirmed combat identity is the **Measured Hand civic-defense method**, a compact system used for protection, escort, and mediation rather than battlefield dominance. It emphasizes precise open-hand strikes, short evasive steps, controlled positioning, wrist releases, off-balancing, non-submission restraints, low sweeps, interruption, strong Exact Block and Counter conversions, environmental redirection, and protection of civilians or allies. It is neither a weapon-dependent style nor a branch of the master's Stone-and-Current Method.

Measured Hand produces less direct damage than the master's system but excels at timing, disarming, flank interception, and creating safe openings. In the historical pair, the master controls the front line and applies stronger pressure while the young elder protects objectives and disrupts threats; both remain independently viable in solo play.

As a young protector, he leads villagers against the bandits enabled by Tomas's father. He uses force to defend people and end the threat, not to pursue wealth or revenge. This event becomes an early expression of the campaign's central principle and the playable historical battle connected directly to the betrayal.

When Tomas's father betrayed the village, the young future elder agreed that the guilty man had to be stopped and held accountable. He opposed punishing and exiling the entire family. When his predecessor rejected that argument, however, he complied with the decision and did not risk his position by resisting further. He has regretted that incomplete resistance ever since.

His rejected alternative is an **individual regional judgment and restitution plan**. Tomas's father would be removed immediately from the steward's office; stolen goods and diverted funds would be recovered; only assets obtained through the crime would be confiscated; and lawful family property would remain protected. The father would be transferred for an individual regional trial, receive a substantial custodial sentence for fraud, conspiracy, and enabling the armed assault, be permanently barred from trusted public, reserve-management, or trade office, and owe restitution to Qiao Lan's surviving family and other people harmed. Every relative would receive separate review and protection.

The proposal does not promise the father restored office, status, or an automatic right to return after completing his sentence. It separates accountability from inherited guilt. By rejecting it in favor of family-wide exile, the previous elder harms innocent relatives while allowing the guilty father to avoid a formal regional trial; the father later distorts that outcome into a story of wholly baseless persecution.

After the ruling, he provides limited humanitarian assistance without pretending that it corrects the injustice. He ensures that the family receives food, clothing, travel provisions, and safe passage. He intervenes to prevent angry villagers from attacking or publicly humiliating them during departure and routes practical assistance through **Lin Mei / 林梅**, an independent caravan matron and regional courier, rather than openly defying his predecessor. Lin is the surname and Mei the given name; English and German dialogue may address her as **Madam Lin**.

Lin Mei is 44 during the historical crisis. She co-manages a respected regional caravan service and has organized legitimate trade journeys to the village for years, but she does not belong to its governing council. She delivers supplies to the guarded outpost, escorts the family safely along the Changling Trade Road to Qingshi, prevents hostile villagers or opportunistic raiders from interfering, and records the departure date, travelers, provisions, and destination in her caravan ledger. Her assistance is humanitarian: she neither conceals the father's guilt nor obstructs lawful evidence. She is not a fighter, conspirator, playable hero, or substitute parent for Tomas.

She also carries a sealed record packet outside the village so that the previous elder cannot become the sole custodian of the case. Lin continues caravan work for approximately twelve years after the exile. When she retires, she writes a restrained factual witness statement and deposits it together with the original ledger at the Regional Trade and Civic Archive. She then settles in Qingshi, remains independent of Tomas's household, and dies before the modern campaign. Her final visual identity remains to be designed.

The evidence survives through a **split-custody chain** rather than one authoritative collection:

1. The official village archive retains the original accusation, inventory discrepancies, depot recovery list, captive testimony, judgment, and exile order.
2. Lin Mei delivers an authenticated packet containing the central evidence and the young elder's written objection to a **Regional Trade and Civic Archive**.
3. The young elder retains his working notes, draft proposal for individual judgment, and personal account of his incomplete resistance. As the present-day elder, he later transfers these papers openly to the Village Museum and Archive rather than keeping them as private leverage.
4. Lin Mei's caravan ledger independently records the departure date, family members, provisions, route, destination, and receipt for delivery of the sealed regional packet; her retirement statement later authenticates her own role without claiming knowledge she did not possess.

Later investigators authenticate the history by comparing handwriting, seals, paper, dates, inventory identifiers, and mutually consistent entries across the four collections.
