These records allow the current elder to speak to Tomas's son with verifiable evidence rather than asking the young man to trust his memory or authority alone.

The young elder does not maintain long-term contact after the exile. His assistance reduces immediate danger but does not prevent later hardship, prejudice, Tomas's radicalization, or the family's separation from the village. He therefore regards what he did as humane but insufficient.

This regret does not invalidate his later wisdom; it explains it. His eventual ideal resolution rests on a lesson learned through failure: authority must distinguish individual guilt from inherited identity, and protection must not become collective vengeance.

**Unresolved:** Lin Mei's final visual identity and Sheng Guo's historical costume and final model-sheet details.

### 8.6 Tomas's son

**Planned:** Tomas's son is **Kael Rell** in English and German and **凯尔·雷尔** in Simplified Chinese. He is **24 during Stage 2**, approximately 15 years after Tomas isolated him from Xu Lian at age nine. Depending on the later stage timeline, he is approximately 24–25 when first introduced.

Kael trained in martial arts from childhood and developed strong bonds with other practitioners. He initially places absolute faith in Tomas's account of the family's history. After learning the truth, he chooses to retain the Rell surname. This does not express continued obedience to Tomas; Kael rejects the idea that a family name carries inherited guilt and decides to redefine its meaning through protection rather than revenge.

Kael's combat identity is a **modern pressure-boxing and chain-wrestling system** developed through full-contact academy training. It combines compact head movement, guarded forward pressure, body hooks, short uppercuts, wrestling entries, trips, carries, positional control, and controlled ground follow-ups. It is neither a traditional village style nor a branch of Fire Warden's Sanda and joint-control system.

Kael excels at pursuing and controlling one dangerous target. His Exact Blocks can convert into body counters or takedown attempts, while his ground routes reposition the opponent instead of sustaining infinite pins. His aerial tools are short and committed—such as a descending shoulder strike or jumping knee—and deliberately weaker than Wind Sentinel's aerial game. He has no default projectile, limited wide-area crowd control, shorter reach, and meaningful commitment risk, preventing his pressure and interruption resistance from making him universally superior.

Kael's wider martial-arts school remains mostly unnamed, while two core companions carry the continuing story. **Soren Beck / 索伦·贝克**, age 25 during Stage 2, is Kael's oldest training partner and a defensive savate fighter who uses long-range precision kicks and evasive footwork. He trusts Kael but has always questioned Tomas's secrecy and revenge rhetoric.

**Chen Yue / 陈悦**, age 23 during Stage 2, is a judo practitioner and trained emergency responder. She emphasizes safe throws, restraint, evacuation, and medical assistance and is the first member of the group willing to refuse an order when civilians or surrendered opponents would be endangered.

Soren and Chen Yue initially accompany Kael because of loyalty to him rather than devotion to Tomas. Their willingness to disagree helps Kael's eventual change grow from observed conduct and trusted relationships rather than one persuasive speech. They later become co-founders of his independent civilian protection group. They are not canonical Story Mode selections, but both become playable in completed Mission Remixes and Random Operations after their Wayfarer Watch content is unlocked.

Kael receives a **two-step moral introduction** before his capture. He, Soren, and Chen Yue are first shown protecting an unaffiliated caravan from ordinary bandits. They use disciplined force, assist injured people, and take nothing from the defeated attackers. A later meeting with Tomas's courier reveals their connection to the revenge campaign without erasing the protective conduct the player has already witnessed.

In a later operation, Tomas falsely tells Kael that the village and Regional Civil Protection Council are transporting falsified evidence and a coerced witness. Kael knowingly leads a targeted but unlawful interception under strict nonlethal orders. He therefore bears responsibility for attacking a lawful escort and for the resulting injuries and damage even though he believes he is exposing an injustice.

When civilians become endangered, Chen Yue abandons the objective to protect and treat them, Soren urges withdrawal, and Kael orders the team to retreat. Kael remains behind to cover them and is defeated and captured by the village patrol without humiliation. He has not participated in kidnapping or deliberate attacks on civilians, but the story does not declare him innocent. He later acknowledges his unlawful violence and begins making amends through independent protection work. Exact stage placement and encounter layout remain for the campaign breakdown.

His change follows a layered sequence rather than one persuasive speech:

1. **Defeat without humiliation:** In a future conflict, the son participates in a skirmish against the village patrol and is captured alive. The patrol treats his injuries, protects surrendered fighters, and does not humiliate him. This contradicts Tomas's portrayal of the village as inherently cruel.
2. **The elder's admission:** The current elder presents evidence of the grandfather's real betrayal while openly admitting that punishing the whole family was unjust and that his own limited resistance was insufficient.
3. **Independent records:** The son examines the official case evidence, the elder's preserved written objection, his mother's letters, and financial records of Tomas's later fraud and custody manipulation.
4. **Observation:** He sees that the modern village reinvests prosperity in education, medicine, public infrastructure, civil protection, and regional cooperation rather than merely hoarding wealth.
5. **Confrontation:** He asks Tomas to reconcile these facts with the story he was taught. Tomas dismisses the evidence, attacks the mother's credibility, or demands loyalty regardless of truth.
6. **Neutral separation:** The son rejects the revenge campaign, refuses to fight for either side, and parts ways with Tomas so he can determine his own path.
7. **Reunion decision:** After gaining emotional distance, he receives a verified request to meet his mother and independently chooses whether to accept, decline, or defer it.

Neutrality is an intermediate moral position: he no longer assists revenge, but he is not required to trust or immediately join the village. His conclusion grows from mercy, evidence, observation, and Tomas's own response rather than an instant conversion.

After a period of neutrality, Kael, Soren, and Chen Yue co-found the **Wayfarer Watch / 行旅守望队 / Reisendenwacht**, an independent civilian mutual-aid cooperative. It is not a village auxiliary, commercial mercenary company, or enforcement arm of the Regional Civil Protection Council.

The Wayfarer Watch operates along the Changling Trade Road, Qingshi's outer routes, mountain settlements, and rural transport corridors toward Yunzhou. It protects travelers, remote communities, medical shipments, researchers, and evacuation routes. Transparent escort fees, cooperative grants, and donations may sustain its work, but it refuses offensive contracts, debt collection, political intimidation, raids, and secret detention.

The group coordinates with the Regional Civil Protection Council during serious incidents while remaining institutionally independent. It documents uses of force, treatment of captives, recovered property, and civilian injuries. Its canonical Character Chronicle missions demonstrate Kael's self-defined protective use of force and his effort to make amends through conduct rather than instant forgiveness. He does not immediately join the village patrol or transfer his loyalty to the elder.

During the final sponsor crisis, the independent group voluntarily cooperates with the village because their protective objectives align. The son may become a selectable guest hero while retaining his separate affiliation. His Meet Now, Reply and Meet Later, or Not Yet answer to his mother does not alter this role, and permanent village membership is not required for a peaceful conclusion.

Kael's guest-hero unlock occurs after he has reviewed the evidence, left Tomas, accepted responsibility for the unlawful interception, co-founded the Wayfarer Watch, and completed its first canonical protection Chronicle without village supervision. At that milestone he becomes profile-wide selectable in the Academy, Random Operations, completed Mission Remixes, and other non-canonical free-selection modes.

In canonical Story Mode, Kael becomes selectable only from the later chapter in which the Wayfarer Watch voluntarily joins the village against the sponsor. He cannot be inserted retroactively into Stages 1 or 2 or earlier canonical encounters. The unlock is independent of the mother–son reunion choice, difficulty, score, credits, and performance grade. Duplicate-hero restrictions remain active. Soren and Chen Yue remain unavailable in canonical Story and Chapter Challenge selections but become selectable in completed Mission Remixes and Random Operations through the Wayfarer Watch unlock path.

Kael's confrontation and capture occur in Stage 4, while his voluntary Wayfarer Watch alliance with the village occurs in Stage 7. The exact Stage 4 encounter structure is defined by the Stage 4 specification.

**Planned (accepted 2026-11-03, campaign review G7):** the first Wayfarer Watch Chronicle, working title ***The Road Between***, is one short Character Chronicle set between Stages 4 and 7. It opens in the Mission Hub after Stage 6. Kael, Soren and Chen Yue are its canonical trio, protecting a mountain caravan from road predators without village supervision. Its first clear unlocks Kael for free-selection modes and starts Soren's and Chen Yue's unlock path. Its specification is written before roadmap milestone M38.

**Planned (campaign review G8):** Kael's playable kit (for Stages 7 and 8 and free modes), Lin Lan's kit, and Soren's and Chen Yue's kits are written in the format of the Stage 5 hero contract (15 named normals, Specials, Ultimates) before their content wave: Kael and Lin Lan for Release 2, Soren and Chen Yue with *The Road Between*. His detailed frame data remains a production decision. Soren's and Chen Yue's model sheets, move lists, frame data, and precise Wayfarer Watch unlock presentation remain later production decisions.

### 8.7 Convergent mother-son reunion branch

**Confirmed (aligned with the Stage 8 specification on 2026-11-03):** The reunion choice is a personal narrative branch, not a campaign branch. The evidence reaches the son before the choice, so no mandatory historical fact depends on whether he agrees to meet his mother.

Xu Lian's invitation reaches Kael through the Regional Civil Protection Council during the Stage 8 epilogue, after he has left Tomas, reviewed the evidence, and led the Wayfarer Watch's independent protection work. This cooling-off period lets him make the choice after demonstrating his own values rather than while injured, detained, or emotionally dependent on either family.

- **Meet Now:** He chooses a neutral meeting in the quiet public courtyard of the **Yunzhou Cooperative Exchange**, which belongs to neither the Xu family enterprise nor the village. A Council liaison remains available nearby but does not participate. The conversation begins cautiously and does not create instant forgiveness, restored custody, or a perfect family reunion.
- **Reply and Meet Later:** He answers the invitation and agrees to meet after more time, without a promised date. The ending preserves the coming meeting without showing it.
- **Not Yet:** He acknowledges the authenticated evidence but is not ready to meet. This is not a rejection: Xu Lian respects the boundary and sends no repeated requests, and the decision is not framed as cruelty, failure, or the morally inferior route.

All three states share the same canonical outcome: the son has left Tomas, rejected inherited revenge, and gained access to his mother's evidence; her testimony enters the historical record; and the main campaign, fighting stages, and peaceful ending remain unchanged. The save profile records `meet_now`, `meet_later`, or `not_yet` for later personal scenes, while campaign progression checks only `son_left_tomas`.

The choice grants no score, credits, achievement, combat power, costume, superior ending rank, or other material advantage. This protects the son's autonomy and prevents the game from rewarding one emotionally sensitive decision as the objectively correct answer.

### 8.8 Tomas's final fate

**Planned:** Tomas survives the final campaign arc. When the sponsor's organization treats him as a disposable instrument, the collapse of his network, his son's rejection of inherited revenge, and the public reconstruction of the historical record finally force him to confront what his obsession has destroyed.

Tomas assists in exposing the sponsor's intermediary network. His cooperation may begin partly from self-preservation, but it ultimately includes a deliberate refusal to continue the revenge cycle. This action is meaningful without functioning as an automatic redemption.

He is captured alive and faces a lawful, documented process through legitimate regional institutions. Stolen or fraudulently obtained assets are traced and used for restitution where possible. His cooperation does not erase fraud, custody manipulation, kidnapping, armed raids, or the exploitation of other people. He receives no instant pardon, does not simply return to the village, and is not promised forgiveness from his former wife or son.

The public account acknowledges both truths separately: Tomas suffered an unjust collective punishment as a child, and Tomas remains responsible for the crimes he chose as an adult. His personal peace comes from relinquishing revenge rather than escaping consequences.

Tomas remains a noncombatant strategist. The final physical boss belongs to the sponsor's organization; Tomas is never converted into a martial-arts boss merely to provide a climactic fight.

### 8.9 Intended dramatic function

**Planned:** Tomas is neither a simple monster nor a misunderstood innocent. His intelligence and childhood grievance make his choices understandable, but he repeatedly chooses exploitation and revenge. His son provides a future path for the family to break the inherited cycle without erasing accountability.

The campaign's eventual peace should require truth, responsibility, and a rejection of inherited revenge. It should not depend on pretending that the father's betrayal, the collective punishment, or Tomas's crimes never occurred.

## 9. Historical and Character Chronicle missions

**Confirmed structure:** When the present-day investigation uncovers the decisive historical records, Story Mode launches a mandatory story-integrated Character Chronicle showing how Tomas's father betrayed the village and how the previous-generation elder discovered and stopped the plan. Players therefore experience the past rather than receiving every fact through exposition. After its first completion, the Chronicle remains permanently available through Mission Mode.

The historical battle introduces two additional playable heroes from the earlier generation: the young future elder and a distinct earlier Mountain Guardian who later becomes the kung fu master of both present-day Mountain Guardian and River Scout. It should:

- Show the father's genuine betrayal clearly enough that later debate does not rest on a false accusation.
- Show why the elder believed urgent action was necessary.
- Preserve the controversy of extending punishment to the entire family.
- Reveal new evidence that recontextualizes Tomas's grievance without making earlier reliable facts false.
- Avoid allowing past heroes to know facts that only later generations uncover.

The first completion occurs as mandatory Stage 5 and uses the historically correct young elder and Liang Zhen. After completion, the canonical version is archived under Character Chronicle and its non-canonical all-hero Mission Remix becomes available. The approved detailed Stage 5 record defines the present-day framing, five-act route, eight ordinary encounters, interactive historical systems, in-engine state-changing scenes, illustrated montage, Flow-safe clips, and still fallbacks.

The earlier Mountain Guardian's confirmed personal name is **Liang Zhen** in English and German and **梁震** in Simplified Chinese. **Liang** is the family name and **Zhen** is the given name. He is **31 years old** during the historical battle, five years older than the young elder. He is presented historically as **Liang Zhen — Field Protector**, a respected senior protector with greater practical combat experience who has begun teaching basic martial principles to younger villagers but has not yet become the legendary master remembered in the modern era. In the modern Academy and dialogue he is **Master Liang**; formal Museum records may identify him as **Liang Zhen, Stone-and-Current Master**. Role titles are localized independently while the approved Simplified Chinese personal name remains **梁震**.

Liang Zhen is not a younger visual copy of the present Mountain Guardian and remains a unique person with his own face, physique, clothing, silhouette, temperament, and combat identity. "Earlier Mountain Guardian" may remain only as an internal continuity description, not his player-facing name.

After the historical battle, he develops into an experienced martial master. Years later he teaches kung fu to both founding heroes, adapting the same principles differently: Mountain Guardian develops grounded structure, protection, control, and deliberate power, while River Scout develops footwork, timing, redirection, mobility, and pursuit. Their different identities therefore grow from one broad tradition without sharing duplicate move sets.

His confirmed martial system is the fictional **Stone-and-Current Method**. **Stone** teaches stable stance, guarded structure, short deliberate power, leverage, clinch control, safe takedowns, and protection of another person without surrendering position. **Current** teaches angled footwork, lane movement, redirection, pursuit, retreat, momentum conversion, and movement through multiple attackers without becoming surrounded.

At age 31 he applies both principles as a balanced all-rounder. Later, Mountain Guardian specializes in Stone while River Scout specializes in Current, with each retaining limited principles from the other branch. His lasting injury eventually encourages him to analyze and teach the method more systematically without implying that the injury itself grants new ability. The modern Academy may demonstrate the shared foundation and the two students' different specializations.

During the historical battle, the more experienced protector initially shields the younger governance apprentice during combat. Later, raiders release or overturn a sabotaged supply wagon near the damaged storehouse. He uses the Stone principle to stop or redirect it long enough for trapped villagers to escape, but his **left knee and lower leg** are pinned and permanently injured against the stone loading platform. Rovan attacks while he cannot stand; the young future elder intercepts Rovan with Measured Hand counters, creates space, and pulls or guides his friend to safety. This reversal of protector and rescued friend becomes central to their lifelong bond.

The injury leaves a consistent limp and prevents sustained running, repeated jumping, and extended field combat without preventing him from teaching structure, timing, upper-body technique, or adapted footwork. In older age he may use a plain walking staff and a supportive left-leg brace. Later wearable technology may improve comfort and stability but never erase the injury. Every young and elderly model sheet, animation, Chronicle scene, and Academy appearance must preserve the affected side. The injury is communicated through posture, movement, damaged surroundings, and the rescue sequence rather than graphic bodily imagery.

The shared battle and rescue create a lasting friendship between the martial master and the current elder. During Stages 1 and 2, the master is alive but retired from field combat because of age and the old injury. He remains mentally sharp and serves as a **mentor with adapted demonstrations** in the modern Academy without replacing the elder's civic leadership or the active heroes' responsibilities.

He jointly leads a **Stone-and-Current Foundations** course with Mountain Guardian and River Scout. The master explains philosophy and tactics, demonstrates stationary structure, hand positioning, redirection, and carefully adapted steps, and uses his walking staff or brace naturally when moving between exercises. His students perform sustained movement, advanced techniques, and high-intensity drills. Lessons may cover rooting against pressure, redirecting an advancing enemy, changing between stable defense and mobile pursuit, protecting a co-op partner, and safely reconstructing principles from the historical rescue.

The elderly master is never restored as a modern field fighter. His playable form is the 31-year-old historical version in the canonical Chronicle and its Mission Remix; his older form remains an instructor, narrator, and living witness.

### 9.1 Archive-led Mission Hub

**Confirmed:** Mission Mode opens through one **Archive-led Mission Hub** with four clearly separated destinations: **Character Chronicles**, **Mission Remix**, **Curated Missions**, and **Random Operations**. Story Mode, Mission Mode, and Chapter Challenge remain separate top-level selections. The Academy remains an independent learning destination outside Play and Mission Mode.

Character Chronicles use the Village Museum and Archive as their narrative frame, emphasizing preserved testimony, authenticated records, and historical context. Curated Missions use authored civil-protection case records. Random Operations use a civil-protection operations board so generated missions remain grounded in protecting people and communities rather than becoming unexplained arcade battles.

Before selection, every mission identifies itself as **Canonical**, **Canon-compatible Side Story**, **Non-canonical Remix**, or **Generated Operation**. Locked entries show concise localized and spoiler-safe unlock clues. Hub navigation and status information support pointer, keyboard, gamepad, touch, focus indicators, accessibility labels, and screen readers.

Applicable cheat codes are available throughout Story Mode, Chapter Challenge, Character Chronicles, Mission Remix, Curated Missions, and Random Operations under the shared Cheat-Assisted reward and record restrictions. Academy allows them only in Free Practice and Cheat Practice. The session-scoped `SAFEPASSAGE` modifier prevents mandatory protected people or objects from crossing their visible Critical condition into a completion-blocking failed, dead, Compromised, Lost, or permanently destroyed state. It preserves warnings, damage presentation, escort and route requirements, enemies, bosses, evidence work, and all required player actions, and never completes an objective automatically.

This structure preserves a clear boundary between campaign truth, canon-compatible side stories, experimental replay, and generated challenges while allowing the catalogue to grow across later stages.

### 9.2 Character Chronicle and Mission Remix

**Planned:** Historical and character-centered side missions use two presentation layers:

1. **Character Chronicle:** The canonical first completion uses the historically correct fixed participants. Tomas's son and his independent martial-arts companions protect travelers and remote communities from bandits in their own Chronicle, while Sheng Guo (盛国 in Simplified Chinese) and Liang Zhen (梁震 in Simplified Chinese) are the canonical pair for the historical bandit-defense Chronicle.
2. **Mission Remix:** After a Chronicle is completed, the mission becomes replayable with any eligible unlocked heroes as a non-canonical combat challenge. Remix results may support score, records, difficulty, and combat rewards without rewriting who participated in history.

The son's Chronicle demonstrates that he is capable of using strength against predators and is not naturally cruel; Tomas has distorted whom he identifies as an enemy. The elder's Chronicle demonstrates restraint after victory and establishes his belief that defensive force must stop when the threat ends.

The historical Chronicle supports all required play modes. In solo, the player selects either historical hero while the other protects a parallel route, civilian shelter, or supply position and remains present in story scenes without requiring continuous companion AI. In co-op, the Stone-and-Current master supplies front-line control and pressure while the Measured Hand young elder intercepts flankers, protects objectives, and creates openings through counters and redirection. Completing the canonical Chronicle unlocks its all-hero Mission Remix.

**Confirmed catalogue and unlock model:** Character Chronicles use a **Paired Chronicle Record**. Records are arranged by their position in the campaign timeline. Spoiler-sensitive future entries appear only as concealed archive slots with localized unlock clues rather than titles, portraits, or facts that reveal unreached story content.

Each unlocked record shows its era and campaign placement, canonical participants, solo and co-op availability, objectives, completed difficulties, discoveries, achievements, and Museum connections. A mandatory Chronicle launches naturally from Story Mode for its first completion. Its results sequence then presents two explicit events:

1. **Record Authenticated:** the canonical Chronicle becomes permanently replayable with its historically correct participants.
2. **Mission Remix Unlocked:** the same authored mission becomes immediately available with eligible unlocked heroes.

Remix access requires no purchase, score threshold, or higher-difficulty clear. Canonical Replay and Mission Remix remain visually distinct and always display their canon status. Both reference the same versioned mission content package so fixes, objectives, environments, and deterministic state cannot drift into contradictory copies.

### 9.3 Curated Missions

**Confirmed:** Mission Mode adds **Curated Missions**, initially planned as four handcrafted missions with different objective structures. "Curated Missions" is the player-facing name; downloadable content or DLC remains a technical packaging and distribution description rather than an automatic claim that the content is paid.

The approved localized catalogue titles are:

| Mission | English | Simplified Chinese | German |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Storm over Qinglan | 青岚风暴 | Sturm über Qinglan |
| 2 | The Medicine Road | 送药之路 | Die Medizinroute |
| 3 | The Vanishing Survey | 消失的勘测资料 | Die verschwundene Vermessung |
| 4 | No Escape at Reedwater | 芦水渡口：无处可逃 | Kein Entkommen in Schilfwasser |

Each Curated Mission is a **canon-compatible side story**: its events can occur consistently within the approved campaign timeline, may deepen characters and communities, and may add localized dialogue, achievements, Museum discoveries, environments, and bounded rewards. It contains no fact required to understand the main campaign, no mandatory sponsor evidence, and no outcome that changes a canonical stage or ending.

Players who do not install or acquire a Curated Mission cannot lose access to an essential hero, starting or required combat technique, difficulty, main-story fact, campaign ending, or required progression system. Curated Missions use **Optional Prestige Rewards**: every first clear grants itemized Credits, the mission's Museum case record, and its Canon-Compatible Side Story Chronicle entry. Mission 4 also grants Lin Lan's approved noncompetitive content-access unlock. Optional objectives and higher ranks may grant themed costumes, profile emblems, titles, artwork, music, or Museum collectibles, but never exclusive required power, mandatory sponsor evidence, main-story progression, a required technique, or a stronger paid advantage.

Replays grant only bounded Credits through the shared anti-farming rules, and first-clear or rank-improvement bonuses cannot be farmed repeatedly. Cheat-Assisted clears preserve mission completion, side-story viewing, Museum access, and Lin Lan's content-access unlock while granting no score, rank, Credits, Hero Mastery, prestige reward, or competitive achievement. If a package is later sold separately, these fairness boundaries remain unchanged.

Curated Missions use an **Objective-First Protective Duty Grade** rather than treating enemy-defeat score as the primary measure. Every result prioritizes primary objective condition, then protection, restraint and collateral control, combat mastery, and resource/time efficiency. *Storm over Qinglan* emphasizes rescued-group condition and safe evacuation routes; *The Medicine Road* emphasizes convoy condition, protected cargo, and stolen-case recovery; *The Vanishing Survey* emphasizes genuine equipment recovered, carrier interception, and avoidance of costly decoy errors; and *No Escape at Reedwater* emphasizes live capture, disabled escape routes, civilian protection, and restrained collateral damage.

C, B, A, and S thresholds are validated independently for each mission, difficulty, and solo/co-op configuration. S means excellent rather than perfect execution and never requires one hero, move, route, or optional purchase. A failed attempt may be retried but does not authenticate the side-story Chronicle entry or grant clear rewards. Cheat-Assisted completion retains the approved access path while remaining unranked and noncompetitive.

Curated Mission packages are versioned, preserve localized and accessibility fallbacks, and declare their supported solo, same-device co-op, and local-Wi-Fi configurations. Whether a package is free or paid remains a separate commercial decision and cannot alter these fairness boundaries.

Curated Missions use **Story-Timeline Availability without Mission Dependencies**. *Storm over Qinglan* and *No Escape at Reedwater* become available after Stage 3, *The Medicine Road* after Stage 5, and *The Vanishing Survey* after Stage 6. Until then, installed entries show localized spoiler-safe clues. Access requires no score, rank, difficulty, Credits, ownership, or completion of another Curated Mission. Reedwater follows Qinglan chronologically but does not require it. Cheat-Assisted Story completion satisfies these content-access gates, and a later installation opens immediately when its gate is already met. The Mission Hub recommends chronology without forcing it.

The four approved missions are the **initial Curated Mission collection**, not a closed set. Later canon-compatible side stories may be inserted at appropriate points across the campaign timeline. Every addition must preserve the permanent Combat Constitution, remain optional to the main story, and use its own independent timeline gate rather than creating a compulsory chain of DLC ownership.

The collection uses a **Permanent Expandable Case Catalogue**. Every case declares a stable case ID, package version, campaign-timeline position, independent Story gate, canon label, supported player configurations, objective and grading profiles, rewards, content dependencies, localization and accessibility data, and optional Automatic Highlight profile. The Mission Hub inserts new cases chronologically and supports filtering by timeline, location, objective family, installed state, completion, rank, and eligible heroes.

Installed or acquired cases never expire or rotate out. New cases may add heroes, environments, weapons, mounts, enemies, bosses, cosmetics, music, and Museum material without changing the Combat Constitution. No case may require ownership of another optional case. Related cases may provide continuity dialogue when earlier records exist but must include a self-contained recap otherwise. Stable identifiers and migrations preserve compatible records and replays; incompatible historical results remain visible as Legacy records.

Same-device co-op uses one installed case. Local-Wi-Fi uses **Compatible Package on Every Device**: both peers must hold and validate the same mission package and gameplay assets before readiness, and the host never streams or silently substitutes them during play. Commercial policy may later permit a host guest-pass, but the guest device must still download and validate the package. Uninstalling a case preserves completion, records, rewards, and hero-unlock state. Lin Lan remains recorded as unlocked; if her reusable hero assets are absent she is shown as **Unlocked—Assets Not Installed** until reinstall, after which no repeated clear is required.

The first Curated Mission collection uses four different **Protective Duty** objectives:

1. **Rescue and Evacuation:** locate endangered civilians, stabilize their situations, and open short authored evacuation routes without long escort AI.
2. **Mobile Escort:** protect a moving medicine or equipment convoy, clear obstructions, defend temporary stops, and preserve cargo.
3. **Recover and Secure:** track stolen community property, records, or technical equipment, identify the genuine target, and recover it before withdrawal.
4. **Nonlethal Apprehension:** pursue and capture a dangerous commander alive through defense pressure, counters, throws, disarms, Resolve, and controlled finishers.

These missions may introduce canon-compatible side-story locations, weapons, mounts, environments, enemy roles, bosses, and even new heroes, but every addition must use the unchanged shared combat engine.

#### Curated Mission 1 Storm over Qinglan

**Confirmed:** **Storm over Qinglan** occurs approximately 10–14 days after Stage 3 and before Stage 4. A severe mountain storm causes landslides around Qinglan. Remaining unaffiliated bandits exploit the emergency to steal relief supplies and block evacuation routes. The incident is not another Tomas operation and provides no sponsor evidence.

The primary objective is **Rescue and Evacuation**. The heroes rescue bridge-maintenance workers, a traveling clinic team, and residents sheltering near a damaged service tunnel. Each group follows a short authored evacuation route. Secure, Strained, and Critical condition bands affect rank and presentation, but all groups survive canonically and no hidden execution timer exists.

The mission introduces a storm-damaged mountain pass, damaged cable bridge, runoff channels, unstable timber cover, and deterministic falling-debris hazards. A temporary **Rescue Hook Pole** uses the existing pickable-weapon schema. Relief-looter variants use existing enemy-role and combat-resource rules, while a unique raider lieutenant boss attempts to seize the final relief wagon. Weather is presentation plus authored deterministic hazards rather than unpredictable simulation.

Mountain Guardian, River Scout, Wind Sentinel, and Fire Warden are selectable. The mission introduces no hero. Its Museum discovery documents Qinglan's community rescue network without changing the main campaign.

**Confirmed route structure:** *Storm over Qinglan* uses a **Five-Segment Rescue Circuit** targeting approximately 30–40 minutes for a first clear and 20–28 minutes for an experienced replay. Its baseline contains approximately eight mandatory combat encounters plus the raider-lieutenant boss, with two optional combat areas reserved for the approved branch pass. Final counts remain validated encounter data.

1. **Qinglan Relief Staging Yard:** the heroes repel looters stealing emergency supplies, receive the Rescue Hook Pole, and learn the visible rescue-condition language through one short encounter and a deterministic debris warning.
2. **Runoff Road — Traveling Clinic Team:** a clinic vehicle is pinned behind fallen timber. The heroes stabilize the route, prevent medicine theft, and protect one short relocation to a nearby safe shelter rather than escorting the team through the whole mission.
3. **Storm Cable Bridge — Maintenance Workers:** the heroes cross the partially usable bridge, interrupt warned runoff surges, protect its damaged anchor assembly, and open a short evacuation route. The Hook Pole can move tagged debris and interrupt selected enemies without becoming mandatory for ordinary combat.
4. **Service Tunnel Approach — Sheltering Residents:** two approach encounters lead to unstable timber cover. The heroes stabilize it and protect the residents through the mission's strongest short evacuation-pressure sequence.
5. **Relief Turnout — Final Wagon Defense:** the rescued groups remain behind a safe combat boundary while the remaining looters attack the final relief wagon. One ordinary defense encounter leads into the boss battle using wagon cover, runoff channels, and breakable timber; indispensable relief supplies cannot be destroyed.

Checkpoints occur after each rescued group and immediately before the boss. Solo interactions receive protected readable windows. Co-op permits one hero to stabilize or guide while the other defends but never requires two players. Only clearly warned designated attacks and hazards can reduce a rescue group's condition, and no hidden countdown exists.

**Confirmed rescue-condition model:** Each group uses one shared **Secure → Strained → Critical** condition rather than separate civilian Health bars. Ordinary enemy contacts, incidental collisions, cosmetic weather, and off-screen activity cannot lower it. One band is lost only when a designated objective attacker completes a warned action, an authored runoff or debris hazard is not interrupted, or a required stabilization interaction is abandoned after its visible danger phase begins. One event removes at most one band, followed by a short protection grace.

Critical never becomes death or mission failure. Further incidents may reduce the bounded objective subscore without creating a hidden bad ending. Each group receives one contextual recovery opportunity: recover the clinic emergency case, fasten the bridge's secondary safety anchor, or reinforce the tunnel shelter. Completion restores one band; when already Secure it grants a small route-safety result bonus rather than combat power. Reaching the safe shelter locks the group's condition permanently.

Solo receives a short protected interaction window, while co-op may divide recovery and defense roles. Difficulty changes threat combinations and warning pressure, not the three states or their meaning. Icons, silhouettes, localized text, sound, and captions communicate transitions without relying on colour. S rank permits a minor recovered mistake and does not require mathematical perfection.

**Confirmed optional branches:** The first branch, **Signal Through the Rain / 雨中信号 / Signal im Regen**, leaves Runoff Road before the cable bridge. Looters are removing batteries and instruments from a damaged cliffside warning post. One optional combat group protects the components; restoring the relay gives one later runoff sequence a longer visible warning and grants a route-safety result bonus without becoming necessary for fair rescue.

The second branch, **Every Crate Accounted For / 物资一件不少 / Jede Kiste zählt**, leaves the route after the Service Tunnel rescue and before Relief Turnout. The heroes recover marked blankets, repair materials, water filters, and emergency food from looters at a small community storehouse. Returning the crates provides one ownership-aware Life, Defense, or Momentum recovery choice per active hero before the boss. These are ordinary present-run supplies, not rare medicine or permanent power.

Either branch expands the **Qinglan Community Rescue Archive / 青岚社区救援档案 / Qinglaner Rettungsarchiv**. The branch achievements use their localized branch titles. Completing both branches in one reward-eligible run grants **Qinglan Stands Together / 青岚同心 / Qinglan hält zusammen**. Missed discoveries remain replayable. Cheat-Assisted completion may reveal the Museum material but cannot award the branch or combined mastery achievements.

**Confirmed enemy roster:** The core relief-looter roles are the **Pass Brawler**, an ordinary close-range fighter; **Relief Snatcher**, a fast lightly defended carrier who moves marked supplies toward a visible withdrawal point; **Hook Looter**, a mid-range controller whose warned utility-hook actions can pull heroes away from interactions or move loose supplies; and **Timber Breaker**, a slow heavy role with long-recovery tagged debris, anchor, and objective-pressure actions.

Extreme adds the **Rainstep Feinter**, whose readable delayed strikes and evasive footwork target heroes without faking objective warnings. Ultimate adds the **Crew Caller**, whose interruptible whistle reserves one authored synchronized flank or objective-pressure action without summoning beyond the encounter budget. Easy emphasizes Brawlers and Snatchers, Medium introduces all core roles, and Hard strengthens combinations before the specialist additions.

Only Relief Snatchers and Timber Breakers receive designated objective actions. Solo permits one objective attacker at a time; co-op permits at most two with staggered warnings. Enemies cannot affect a group after shelter lock. The faction uses patched rain capes, scavenged tools, mismatched clothing, and improvised carts; it has no firearms, grenades, military armour, sponsor equipment, or shared supply seals. Defeated members may surrender or be restrained, and the mission depicts no executions.

**Confirmed Rescue Hook Pole contract:** The portable pole is acquired with `J` at the Relief Staging Yard and uses the shared pickable long-weapon lifecycle, including integrity, hero-affinity actions, throws, drops, disarms, breakage, and `TRUSTYTOOLS`. Its tactical identity is controlled pull, wide spacing, and tagged debris redirection rather than superior damage. Authored rescue uses include moving light timber, drawing a supply case into reach, fastening a bridge safety line, and redirecting one rolling-debris hazard. It cannot pull civilians, bosses, large boulders, vehicles, or indispensable structures.

Mandatory progress never depends on retaining the portable pole. Bridge and tunnel objective nodes provide fixed rescue fittings, and deterministic recovery occurs only where the portable weapon remains tactically relevant. The pole is not a pre-stage loadout, permanent Store weapon, or required purchase.

**Confirmed storm-environment contract:** Runoff surges use deterministic lane profiles with directional visual, audio, caption, and non-colour warnings. Falling debris follows fixed seeded patterns with ground silhouettes and may hit enemies or damage tagged timber. Unstable timber uses Intact, Damaged, and Broken states, with selected pieces producing one safe throwable fragment under the environmental quota. The cable bridge uses authored presentation sway and bounded movement pressure rather than random collapse or physics-dependent outcomes.

The anchor assembly uses Secure, Threatened, and Stabilized objective states and cannot be damaged beyond recovery. The final relief wagon provides cover and breakable exterior components while indispensable supplies remain protected. Hazards pause during briefings, rescue dialogue, checkpoint restoration, and protected interactions. Reduced-motion options reduce rain streaks, bridge sway, and camera movement without changing hazard timing. Solo and co-op use the same hazard schedule; co-op adds role division rather than extra simultaneous hazards.

**Confirmed boss:** The raider lieutenant is **Mara Venn / 玛拉·文**, age 37, known as **the Storm Jackal / 暴雨豺狼 / der Sturmschakal**. She formerly led a legitimate regional salvage and road-recovery crew, then chose to divert recovered goods into black-market resale and organized opportunistic looting. She knowingly steals marked relief supplies and blocks active evacuations despite calling them abandoned salvage. Her practical background explains her route, anchor, cargo, and tool knowledge without excusing her conduct. She has no connection to Tomas, Garran, the sponsor, or the Meridian network.

Mara's first phase uses a reinforced recovery hook for long spacing, controlled pulls, sweeps, tagged timber redirection, and at most one authored support action within the threat budget. The hook has ordinary visible integrity and can be disarmed or broken. Her second phase retains the short reinforced handle as a pry-bar weapon and shifts to compact body strikes, clinch entries, counters, short throws, and wagon-cover repositioning, with punishable failed grapples.

At low Resolve, the third phase adds one clearly telegraphed attempt to release the relief wagon brake toward the runoff slope. Interruption earns a short stagger. Failure damages one exterior wagon component and lowers protection grading without destroying indispensable supplies or failing the mission. Mara then uses shorter desperation strings with no further reinforcements.

She obeys Health, Defense, Resolve, defense-consuming Combo Breaker, anti-infinite, readable transition, and co-op pressure rules. She cannot pull both heroes into an unavoidable attack or use civilians as shields; one tagged partner-rescue response applies to her eligible grab. Defeat produces Resolve break, controlled restraint, medical assessment, and live custody. Local salvage records identify only black-market buyers and provide no sponsor clue.

**Confirmed encounter baseline:** The eight mandatory ordinary encounters are: Staging Yard with Pass Brawlers and one Snatcher; Runoff Road approach introducing Hook Looter; clinic rescue combining Snatchers with one route-threatening Timber Breaker; bridge entrance combining Brawlers and Hooks in narrow lanes; bridge-anchor defense using one Breaker and staggered Snatchers; lower tunnel approach combining Brawlers and Hooks under debris warnings; tunnel-cover stabilization using Breaker-led objective pressure and one Snatcher route; and Relief Turnout using the full core roster before Mara without exhausting boss resources. The warning-post branch emphasizes Hook Looters around stolen instruments, while the storehouse branch emphasizes Snatchers moving relief crates.

Initial mandatory-route solo population targets are approximately 16 ordinary enemies on Easy, 22 on Medium, 25 on Hard, 27 on Extreme including Rainstep Feinter, and 30 on Ultimate including Feinter and Crew Caller. Optional encounters add bounded enemies outside these totals. Easy delays Hook Looter until the bridge and uses only one mandatory Breaker; Medium introduces the full core sequence; Hard strengthens combinations; Extreme adds Feinter only after the first rescue; Ultimate places Crew Caller in final wagon defense and the optional storehouse but never simultaneously with Mara.

Co-op targets approximately 35–40 percent more total threat through ordinary enemies and complementary lanes rather than doubled specialists, hazards, objective attackers, or boss support. Hazard schedules and rescue thresholds remain unchanged. Mara receives at most one non-replenishing support group in Phase 1. An encounter cannot begin until required civilians are inside their authored protection boundary. All population values remain playtest baselines.

**Confirmed recovery catalogue:** Life uses **Warm Rice Porridge / 热米粥 / Warmer Reisbrei**; Defense uses **Mineral Citrus Water / 矿物柑橘水 / Mineral-Zitruswasser**; Momentum uses **Sesame-Nut Bar / 芝麻坚果条 / Sesam-Nuss-Riegel**; and the scarce balanced item is **Qinglan Rescue Meal / 青岚救援餐盒 / Qinglan-Rettungsmahlzeit**. Their effects are gameplay abstractions, not medical claims.

The Medium solo baseline is two Life, two Defense, two Momentum, and one balanced meal. Easy adds one of each common category; Hard removes one authored common item, Extreme two, and Ultimate three. The balanced meal remains at the secured residents' shelter before Relief Turnout on every difficulty. The optional storehouse reward remains a separate ownership-aware choice.

Medium co-op uses three items from each common category and one balanced meal rather than duplicating every container. Validated placements occur at shelters and safe route points, not active civilian danger zones. Items retain `J` carry, consume, and throw behavior. Marked objective supplies cannot be consumed or replaced. `FULLPANTRY` affects only eligible ordinary containers and cannot replace rescue equipment or objectives. Checkpoint restore preserves consumption and carried ownership exactly.

**Confirmed grading model:** Qinglan uses a 100-point **Objective-First Protective Duty Grade**: Rescue Condition 45; Route and Relief Protection 20; Combat Mastery 25; and Resource and Active-Time Efficiency 10. All three groups must finish Secure for S eligibility; any final Strained condition caps rank at A and any final Critical condition caps it at B. A condition that falls and is recovered to Secure does not remove S eligibility. Mara's successful wagon-brake action lowers route protection without automatically preventing S.

Initial tuning candidates are C for clear, B at 60, A at 75, and S at 90 with all groups Secure. Final thresholds are validated per difficulty and solo/co-op profile. Optional branches grant bounded score and compensate their additional time but are not required for S. Active time excludes pause, cinematics, synchronized holds, loading, and protected dialogue. Scenery destruction, harmless repetition, surrendered enemies, sheltered civilians, and protected boss states cannot be farmed. Cheat-Assisted clears retain approved access and Museum progress without score, grade record, Credits, mastery achievement, or prestige reward.

**Confirmed weapon and mount scope:** The unique pickup is the Rescue Hook Pole. Pass Brawlers may drop a **Wooden Cudgel** using an existing short-blunt profile, and Hook Looters may drop a **Short Utility Hook** using an existing short control-tool profile. Mara's reinforced recovery hook and shortened pry-bar handle remain boss equipment and post-capture evidence rather than player pickups. The mission contains no firearms, grenades, or pre-stage weapons.

*Storm over Qinglan* has no playable mount, mounted enemy, horse, or motorbike. The relief wagon is an objective and environmental object rather than a rideable vehicle. `STEADYRIDE` remains visible with a localized **Not Applicable in This Mission** state. This preserves the mission's rescue, footing, and environmental-control identity and distinguishes it from Stage 3's riding content.

**Confirmed achievement catalogue:** The mission uses eight achievements: **After the Storm / 风雨之后 / Nach dem Sturm** for a reward-eligible clear; the approved **Signal Through the Rain** and **Every Crate Accounted For** branch achievements; **Qinglan Stands Together** for both branches in one eligible run; **Everyone Sheltered / 人人安然 / Alle in Sicherheit** for all groups finishing Secure; **Hook, Brace, Rescue / 钩、固、救 / Haken, sichern, retten** for all three contextual recovery actions plus one Hook Pole redirection; **The Wagon Holds / 救援车屹立 / Der Wagen hält** for interrupting Mara's brake release and preserving every exterior wagon component; and **Guardians in the Downpour / 暴雨守护者 / Wächter im Wolkenbruch** for both co-op heroes completing a rescue interaction and tagged partner defense.

The co-op achievement is not required for S, Museum completion, or any solo reward. First reward-eligible clear grants itemized Credits, the case Chronicle, Qinglan Community Rescue Archive, and its music/art entries. First A grants the text-free **Qinglan Rescue Knot** profile emblem; first S grants the localized **Storm Responder** profile title. Branch completion produces the approved Museum upgrade. Rewards remain bounded Credits, presentation, Museum recognition, and cosmetics without combat power. Cheat-Assisted completion unlocks narrative, Chronicle, Museum, art, and music access but none of the eight achievements, Credits, ranks, emblem, or title.

**Confirmed presentation:** A 25–35-second skippable **Storm Warning** opening shows weather crossing Qinglan, blocked routes, the clinic vehicle, damaged bridge, and a distress signal reaching the village. Sheng Guo authorizes the response and Stage 3 trail steward Gao Wen serves as on-site coordinator. The elder briefing and loadout explain the three groups, visible conditions, warnings, and protection priority before readiness. Rescue arrivals, optional state, Mara's entrance, and other state-sensitive material remain in-engine.

A 30–45-second skippable **After the Storm** coda shows all groups sheltered, relief distribution continuing, Mara receiving medical assessment before documented custody, and community repair. Short still inserts reflect group conditions and branch results without altering canonical survival. Generated footage contains no embedded text, dialogue, subtitles, or logos; mandatory content receives English, Simplified Chinese, and German subtitles/transcripts. Both cinematics unlock for Museum replay.

**Confirmed music family:** **Hands through the Rain** contains **Rain over Qinglan** for opening/briefing; **Road beneath the Runoff** for traversal and ordinary combat; **Guide Them Home** for rescue pressure; **The Storm Jackal** for Mara; and **Shelter Lights** for coda and Museum. Restrained strings, wooden percussion, low drums, rain-like shakers, warm winds, and a small community motif distinguish it from Stage 3's *Mist and Resolve*.

Segment-authored rain/runoff ambience and distinct anchor, debris, Hook Pole, supply, shelter, and transition effects remain separate from gameplay warnings. Decorative thunder cannot imitate warnings. Essential cues receive mix priority, captions, and non-audio presentation. Reduced effects preserve timing and information. Generated audio is edited candidate material requiring provenance and commercial-use verification.

**Confirmed Automatic Highlight profile:** Qinglan maps the six approved families to rescue-aware facts. Perfect Combo and Two Threats One Move retain their universal legality and hero-normalization rules. Guardian Intervention recognizes interruption of a committed Snatcher, Breaker, runoff, debris, anchor, or wagon threat before condition/protection loss without requiring last-frame delay. Defensive Mastery recognizes a clean Exact Block or counter against a Breaker objective strike or Mara hook action followed by a legal damage-free punish.

Team Harmony recognizes one co-op hero performing rescue/stabilization while the other interrupts a designated threat, followed by shelter arrival without band loss during that sequence. Environmental Ingenuity recognizes a Hook Pole or debris redirection that interrupts an objective attacker and meaningfully affects another hostile target. Candidates retain protected group, objective state, responsible hero, prevented outcome, and frame facts. Screenshots favour intervention or shelter resolution rather than apparent civilian injury. The system never rewards delaying rescue or accepting damage, remains maximum-three/one-per-family, labels assisted rules, changes no simulation, and grants no gameplay reward.

**Confirmed persistence contract:** The four recovery checkpoints follow the clinic shelter, bridge-worker shelter and finalized anchor, tunnel-resident shelter, and Relief Turnout immediately before Mara. Mission start is the fallback before Checkpoint 1. Each snapshot stores group conditions, incidents, recovery and shelter locks; clinic case, anchor, reinforcement, warning relay, relief crates and branch states; pending Museum state; hazard seed/index; timber, fragment, anchor and wagon state; resolved enemies; hero resources, lives/continues, loadouts and cheats; food, carried items and weapon ownership/integrity/location; score, active time, grade facts, achievement and Highlight state; and co-op authority/recovery history.

Restoration cannot reroll hazards, respawn supplies, improve group condition, repair wagon damage, duplicate weapons, or reopen branches. Results retain stable case/version, configuration, score and four grade components, every group and objective result, branch/anchor/wagon/brake/Hook/supply/environment facts, enemy outcomes, resources, achievements, Museum and Highlight references, active time, continues, recovery and network events. Comparable records separate difficulty, player count, hero configuration, loadout, ruleset, and version; replays use the approved tiered retention. Validated technical recovery preserves eligibility, while ordinary continue behavior follows the shared campaign rules and remains recorded.

**Confirmed player support:** First launch presents a localized **Rescue Operations** control card explaining condition bands, designated objective attackers, current bindings, one-band-per-event and recovery rules, and the absence of a hidden civilian-death timer. It remains available from Guide and pause. Every new interaction receives one safe demonstration before combat pressure, and prompts use current bindings.

Installing the case adds an optional **Storm Rescue Drill** to Academy's Resources, Equipment, and Environments course. Demonstration, guided practice, independent trial, and optional mastery cover objective interruption, stabilization, Hook Pole debris redirection, and shelter arrival. The lesson grants no exclusive technique or required power and never gates the mission.

Rescue states and threats use icon, silhouette, localized text, sound, captions, visible role markers, and warned target lines. Rain, lightning, sway, particles, shake, and reflections have independent reduction controls without changing timing. Screen readers announce objective state transitions. Stabilization supports hold, toggle, and reduced-input alternatives; simulation-changing windows use Assisted records. Pointer, audio, colour, and motion effects are never required.

Hero cheats and `QUIETCROWD`, `FULLPANTRY`, and `HOMECOMING` retain their scopes. `TRUSTYTOOLS` protects carried weapons but not fixed fittings. `STEADYRIDE` is Not Applicable because there is no mount. `SAFEPASSAGE` is Not Applicable because groups cannot enter a completion-blocking failure state and indispensable supplies cannot be destroyed. No new case-specific cheat is added.

**Confirmed Case-to-Archive acceptance matrix:** *Storm over Qinglan* is not release-ready until validation covers all four eligible heroes and representative co-op pairs; every difficulty; Standard, Open, Assisted, and Cheat-Assisted records; solo, same-device co-op, and local-Wi-Fi co-op; all three rescue groups, condition transitions, recoveries, shelter locks, optional branches, enemy roles, specialists, weapons, hazards, Mara phases, brake outcome, capture, checkpoints, reconnect/host recovery, replay facts, Highlights, achievements, Museum material, and rewards. The matrix also covers package installation, update, uninstall/reinstall, version migration and Legacy records; EN, zh-Hans, and DE; keyboard, gamepad, remapping, pointer/touch UI, screen reader, captions, reduced-motion/effects options, and alternative stabilization inputs; and deterministic browser/network behavior at the supported 60-FPS target and 30-FPS minimum without changing simulation timing.

Release requires zero unresolved Critical or High-severity defects and explicit regression evidence against civilian death, off-screen objective damage, rescue or route softlocks, checkpoint rerolls, duplicated items or rewards, route traps, objective-state improvement on restore, and score/resource exploits. Exact encounter counts, timing windows, grade thresholds, and performance budgets remain playtest-tunable production values rather than reopened feature decisions.

**Approved reviewed clarifications:** An equipped Rescue Hook Pole uses a visible contextual Hook-Opportunity Focus: `T` or the authored directional-`T` route performs the tagged rescue action, ordinary weapon attacks remain outside that focus, and `J` retains held-weapon throw/discard priority. Fixed fallback fittings use empty-hand Grab/Interact or the optional separate Interact binding, so no mission-only abstract action is added. Mara's Phase 1 ends at the first legal long-hook break/disarm or the authored Phase-2 threshold; either route converts her boss-only equipment to its shortened-handle state. Phase 2 cannot be skipped, Phase 3 waits for at least one bounded Phase-2 decision cycle, and exact thresholds remain tuning data.

**Curated Mission 1 status:** Feature model and reviewed specification complete and approved. The focused records are `storm_over_qinglan_feature_story_line.md` and `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-01-fight-for-peace-storm-over-qinglan-specification.md`. Implementation planning has not begun.

#### Curated Mission 2 The Medicine Road

**Confirmed:** **The Medicine Road** occurs after Stage 5's present-day investigation and before Stage 6. The village clinic and Regional Civil Protection Council send medicine, diagnostic equipment, and emergency supplies to remote communities. An independent road gang attacks to steal the cargo for resale; the incident has no connection to Tomas or the sponsor.

The primary objective is **Mobile Escort**. The heroes protect a compact clinic cargo vehicle along the Changling Trade Road, clear roadblocks and environmental obstructions, defend two authored repair stops, use mid-class motorbikes in bounded pursuit sections, prevent Cargo Snatchers from removing marked supply cases, and recover any cases taken before the convoy continues. Convoy and cargo condition affect rank, Credits, achievements, dialogue, and Museum presentation, while the essential delivery succeeds canonically.

The route includes a forest road, old service tunnel, stone viaduct, and remote clinic destination. Pickable **Tire Iron** and **Folding Road Baton** profiles use existing weapon rules. Road Blockers, Bike Harriers, Cargo Snatchers, and ranged Lookouts use shared enemy systems. The road-gang leader begins with a mounted phase and then fights through an ordinary dismounted boss graph. Vehicle, roadblock, repair, and cargo states remain deterministic and checkpoint-safe.

Mountain Guardian, River Scout, Wind Sentinel, and Fire Warden are selectable. The mission introduces no hero. Its Museum discovery documents the regional mobile-clinic network.

**Confirmed route — Five-Link Medical Corridor:** **Council Medical Dispatch Yard** confirms the compact clinic vehicle, sealed cases, selected heroes, difficulty, loadouts and manifest, then uses a short introductory Road Blocker attack without permitting pre-tutorial cargo loss. **Forest Switchbacks** uses two authored moving-defense sections separated by a secure anchor, barrier clearance, the first Cargo Snatchers and a bounded Bike Harrier pursuit. **Old Service Tunnel** creates the first repair stop around a damaged wheel assembly, protected mechanic, lighting restoration and Lookout-supported ambush; the Tire Iron receives its first authored placement there.

**Stone Viaduct and Service Turnout** contains the strongest cargo-removal attempt across guarded lanes, recovery of displaced cases and a second repair stop for the cargo-restraint frame; the Folding Road Baton appears through an emergency rack or eligible drop. **Remote Clinic Approach** gives the road-gang leader a genuine motorbike-Stability phase followed by a complete dismounted boss graph, restraint and medical assessment before canonical essential delivery. Target duration is 30–40 minutes first clear and 20–28 experienced replay, with approximately eight mandatory ordinary encounters plus the boss. The vehicle advances between authored secure anchors; compact moving-defense sections are deterministic and players never steer it. Cargo/vehicle condition, repair efficiency, recovered cases, collateral and combat mastery affect results, while the essential delivery always succeeds. The incident remains independent of Tomas, the sponsor and mandatory campaign evidence.

**Confirmed objective model — Four-Part Medical Delivery Ledger:** **Clinic Vehicle Condition** is Roadworthy when no designated vehicle-damage action completes, Damaged when one or more clearly warned attacks or hazards affect its exterior, and Stabilized when a damaged vehicle completes both repairs safely. The clinic vehicle cannot explode, be permanently destroyed, run over people, or cause mission failure. **Cargo Manifest** tracks six marked cases—two medicine, two diagnostic-equipment and two emergency-supply cases. Each case has a custody state of Secured, Displaced or Recovered and a seal condition of Intact or Scuffed. Cases cannot be consumed, opened, destroyed or permanently lost; if a Cargo Snatcher exits with one, it moves to a predetermined later recovery point, and a recovered case locks against repeat removal.

**Repair Ledger** tracks the wheel assembly and cargo-restraint frame independently as Ready for Repair, Interrupted or Completed. Interruption pauses progress and affects evaluation but never resets completed work. The protected mechanic has no ordinary Health or death state. Solo receives a protected repair window, while co-op may split repair and defense. **Route Clearance** tracks each authored obstruction as Blocked, Being Cleared or Reopened, uses no hidden timer, and always provides both a normal interaction and fixed fallback so a missing tool cannot create a softlock.

**Confirmed deterministic condition transitions:** The two authored repairs represent required road-stress maintenance and are condition-neutral by themselves; reaching Ready for Repair does not make a Roadworthy vehicle Damaged. Only a clearly warned designated vehicle-impact action changes Roadworthy to Damaged. A Damaged vehicle stays Damaged until both repairs are Completed, then becomes Stabilized. After CP3 the clinic vehicle enters the protected clinic approach, so Luo Jian and ordinary combat cannot cause post-repair vehicle damage. Every case begins Secured and Intact. A completed Snatcher removal channel makes it Displaced; interruption before escape permits recovery with the seal Intact. Completing the warned escape/rough-transfer consequence moves it to the fixed later recovery point and makes the seal Scuffed. Ordinary drops, throws, collisions, environment and rollback never Scuff it, and Scuffed never returns to Intact. One continuous case sequence counts as one resolved delivery incident for S-rank evaluation even when its linked history includes both displacement and Scuffing.

Only designated, warned objective actions may change these ledgers; ordinary contact, off-screen simulation, cosmetic effects and network correction cannot damage them. Solo permits only one cargo threat approaching completion at a time, while co-op permits at most two staggered threats against different cases. Essential supplies always arrive, but incidents, scuffed seals, vehicle damage, interrupted repairs and inefficient clearance lower score and recognition. Checkpoints preserve the exact ledger without rerolling or improving it.

**Confirmed enemy family — Coordinated Road-Gang Roster:** **Road Blockers** build and guard tagged barriers as slow melee pressure; only their clearly warned designated objective actions can worsen Route Clearance. **Bike Harriers** perform bounded motorbike passes and flanks, then safely dismount into ordinary combat when Stability reaches zero. **Cargo Snatchers** use a visible, interruptible carry channel against one marked case, carry no more than one case, drop or transfer it through legal recovery states when interrupted, never use it as a shield, and cannot retarget a Recovered-locked case. **Ridge Lookouts** provide finite ranged pressure with compact slings and blunt clay pellets or weighted markers; every shot has a visible line or ground warning. They may interrupt an unprotected repair channel but cannot damage cargo, the vehicle or mechanic from off-screen.

Extreme adds a **Tow-Chain Enforcer** who repositions only tagged loose barriers and cargo obstacles to change lane pressure. The chain cannot pull heroes, civilians, marked cases, vehicles, bikes, bosses or indispensable structures and has no off-screen action. Ultimate adds a **Road-Gang Dispatcher** whose visible whistle and hand-signal channel coordinates one existing Harrier or Snatcher action; interruption cancels the order, and it cannot create hidden spawns, reinforcements or unavoidable combinations. Only Cargo Snatchers and designated Road Blockers alter objectives. Solo permits one approaching objective action and co-op at most two staggered actions against different targets. Difficulty emphasizes coordination and timing rather than inflated Health. All gang members may surrender or enter live restraint, use ordinary road equipment, and carry no firearms, grenades, military gear or sponsor insignia. `QUIETCROWD` may reduce ordinary density but cannot remove a selected advanced role or the boss.

**Confirmed boss — Luo Jian / 罗坚, Three-Phase Roadmaster:** **Road Closure Run** begins on a mid-class motorbike with clearly signalled passing strikes, braking feints, lane changes and barrier kicks. Heroes reduce his separate Stability rather than Health; heroes who lose or leave their own bikes receive safe on-foot attack windows during slower turns and may redirect him into tagged barriers. Zero Stability produces a safe dismount. **Baton Interception** gives Luo Jian a reinforced folding road baton with complete standing, aerial, low and ground routes emphasizing guard pressure, healing interruption and positional redirection. A legal disarm or the authored Health threshold removes the baton, but the phase must complete at least one bounded decision cycle and cannot be skipped. **Cornered Leader** is a complete empty-hand phase using rough boxing, shoulder checks, low kicks, short throws, Exact Blocks and one defense-consuming Combo Breaker, without armour, rage immunity, reinforcements, infinite guard or objective immunity.

In co-op, Luo Jian may temporarily isolate one hero, but every restraint permits partner rescue and cannot form an unavoidable two-player trap. He follows all shared anti-infinite, ground-combat, defense and difficulty rules; higher difficulty adds feints, safer conversions and stronger decisions rather than excessive Health. Defeat ends in live restraint and medical assessment. Luo Jian confirms that his gang independently targeted the shipment for resale and has no Tomas or sponsor connection. The medical delivery then reaches the remote clinic canonically.

**Confirmed environment — Medical-Corridor Impact Network:** Council Medical Dispatch Yard uses empty transport crates, handcarts, folding safety barriers and loading frames for wall reactions and light breakage while sealed medical cases remain protected objective objects. Forest Switchbacks uses timber guard barriers, maintenance crates, route-marker posts and stacked drainage pipes for rebounds, knockdowns and throwable fragments without blocking the only road. Old Service Tunnel uses tool carts, collapsible maintenance fencing, empty cable reels, service cabinets and lighting-control panels; lighting restoration is an objective interaction and cannot create electrocution or unavoidable darkness damage. Stone Viaduct uses sandbag stacks, empty cable spools, removable road barriers and reinforced guardrails, which cannot open lethal falls or permit characters to be thrown from the viaduct. Remote Clinic Approach uses benches, empty supply carts, water containers and awning supports while the clinic entrance, patients, staff and delivered supplies remain protected.

Broken barrier slats, empty lightweight containers and small maintenance pieces may become temporary `J`-system projectiles, but they are environmental fragments rather than persistent weapons and disappear safely after their authored use. Every impact follows shared damage, stun, bounce, Resolve, scaling and anti-infinite rules. Combat objects and objective objects are strictly separated: cosmetic destruction or an ordinary collision with the clinic vehicle cannot alter the Medical Delivery Ledger. Only approved warned objective actions can do so. Solo and co-op use the same object schedules, and reduced-effects settings simplify debris and camera reactions without changing collision, warnings or timing.

**Confirmed resources — Medicine Road Provision Set:** Life recovery is **Ginger Chicken Congee / 姜香鸡肉粥 / Ingwer-Hühnerreisbrei**; Defense recovery is **Salted Plum Electrolyte Drink / 咸梅电解质饮料 / Elektrolytgetränk mit Salzpflaume**; Attack recovery is **Sesame-Peanut Energy Cake / 芝麻花生能量糕 / Sesam-Erdnuss-Energiekuchen**; and balanced recovery is **Clinic Route Lunchbox / 医疗路线餐盒 / Proviantbox der Medizinroute**. Medium solo places three Congees, two Electrolyte Drinks and two Energy Cakes at fixed safe locations. Co-op adds one common item in each category, and every active hero receives access to one Lunchbox before Luo Jian. Easy adds one common item per category; Hard, Extreme and Ultimate remove one, two or three common items respectively without eliminating any recovery category or the pre-boss Lunchboxes.

Provisions are separate from the six protected medical cases and appear only in personal lockers, roadside rest caches, maintenance cupboards or secured clinic-route containers. Collection, carrying, throwing, consumption and checkpoint restoration therefore cannot change the Cargo Manifest or duplicate medical supplies. All provisions use shared `J`, ownership, throw, checkpoint, anti-duplication and co-op-sharing rules. Their resource effects are an abstract gameplay convention and do not present food as real emergency medical treatment.

**Confirmed optional content — Two-Branch Community Care Model:** **Beacon Beyond the Tunnel / 隧道尽头的信标 / Signal hinter dem Tunnel** opens a short maintenance alcove after mandatory lighting restoration, where the heroes may defend the technician while an old emergency-route beacon is reactivated. The beacon is unnecessary to convoy progress, cannot be permanently destroyed, and reveals how remote communities communicated with travelling clinic teams before the modern network. **One More Clinic / 还有一间诊所 / Eine weitere Klinik** lets the heroes carry a supplementary relief parcel from the viaduct turnout along a short guarded footpath to a secure locker serving a hillside satellite clinic. The parcel is separate from the six manifest cases, cannot be opened or destroyed, and has no Medical Delivery Ledger effect; if declined, Council personnel deliver it later.

Completing both branches in one eligible run grants **Care Reaches Every Road / 医疗通达每条道路 / Hilfe erreicht jeden Weg**, adds a restored text-free emergency-beacon lens and regional clinic-route token to the Museum, and unlocks Elder commentary that peace has meaning only when safety and medicine reach communities beyond the prosperous centre. Neither branch is required for completion, S rank, essential delivery, sponsor evidence or the base Museum entry, and branch active time is compensated during grading. Cheat-Assisted play may reveal the Museum and Chronicle content but grants no achievement, rank, Credits or prestige reward. Locked entries provide localized discovery clues.

**Confirmed result model — 100-Point Medical Escort Grade:** **Medical Delivery Integrity** is 45 points: cargo custody and seal condition 20, clinic vehicle condition 10, the two repair outcomes 10, and route-clearance control 5. **Protection and Collateral Control** is 20 points covering mechanic, clinic-zone and civilian safety plus restrained environmental damage. **Combat and Nonlethal Mastery** is 25 points covering combo variety, defense, Exact Blocks, counters, interruption choices, live restraints and Luo Jian performance. **Resource and Active-Time Efficiency** is 10 points covering provision use, avoidable delays and efficient encounter completion. Candidate thresholds are C below 60, B at 60, A at 75 and S at 90.

A valid clear requires Luo Jian's live restraint, arrival of all six essential cases and canonical delivery. S represents excellence rather than perfection and permits one resolved delivery incident—one Scuffed seal, one recovered displacement, one interrupted repair, or vehicle damage repaired to Stabilized—but not several combined incidents. Repeated avoidable objective incidents, an unrepaired Damaged vehicle, or three or more Scuffed seals cap A; major avoidable protected-zone damage caps B. Fixed fallback interactions are valid and impose no automatic cap. Optional branches are evaluated separately, never required for S, and have their active time removed from efficiency comparison. Threshold details are calibrated independently for each difficulty and solo/co-op configuration. Cheat-Assisted completion is unranked and grants no score, Credits, achievement, Mastery or prestige reward.

**Confirmed persistence — Four-Anchor Convoy Snapshot:** **CP1 Switchback Secured** follows the Forest Switchbacks and first bounded pursuit, preserving initial cargo incidents, recovered-case locks, vehicle condition, cleared barriers, bike outcomes, resources and defeated enemies. **CP2 Tunnel Reopened** follows the wheel-assembly repair and final *Beacon Beyond the Tunnel* decision, preserving repair interruptions, tunnel lighting, beacon state, mechanic safety, environmental condition and obtained items. **CP3 Viaduct Cleared** follows case recovery, cargo-restraint repair and final *One More Clinic* decision, preserving all case custody/seal states, both repairs, optional parcel state, route clearance, provisions and grade conditions. **CP4 Roadmaster Dismounted** follows Luo Jian's motorbike-Stability defeat, preserving hero and boss resources, bike outcomes, carried tools, score history and the exact Baton Interception entry.

Every snapshot also records heroes/loadouts, Health/Defense/Attack resources, held weapons/provisions, environmental and enemy/restraint states, optional branches, objective incidents, active-time accounting, rank caps, difficulty, cheats, accessibility assists, Highlight candidates, deterministic seed, player configuration and local-Wi-Fi authority. Restore cannot reseal or relock a case, improve vehicle/repair condition, erase an interruption/collision/collateral event/rank cap, respawn content or rewards, reroll recovery/encounters/Highlights, repeat repairs or branches for score, or convert a failed mount outcome into success. Competitive records separate difficulty, solo, same-device co-op, local-Wi-Fi co-op, continue usage, Assisted configuration and Cheat-Assisted completion. Compatible records remain comparable; incompatible historical results remain visible as Legacy records.
