Enemy-caused or mandatory damage cannot lower grade. Cheat-Assisted completion preserves the case clear, Lin unlock, Chronicle, Museum, and media but grants no score, rank record, Credits, mastery achievement, emblem, or title.

**Confirmed achievement and reward catalogue:** Reedwater uses eight achievements: **No Escape at Reedwater / 芦水渡口：无处可逃 / Kein Entkommen in Schilfwasser** for a reward-eligible authenticated clear; the approved **The Right to Passage** and **Secure Every Line** branch achievements; **Reedwater Stands Free** for both branches in one eligible run; **Every Route Closed / 三路尽封 / Jeder Fluchtweg geschlossen** for disabling all three escape assets; **Safe Passage for All / 众人安渡 / Sichere Passage für alle** for three undisrupted civilian zones without major protected-property damage; **The Last Gate Stays Open / 最后一门不落 / Das letzte Tor bleibt offen** for interrupting Cassian's emergency gate while preserving protected deck components; and co-op **Guardians at the Crossing / 渡口双卫 / Wächter an der Überfahrt** for both heroes completing a custody interaction and tagged partner defense.

Any valid clear, including Cheat-Assisted, permanently unlocks Lin in her approved modes plus the case Chronicle, base Museum record, artwork, and music. First reward-eligible clear grants itemized Credits and the clear achievement. First A grants the text-free **Open Passage** profile emblem; first S grants **Keeper of Safe Passage / 安渡守护者 / Hüter der sicheren Passage**. Branches expand the Museum. The co-op achievement is never required for S, Museum completion, Lin, or solo rewards. Cheat-Assisted completion grants no achievements, Credits, rank, emblem, title, Mastery, or prestige reward. No reward grants combat power, weapons, moves, difficulty, or campaign progress.

**Confirmed presentation:** A 25-35-second skippable **The Toll at Dawn** opening shows Reedwater beginning work, the illegal toll line, a non-graphic refusal and blocked passage, the relay/winch/motorbike network, the Council live-capture authorization reaching Lin, and the selected heroes arriving through the following in-engine briefing. Generated shots use stable compositions and small motion; complex actions, hero selection, and state-dependent content remain in-engine. The briefing explains Cassian, the Escape-Network Ledger, civilian zones, protected scenery, custody, Lin's guest status, current bindings, difficulty, loadouts, and co-op readiness, with dialogue adapting to Lin's role.

A 30-45-second skippable **Passage Restored** coda shows documented medical custody, returned documents/property, removal of illegal toll barriers, safe public passage, resumed ferry activity, and Lin observing the restored crossing before her unlock presentation. Bounded still inserts may reflect assets, disruptions, collateral, and branches without changing canon. Generated footage contains no embedded text, dialogue, subtitles, logos, or language-dependent signage. EN, zh-Hans, and DE subtitles/transcripts cover mandatory content; both cinematics unlock in the Museum.

**Confirmed music family:** **River Without Tolls** contains **Reedwater Before the Bell** for opening/briefing; **Close the Crossing** for market/warehouse; **Lines Under Tension** for crane/winch pressure; **The Tollmaster's Last Run** for Cassian; and **Passage Restored** for coda/Museum. Plucked strings, woodblocks, restrained hand drums, low strings, river ambience, and a small ferry-bell motif distinguish the case. Bells and horns cannot imitate warnings. Motorbike, relay, winch, crane, barrier, restraint, ferry, rope, water, and custody SFX remain mixer-separated; essential cues have priority, captions, and non-audio equivalents. Generated audio requires editing, provenance, and commercial-use verification.

**Confirmed Automatic Highlight profile:** Reedwater retains universal Perfect Combo and Two Threats One Move legality. Guardian Intervention recognizes prevention of a committed evacuation disruption, relay/winch activation, motorbike escape, or Cassian emergency-gate consequence. Defensive Mastery recognizes an Exact Block or Counter against an Enforcer, Ferry Feinter, or Cassian baton action followed by a legal damage-free punish. Team Harmony recognizes one co-op hero completing evacuation or custody while the partner interrupts a designated threat, followed by zone clearance or documented custody. Environmental Ingenuity recognizes a Mooring Hook, cargo, handcart, shutter, or barrier action that interrupts an objective actor and meaningfully affects another hostile.

Candidates store the zone/asset, threat, pre/post state, responsible hero/team, prevented consequence, assistance label, and deterministic frame facts. Screenshots prefer intervention, cleared passage, or secured custody rather than distressed civilians or helpless restrained targets. Detection never rewards delay, an Active asset, repeated-warning provocation, protected-property damage, or boss extension. It remains maximum three and one per family, labels co-op/Assisted/Cheat-Assisted attribution, changes no simulation or reward, and grants no gameplay benefit.

**Confirmed persistence contract:** Reedwater uses the **Four-Gate Custody Snapshot**. CP1 follows relay resolution and lieutenant custody; CP2 follows winch resolution and lieutenant custody; CP3 follows Bike Captain custody with Cassian's motorbike still Active; CP4 follows Mounted Toll Run with the motorbike Disabled/Used and Cassian's exact carried state before Deck Counter-Control. Mission start is the fallback before CP1.

Snapshots preserve civilian evacuation/disruption/locks; all asset states and pending/consumed consequences; custody/handoffs; branches, witnesses, property, safety equipment, Museum and per-hero choices; deterministic crane/environment/protected-damage attribution/ferry/gate state; enemies and conditional reinforcements; hero resources, lives/continues, loadouts, cheats, assistance, weapons, food and ownership; score, caps, time, achievements, Highlights and anti-farm facts; and co-op authority, inputs, reconnect, rollback and technical recovery.

Restore cannot reroll hazards/windows, reactivate or change assets, repeat/erase consequences, reopen civilian zones/custody/branches, repair collateral/weapons/Cassian/barriers/bikes, respawn or duplicate supplies/rewards, repeat Museum credit, or improve CP4. Results retain the complete custody, network, civilian, collateral, branch, boss, resource, reward, Highlight, recovery, and network ledger with case/version/configuration. Comparable categories remain separated, Curated tiered replay retention applies, validated technical recovery may preserve eligibility, and ordinary continues remain recorded under shared rules.

**Confirmed player support:** First launch presents a localized **Apprehension Operations** card explaining Active/Disabled/Used assets; Occupied/Evacuating/Cleared/Disrupted zones; Resolve, restraint, custody, protected scenery, motorbike Stability, current bindings, and the absence of civilian death or permanent early escape. It remains available from Guide and pause, and each new interaction receives a safe demonstration before full pressure.

Installing the case adds **Reedwater Apprehension Drill** under Academy Resources, Equipment, and Environments. Demonstration, guided practice, independent trial, and optional mastery cover relay/winch interruption, evacuation clearance, restraint with/without a held weapon, Mooring Hook control, motorbike Stability, and emergency-gate interruption. The briefing links to it. Before permanent unlock, selected guest Lin may be used only in this drill and Reedwater, grants no Mastery/wider eligibility, and retains player-recorded combo demonstrations for her full Academy profile after clear.

Escape, civilian, custody, hazard, and collateral information uses icon, silhouette, localized text, audio, captions, target lines, and screen-reader announcements. Crane/water/bike/reflection/particle/flash/shake reductions preserve timing. Restraint and evacuation support hold, toggle, and reduced-input forms; simulation-changing help uses Assisted records. No objective requires pointer, colour, audio, or motion. Applicable hero cheats plus `QUIETCROWD`, `FULLPANTRY`, and `HOMECOMING` retain shared behavior. `QUIETCROWD` cannot remove Cassian, lieutenants, custody, or asset decisions; `TRUSTYTOOLS` affects carried weapons only; `STEADYRIDE` and `SAFEPASSAGE` are Not Applicable because players cannot ride and no protected target can suffer completion-blocking loss. No case-specific cheat is added.

**Confirmed Warrant-to-Archive acceptance matrix:** Reedwater validation covers all five heroes solo and all ten unique pairs; every difficulty; Standard Loadout, Open Loadout, Assisted, and Cheat-Assisted; solo, same-device, and local-Wi-Fi; supported browsers at 60-FPS target and 30-FPS minimum without simulation differences. It covers every asset state/combination, every civilian transition/disruption, automated grading for all 64 asset/civilian outcome sets, all branch states, every custody/handoff, enemy/specialist/conditional group, weapon lifecycle, environment, Cassian phase and gate, CP1-CP4 recovery, replay/result/Highlight/reward path, package lifecycle/version/Legacy state, guest/permanent Lin access, and EN/zh-Hans/DE input/accessibility configurations.

Release requires explicit negative proof against civilian or custody-target death, permanent Cassian escape, off-screen/unwarned objective changes, repeated consequences, route/weapon/custody/branch/checkpoint softlocks, water falls, lethal crashes, random ferry movement, asset/Cassian improvement on restore, rerolls, duplication, repeated unlock/reward, score farming, co-op-only or Lin-required solutions, and unintended sponsor continuity. Zero unresolved Critical or High-severity defects are permitted. Exact frame data, timings, final thresholds, artwork, media, performance budgets, and encounter tuning remain production validation rather than feature gaps.

### 9.4 Random Operations

**Confirmed:** Mission Mode includes a deterministic seeded **Random Operations** module with two initial objective families:

1. **Defeat:** The player selects difficulty and a unique hero; co-op players each select a different hero. All currently controllable heroes are eligible. The operation selects a compatible stage background and generates five waves from role-aware, difficulty-appropriate encounter budgets. Victory requires defeating every enemy in all five waves.
2. **Safeguard:** The operation selects a compatible arena and a person or important object to protect until an evacuation or recovery team completes extraction. Candidate targets may include an injured villager, traveler, researcher, medicine shipment, technical device, or historical material. Target-specific rules must remain readable and fair in solo and co-op.

Random selection is constrained by validated arena, spawn, navigation, hazard, objective, enemy-role, pressure-budget, and co-op compatibility tags; it never freely combines content that has not been tested together. Difficulty uses the campaign's established multidimensional profiles rather than merely increasing health and damage.

After play, an operation may be saved under its seed identifier and an optional player-provided name. Exact reconstruction stores the seed together with the generator version, content version, objective, difficulty, hero configuration, resolved arena, enemy waves, and objective profile so that later content additions do not silently change the saved mission.

**Confirmed eligibility model:** Random Operations uses a **Spoiler-Safe Validated Content Pool**. Only content from completed Story stages may enter the generator. Arenas are bounded, validated combat spaces adapted from cleared stages rather than randomly stitched sections of full maps. Every arena profile declares supported objectives, spawn and extraction points, navigation and camera boundaries, allowed hazards and breakables, solo and co-op support, enemy-size limits, and pressure limits.

All heroes unlocked for free-selection modes are eligible, including historical heroes, Kael, Soren, and Chen Yue after their approved unlock conditions. Duplicate heroes remain prohibited. Enemy families enter the pool only after their originating Story stage is complete; difficulty-specific specialists remain restricted to their approved difficulty or higher.

The initial Random Operations release excludes Story bosses, cinematic encounters, mounts, and unique scripted hazards. Any later inclusion requires a separately authored and validated operation profile. Safeguard targets and environmental details must match the selected arena's context. Newly completed stages may expand the current pool, but saved operations retain their resolved content and never change silently.

**Confirmed Defeat pacing:** Defeat operations use a deterministic **Five-Act Escalation** targeting approximately 10-15 minutes. Wave 1 introduces core roles; Wave 2 combines complementary roles; Wave 3 adds the first advanced formation or difficulty-eligible specialist; Wave 4 creates the strongest mixed positional and environmental pressure; and Wave 5 uses an elite- or captain-led command formation without inserting a Story boss.

Resources persist between waves. Completing Waves 2 and 4 opens one ownership-aware recovery cache per active hero, allowing that owner to choose Life, Defense, or Momentum recovery. Intermission countdowns may be skipped only after every active player confirms readiness. A wave ends only when all active enemies are defeated, surrender, or complete an authored withdrawal.

The full five-wave manifest is resolved from the seed before play begins. The generator cannot alter later waves to counter observed player performance. Difficulty changes validated budgets, formations, coordination, and eligible specialists without removing warnings or changing shared combat rules.

**Confirmed Safeguard structure:** Safeguard uses **Three-Phase Visible Extraction** targeting approximately 8-12 minutes. **Establish the Perimeter** introduces the target, extraction point, routes, hazards, supplies, and a protected preparation window. **Protection Pressure** sends deterministic threat groups through validated routes while a visible extraction-progress indicator replaces hidden survival timers. **Secure the Transfer** brings in the evacuation or recovery team and requires a short final corridor-clearance and handoff.

Only designated objective attackers using clearly warned actions may damage the target. Off-screen attacks, ordinary incidental collisions, unavoidable area effects, and preparation-period attacks cannot do so. Person targets use Secure, Strained, Critical, and Compromised conditions; object targets use Stable, Damaged, Critical, and Lost integrity. Compromised or Lost ends the generated operation unsuccessfully, but a person is incapacitated or receives emergency evacuation rather than dying.

One limited midpoint stabilization interaction may restore one condition band. Condition, completion time, hero damage, and resource efficiency affect rank and rewards. Objective attacks obey simultaneous-pressure caps, and target-arena combinations come only from validated context-compatible profiles.

**Confirmed co-op scaling:** Random Operations uses **Shared Budget with Split Pressure**. The seed resolves one co-op manifest before play, initially targeting approximately 35-45 percent more total threat than solo rather than double counts, Health, target damage, or duration. Additional pressure comes primarily from ordinary enemies, complementary roles, a second readable approach lane, and limited support units. Enemy move timing, damage, warnings, defense, and combat legality remain unchanged, and difficulty specialists are not automatically duplicated.

Enemy attention is distributed across both heroes instead of continuously surrounding one player. Co-op permits at most one additional major enemy action over the validated solo profile. Safeguard permits at most two simultaneous objective attackers with staggered warnings, while target thresholds and extraction duration remain identical to solo. Recovery caches remain individually owned, and co-op mechanics are advantageous without becoming mandatory.

Disconnects do not reduce the manifest or despawn enemies mid-wave. The established restricted survival proxy and recovery rules remain active until reconnection or a safe boundary. Solo and co-op records remain separate. Exact percentages and caps are balance values, but doubling enemies or objective pressure is forbidden.

**Confirmed rewards and records:** Random Operations uses an **Objective-First Operations Ledger**. Successful results prioritize objective performance, then combat performance, efficiency, and the established difficulty multiplier. Safeguard target condition outweighs clear speed. Payouts itemize completion, difficulty, rank, first clear, achievements, and bounded Hero Mastery; replays may grant bounded Credits under the shared anti-farming economy. Generated operations never award mandatory story progress, sponsor evidence, essential techniques, or exclusive required power.

Failed operations retain progress information but award no score, rank, Credits, or competitive result. Cheat-Assisted operations remain playable but award no score, rank, Credits, Mastery, achievements, or competitive records.

Every result stores seed, optional name, generator and content versions, objective, arena, difficulty, resolved manifest, hero configuration, solo or co-op, ruleset and loadout category, score, rank, time, damage, best combo, resources, objective-specific statistics, and disconnect or recovery events. Local records separate objective, difficulty, player count, hero configuration, and loadout category. Saved-seed challenges retain their own comparable history.

**Confirmed save and difficulty flow:** A generated operation may be saved from its briefing before play or from result evaluation after victory or defeat. Result evaluation provides **Save Operation**, optional naming, **Replay Now**, and, when an identical operation already exists, **Update Name** or **Save as Copy**. The save stores the seed plus the complete resolved manifest so later content additions cannot silently redefine it.

The selection sequence is `objective -> solo or co-op and profiles -> difficulty -> heroes and loadouts -> generate -> briefing -> start`. Difficulty is selected before generation because it determines eligible roles, formation and pressure budgets, resources, objective pressure, and co-op transformation. Changing difficulty from the briefing creates a distinct manifest from the same root seed.

Easy provides core roles, longer decision delays, lower simultaneous pressure, generous recovery, and less frequent objective attacks. Medium is the reference profile. Hard expands moves, coordination, elite variants, contested resources, and bounded published statistics. Extreme adds challenge specialists, group attacks, stronger defensive decisions, fewer resources, and higher objective pressure. Ultimate uses the full eligible roster, master variants, rare fair formations, and the tightest validated recovery.

Difficulty never changes player controls, hero move properties, Exact Block, Counter, cancel or Combo Breaker timing, the five-wave or three-phase structures, essential warnings, the Combat Constitution, or spoiler eligibility. Higher profiles increase rewards and retain separate records without monopolizing required power. Easy, Medium, and Hard begin available; Extreme and Ultimate use permanent global profile unlocks. A co-op host may select a tier unlocked on the host profile under the established personal-credit rules.

**Confirmed sharing and migration:** Random Operations uses **Dual-Path Operation Sharing**. A compact share code carries the root seed, objective, difficulty, solo or co-op manifest type, generator version, content-set identifier, and integrity checksum. Compatible recipients reconstruct the exact operation; an optional user name is presentation-only.

A portable `.ffp-operation` file stores the complete resolved manifest for long-term backup and sharing and uses the profile import system's validation principles. Exact replay preserves objective, difficulty, player-count profile, arena, enemies, resources, and target while allowing another eligible hero or pair; records remain separated by hero configuration. Changing player count, objective, or difficulty creates a separately identified **Regenerate Variant**.

Saved operations continue from their stored manifest after updates only when every referenced item remains compatible. The game never silently substitutes missing content. Missing requirements produce a localized list; an incompatible operation remains archived and exportable but cannot launch. **Create Updated Variant** produces a new current-version copy without overwriting the original. Security-critical or defective content may be disabled explicitly with a recorded reason.

**Confirmed anti-exploit model:** Random Operations uses **Completion-Bound Integrity** without daily limits, energy, expiring bonuses, or system-clock dependencies. Generation, rerolling, naming, importing, and sharing award nothing. First-clear bonuses apply once per objective, difficulty, and solo or co-op category rather than per seed. Rank-improvement rewards pay only the improvement over the previous category best. Successful replays retain a modest completion payout, while discovery and mastery bonuses do not refresh through new seeds. Hero Mastery is bounded by objective, difficulty, and hero.

Competitive comparison is valid only within the same seed, manifest version, difficulty, player-count profile, and loadout category. Cross-seed results are personal highlights rather than direct rankings. Active simulation time excludes pause, loading, synchronization holds, and recovery. Surrendered enemies, protected targets, allies, invulnerable transitions, repeatable objects, stalling, and post-extraction enemies cannot produce score.

Share codes and files require valid checksums and compatible content. Imported names are sanitized and length-bounded. Severe desynchronization, unverified correction, or excessive survival-proxy control may preserve valid completion while removing competitive-record eligibility; ordinary validated reconnect and host migration remain eligible.

**Confirmed release validation:** Random Operations uses a **Seed-to-Result Validation Matrix**. Every objective, difficulty, and solo or co-op profile receives at least 10,000 automated manifest generations covering arena, target, spawn, extraction, enemy, hazard, resource, navigation, budget, pressure, and catalogue eligibility. Invalid routes, visibility, overlaps, unreachable targets, incompatible roles, and impossible objectives are release blockers.

Determinism tests reproduce manifests and hashes across supported browsers, languages, quality levels, input devices, display rates, save and reload, sharing, import, rollback, reconnect, correction, migration, and archived versions. Gameplay coverage proves five-wave Defeat, three-phase Safeguard, pacing, solo and co-op budgets, representative heroes and pairs, difficulty rules, anti-infinite behavior, objective fairness, economy integrity, and freedom from traps, starvation, stalls, and score loops.

English, Simplified Chinese, German, keyboard, gamepad, touch-capable UI, screen reader, reduced effects, non-color cues, and motion-independent completion must pass. Corrupt files, missing content, storage limits, duplicates, naming, deletion, backup, legacy manifests, rewards, proxy eligibility, and anti-farming rules receive explicit coverage. Release permits no open Critical or High-severity defect in these paths. Exact numerical balance remains production validation rather than a feature-model gap.

### 9.5 Chapter Challenge

**Confirmed:** **Chapter Challenge** is a separate top-level mode rather than a Mission Mode submodule. It unlocks permanently when the player reaches the current campaign ending and is not relocked when later chapters are added. Each newly added authored campaign stage enters Chapter Challenge after that stage has been completed in Story Mode.

Players freely select an eligible authored stage, difficulty, and unique hero configuration for solo or co-op score attempts. Records preserve difficulty, hero or hero pair, mode, content version, and the established Standard Loadout or Open Loadout category. Cheat-assisted play remains available but awards no score, rank, Credits, or competitive record.

The initial ranking scope is the local profile and local co-op record book. Any future online leaderboard remains a separate service, security, moderation, versioning, and anti-cheat decision.

**Confirmed unlock and eligibility:** Chapter Challenge uses a **Permanent Campaign Gate with Story-Hero Pool**. Reaching the ending of the currently installed main campaign under Standard, Open, Assisted, or Cheat-Assisted Story rules unlocks access permanently and records the campaign version. Cheat-Assisted access creates no competitive result. Later expansion never relocks the mode; each new authored main-campaign stage becomes eligible permanently after one Story completion, including a Cheat-Assisted completion for access purposes.

Character Chronicles, Curated Missions, and Random Operations remain in Mission Mode and never enter the Chapter Challenge stage catalogue. Ordinary eligible stages permit any currently unlocked canonical Story hero, with records separated by unique hero configuration and no duplicate heroes. Mission-only characters, including Soren Beck, Chen Yue, Lin Lan, and future Curated- or Remix-only heroes, are excluded. Kael becomes eligible only after his approved canonical Story unlock.

Stage 5 is the exception: its historical Chapter Challenge uses only young Sheng Guo and Liang Zhen. The all-hero historical version remains Mission Remix, preserving the boundary between authored campaign mastery and non-canonical experimentation.

**Confirmed run model:** Chapter Challenge uses **Full-Run Ranking with Safe Practice**. A Ranked Full Chapter begins at the authored start after difficulty, unique heroes, and loadouts are confirmed. Story cinematics are skipped by default but may be viewed before timing begins. The complete route, objectives, optional branches, encounters, pickups, environment, and boss remain intact.

Authored checkpoints create deterministic recovery snapshots but are not freely selectable during a ranked run. Validated crash, reconnect, or host-migration recovery may preserve eligibility. One transactional Suspended Chapter Run may be stored at an authored checkpoint; resuming consumes the suspension record to prevent repeat reloads.

After defeat, **Retry Chapter** starts a new ranked attempt from the beginning. **Continue from Checkpoint** permits completion but converts the attempt into an unranked Continued Run with no competitive score, rank, record, or Difficulty achievement. Severe unverified recovery or excessive proxy control also converts the run to Continued instead of erasing legitimate completion.

Checkpoint Practice allows any checkpoint previously reached in Story or Chapter Challenge, free restarting, and selected legal difficulty, hero configuration, and loadout. It awards no score, rank, Credits, achievements, Mastery, or competitive record and may link to Academy, Move List, and Guide material. Retry restores exact authored state; voluntary checkpoint restart or configuration change creates a new practice session.

**Confirmed scoring:** Chapter Challenge uses a **Stage-Normalized Mastery Score**. Every stage prioritizes objective mastery, combat mastery, resource efficiency, and active simulation time while using stage-, difficulty-, and solo/co-op-specific authored values. Objective protection outweighs speed where appropriate. C, B, A, and S thresholds come from validated routes rather than one raw universal score.

S rank requires strong objective and combat performance but not mathematical perfection, mutually exclusive branches, or one specific hero, purchased move, weapon, or combo. Optional content offers meaningful score without forcing one route unless a separately declared full-completion challenge applies. Standard and Open Loadout categories remain separate.

Results itemize completion, difficulty, rank, first-rank, achievement, and improvement rewards. Replays retain bounded completion Credits; rank improvement pays only the difference between reward bands. S rank may award prestige cosmetics, Museum presentation, profile titles, or badges but never required techniques or unconditional power. Cheat-Assisted and Continued Runs have no competitive result, while Assisted records remain separate. Records separate stage and content version, difficulty, player count, hero configuration, loadout category, and ruleset.

**Confirmed record presentation:** Collection and Records contains a **Two-Layer Village Scoreboard** rather than another gameplay mode. The ranked layer displays best eligible Story, Chapter Challenge, Character Chronicle, Mission Remix, Curated Mission, and Random Operations results in separate comparable categories by stage or mission, difficulty, player count, hero configuration, loadout/ruleset category, and content version.

A **Complete Run Archive** stores every completed score-bearing run chronologically with score, rank, date, active time, content identity, seed where applicable, difficulty, mode, heroes, loadouts, objective results, combo and damage statistics, resources, condition grades, version, and eligibility. Practice, Cheat-Assisted, failed, and Continued attempts have no competitive score but may appear in a separately labelled activity history.

Old-version results remain under Legacy Records and are never silently compared with current data. Scores are never silently deleted; storage pressure prompts export or compaction of old details while preserving best records. Verified local-Wi-Fi results are stored by each persistent participant, while temporary Guest results do not persist.

**Confirmed replay retention and migration:** Chapter Challenge uses **Versioned Replay Capsules**. Every score retains compact Run Archive metadata. Full deterministic replay data stores abstract inputs, simulation seed, content and rules versions, authoritative co-op history, and integrity hashes rather than video.

The profile retains the personal-best replay for every record category, the five most recent eligible Chapter Challenge replays, and every player-pinned replay. Personal-best and pinned replay data is never silently deleted. Storage pressure may compact older unpinned replay data while preserving its score record. Replays support validated `.ffp-replay` export and import; co-op capsules include both verified input streams.

Compatible updates preserve playback. Incompatible rules or content preserve the original score and earned rank under Legacy Records but disable playback rather than simulating it inaccurately. Ranks are never recalculated against new thresholds. Suspended ranked runs resume only under compatible versions; otherwise the partial attempt is archived and **Restart under Current Version** creates a new run. Imported or modified capsules require valid checksums before playback or comparison.

**Confirmed release validation:** Chapter Challenge uses a **Chapter-to-Archive Acceptance Matrix**. Every eligible stage, canonical Story hero, representative legal pair, difficulty, player-count profile, loadout/ruleset category, Stage 5 roster boundary, expansion gate, scoring component, rank band, reward, checkpoint path, retry path, suspension, recovery, Scoreboard category, Run Archive entry, replay-retention class, export/import path, backup, migration, compaction, and legacy state receives explicit coverage.

Scores and replays must reproduce deterministic objectives, enemies, resources, boss states, time, rank, and result across supported browsers, languages, input devices, quality levels, display rates, save/resume, reconnect, rollback, and host migration. Tests reject record injection, reward duplication, checkpoint farming, rank-difference overpayment, invalid hero access, corrupted replays, and incompatible-state launch.

English, Simplified Chinese, German, keyboard, gamepad, pointer, touch-capable UI, screen reader, reduced motion, reduced effects, captions, non-color cues, and audio-independent use must pass. Reference systems target 60 FPS and minimum systems stable 30 FPS without simulation changes. Release permits no open Critical or High-severity defect. Exact thresholds remain validated stage data. The Chapter Challenge feature model is complete.

### 9.6 Backstory presentation

**Planned:** Tomas's marriage, financial betrayal, custody manipulation, and the son's upbringing are revealed gradually through several short text-free video or still-art chapters with localized narration, dialogue, subtitles, and transcripts. The sequence should cover genuine courtship, marriage and parenthood, continuing family exclusion, Tomas's turn toward fraud, the separation, the son's isolation, and the later recovery of the mother's records.

The backstory must not arrive as one uninterrupted exposition dump. Each chapter appears when the present-day investigation or the son's experience makes that part of the past relevant.

**Confirmed placement (2026-11-03):** each chapter is two or three still or small-motion shots with transcript.

| Chapter | Placement |
| --- | --- |
| Courtship, marriage and parenthood; the separation and custody; the son's isolation | Stage 4, inside *Truth under Seal* |
| The exile and Oren's teaching | Stage 5, present-day frame |
| Continuing family exclusion; Tomas's turn toward fraud and his fortune | Stage 6, after the abandoned operation |
| The recovery of Xu Lian's records | Stage 7, with the lawful notice |

### 9.7 Campaign Chronicle archive

**Confirmed:** Collection and Records -> Museum and Archive contains the localized **Campaign Chronicle / 战役纪事 / Kampagnenchronik**. Guide remains mechanical reference, and no additional top-level navigation module is created.

The Chronicle contains a progressive Campaign Timeline, completed Stage Chapters, Interludes, Character Paths, authenticated Historical Chapters, and a continuous **Complete Story** after campaign completion. Unreached content remains concealed behind spoiler-safe clues. English, Simplified Chinese, and German text is fully authored and may be selected independently from voice language.

The archive supports search, chapter navigation, bookmarks, text scaling, high contrast, keyboard and gamepad operation, and screen readers. Chapters may link to unlocked pictures, cinematics, music, transcripts, Sponsor Dossier evidence, and character records. Curated DLC appears separately under **Canon-Compatible Side Stories**; Mission Remix and Random Operations never become campaign canon. Personal choices such as Kael's decision about meeting his mother appear under **Personal Paths** without changing the main canonical narrative. Story Mode remains responsible for presenting every essential fact when it occurs.

## 10. Unknown sponsor campaign mystery

### 10.1 Confirmed evidence

**Canon:** The sponsor mystery uses an anchored structure. Mandatory evidence is reliable; uncertainty concerns its meaning, not whether it was fabricated.

- **Stage 1:** A damaged supply seal and incomplete order code prove outside support for the river-gate occupation.
- **Stage 2:** The same seal and related code on Marek's weapon supplies prove that one supply network supported both attacks. A route marker leads to a deliberately abandoned intermediary depot.

### 10.2 Mystery rules

**Canon:** Each later stage should resolve its own conflict and reveal one small mandatory fact about the sponsor at or near the ending. Optional discoveries may deepen context but cannot be required to understand the main plot.

New clues may expand or reinterpret earlier evidence but must not make previously confirmed facts false merely to produce a surprise. The Sponsor Dossier distinguishes confirmed facts from theories and never reveals the planned number of stages through empty clue slots or fixed totals.

**Planned:** Tomas and the sponsor follow a **Mutual Exploitation Through Intermediaries** relationship. Tomas never knowingly joins the sponsor's organization and does not learn its identity merely by purchasing support. Sponsor-linked brokers enable selected operations while concealing the source. Tomas believes the network serves his revenge; the sponsor uses his campaign to test defenses, disrupt research, and gather intelligence without exposing itself.

This relationship preserves Tomas's agency. His revenge, fraud, family manipulation, and bandit alliance remain his own choices rather than orders imposed by the sponsor. Late in the campaign, a deliberately abandoned operation proves that the sponsor regarded him as disposable; he first preserves that evidence as leverage and only later surrenders it as part of rejecting the revenge cycle.

**Planned historical connection:** The sponsor's modern intermediary system incorporates institutional remnants of Rovan Kest's old caravan-raider economy: routes, broker relationships, forged trade identities, storage practices, and deniable transaction layers that survived and changed hands over decades. Rovan did not foresee or command the modern organization, and blood descent does not confer guilt or leadership. Contemporary decision-makers transformed the inherited infrastructure for their own purposes.

**Confirmed organizational nature:** The sponsor is a modern **commercial-criminal consortium** composed of layered logistics brokers, asset traders, private security contractors, smugglers, data intermediaries, and shell companies. It mixes legitimate-looking public businesses with deliberately criminal intermediaries. Most ordinary employees of its public fronts do not know that the hidden network exists and do not inherit responsibility merely through employment.

The consortium supplies deniable local actors rather than openly attacking communities. The Stage 1 occupation force and Marek's Stage 2 raiders are disposable clients, not formal members. Tomas later purchases services through concealed brokers without knowing who controls the system. A small contemporary steering circle authorizes major operations, ensuring that identifiable present-day decision-makers can ultimately face accountability.

**Confirmed central motive:** The consortium practices **engineered dependency and distressed acquisition**. It creates, prolongs, or exploits crises so that otherwise independent communities must surrender assets, knowledge, operational data, transport routes, intellectual control, or long-term contracts at distressed prices. It profits when communities depend on consortium-linked transport, security, financing, water access, technical support, and emergency supplies.

The village becomes both a valuable target and a strategic threat because it combines growing water security, resilient agriculture, medical research, education, civil protection, and later advanced technology without accepting predatory control. The consortium does not necessarily seek the village's physical destruction. It seeks to break the village's independence and convert its achievements into controllable assets.

This motive connects the existing stages without changing their finalized events: Stage 1 lets the network test pressure against critical water infrastructure and observe the village's response; Stage 2 uses Marek's greed to test defenses and pursue technology and movable wealth; Tomas's later campaign provides further deniable pressure and intelligence; and future medical research creates opportunities for intellectual-property and supply-chain coercion. The steering circle describes this practice as efficient risk management, while the story identifies it as manufactured suffering for profit and influence.

The consortium uses a dual identity. Its legitimate-looking coordination front is the **Meridian Trade and Resilience Forum / 经纬贸易与韧性论坛 / Meridian-Forum für Handel und Resilienz**. The Forum publicly promotes logistics standards, emergency supply coordination, infrastructure investment, and regional resilience. Many member organizations and ordinary employees participate lawfully and know nothing about the hidden criminal layer.

The concealed steering agreement is the **Meridian Compact / 经纬协约 / Meridian-Pakt**. Selected leaders and intermediaries use it to coordinate engineered crises, distressed acquisitions, deniable force, intelligence gathering, and asset control. “Meridian” refers to routes, mapping, and the leadership's belief that it can draw lines of control across independent communities.

Neither name, abbreviation, emblem, nor forum membership appears in the locked Stage 1 or Stage 2 presentation. Later investigation may identify the Forum before proving the Compact, while the Compact itself is established only near the final reveal. Membership in the public Forum alone is never treated as proof of criminal guilt.

The Meridian Compact is governed by a **three-person contemporary steering circle** rather than one supreme mastermind or a large anonymous board:

1. The **Forum Chair and Strategy Director** publicly leads the Meridian Forum, selects dependency targets, approves long-term pressure campaigns, and protects the Compact through reputation and institutional influence. This leader remains a noncombatant and becomes the principal strategic and legal antagonist.
2. The **Compact Operations Director** controls private security contractors, weapons channels, field commanders, and deniable deployments. This leader commands the final combat force and serves as the campaign's final physical boss.
3. The **Acquisition and Intelligence Architect** controls shell companies, distressed purchases, data collection, hidden ledgers, and intermediary payments and attempts to erase or relocate decisive evidence during the final arc.

Major coercive operations require approval from at least two steering members, creating a documentary chain that can prove individual responsibility. There is no secret fourth leader, immortal founder, or unseen higher authority behind the circle. Strategy, physical coercion, and financial evidence therefore produce separate but connected forms of accountability.

The three steering members are unrelated professionals whose alliance is based on shared interests and deliberate choices rather than family inheritance:

- **Han Weiming / 韩维明**, age 60 during Stage 2, is the Forum Chair and Strategy Director. Han is the family name and Weiming the given name. Publicly, he is a respected infrastructure economist and regional logistics planner. He is calm, persuasive, noncombatant, and convinced that independent communities represent inefficient risks that should be placed under coordinated control.
- **Rhea Calder / 瑞娅·卡尔德**, age 44 during Stage 2, is the Compact Operations Director. A former private-security commander, she professionalized the consortium's deniable forces, personally excels in modern full-contact combat, commands the final force, and serves as the campaign's final physical boss.
- **Damon Kess / 达蒙·凯斯**, age 52 during Stage 2, is the Acquisition and Intelligence Architect. Publicly, he presents himself as an investment-risk and supply-chain analytics specialist. He designs shell ownership, distressed purchases, data extraction, hidden payments, and evidence relocation and avoids direct combat while attempting to destroy the Compact's records during the final arc.

None is related to Rovan, Tomas, the Xu family, or another established antagonist. Their guilt arises from their own informed participation.

#### Han Weiming's path to the Compact

In his early thirties, Han coordinates emergency transport and pooled supplies during a severe regional flood and route collapse. His centralized planning genuinely saves lives and restores deliveries faster than isolated local responses could have achieved. This success establishes his public reputation and his sincere belief in expert regional coordination.

After the emergency, several communities ask for control of their reserves, routes, and decision-making to be returned. Han interprets this not as legitimate self-determination but as irrational resistance to efficient planning. Voluntary emergency coordination gradually becomes long-term contracts presented as permanent safeguards, then financial pressure against communities that refuse them, and finally manufactured shortages and deniable threats designed to prove those communities need centralized control.

Han continues to describe his system as stability and risk management, but he knowingly authorizes artificial crises and violence. He treats people as variables inside an infrastructure system rather than participants with rights. He is therefore Sheng Guo's philosophical opposite: both experienced serious crises, but Sheng builds informed local capacity, consent, and accountability, while Han uses crisis to justify permanent control.

#### Rhea Calder's path to the Compact

Early in her career, Rhea works in emergency convoy protection and civilian evacuation. During a chaotic depot incident, poorly trained private guards panic, injure civilians, and lose control of critical supplies. Rhea responds by developing a disciplined doctrine centered on preparation, clear command, proportional escalation, capture, and controlled force. Her early reforms genuinely improve safety.

Public institutions cannot consistently fund or adopt her system. Han offers personnel, intelligence, resources, and authority, initially for legitimate emergency protection. Rhea gradually accepts “preemptive stabilization” against communities classified as risks and eventually commands deniable raids and coercive acquisitions. She argues that precise force causes less suffering than uncontrolled conflict.

Rhea does not enjoy cruelty and prohibits undisciplined violence, torture, and pointless destruction. This restraint does not absolve her: her orderly professional force makes exploitation more scalable, and she knowingly places operational success and imposed order above consent and justice. She respects the heroes' discipline but regards their defense of local autonomy as strategically irresponsible. The final confrontation therefore contrasts force serving imposed control with force protecting peace and self-determination.

Rhea's final-boss combat identity is the fictional **Vector Command System**, an adaptive security-control discipline. Its empty-hand foundation combines compact boxing, low-line kicks, elbows, clinch redirection, throws, disarms, rapid ground recovery, interception, and Counter conversions. Its aerial routes use short vaulting knees and descending strikes rather than acrobatic flight.

Rhea obeys the combat engine's core constraints: she has a Defense Bar, Exact Blocks, Counter opportunities, a defense-consuming Combo Breaker, combo scaling, anti-infinite rules, and readable recovery. A later phase gives her access to a telescoping control baton under normal durability, disarm, pickup, and throw rules. Limited protective wearable equipment can reduce selected impacts but breaks through readable mechanics and never grants unexplained invulnerability.

Commanded squad pressure may precede the personal duel, but Rhea ultimately must fight without delegating the risk. Failed counters, broken commands, depleted defense, and weapon loss create punishable windows. She receives advanced patterns and adaptive decision-making, not unavoidable transitions, permanent super armor, immunity to co-op mechanics, or rule-breaking infinite combos.

#### Damon Kess's path to the Compact

Damon begins as a gifted supply-chain auditor and risk analyst who exposes fraud, concealed ownership, and contractual abuse. The work teaches him that records do more than prove wrongdoing: in the right hands, they can create leverage. He gradually shifts from reporting vulnerabilities to selling privileged knowledge through confidential settlements, shell ownership structures, distressed acquisitions, and carefully timed disclosures.

Within the Compact, Damon designs the financial and informational architecture that converts Han's strategy and Rhea's operations into ownership and profit. He models which communities can be pressured, relocates payments and evidence, and maintains a hidden ledger of obligations, intermediaries, and beneficial owners. Unlike Han, he does not claim that control creates social stability; unlike Rhea, he has no professional doctrine of disciplined force. He treats human harm as an unpriced cost to be concealed or transferred whenever it threatens a transaction.

Damon never fully trusts his partners. He maintains a redundant contingency archive containing enough authentic evidence to protect himself through leverage if the Compact turns against him. During the final crisis, he attempts to erase the operational archive while retaining controlled copies with which to bargain. He may assist investigators only when self-preservation requires it. Any evidence he provides can reduce uncertainty or expose additional intermediaries, but it earns neither immunity nor moral redemption; his own informed participation remains independently provable.

#### Steering-circle relationships and final fracture

The three leaders maintain a functional alliance governed by structured mutual distrust rather than friendship or constant secret betrayal. Each possesses capabilities the other two need: Han supplies public legitimacy, long-term target selection, and the ideology of centralized stability; Rhea tests whether plans are operationally viable and commands the trained field forces; Damon supplies financial models, intelligence, shell ownership, and evidence control.

Their two-of-three approval rule creates shifting professional coalitions. Han and Damon commonly approve dependency schemes and distressed acquisitions. Han and Rhea authorize major operations presented internally as stabilization. Rhea and Damon may jointly reject or modify a proposal from Han that is operationally reckless, but they otherwise align only when their immediate interests overlap.

Han regards Damon as necessary but fundamentally untrustworthy. Damon treats Han as a respectable public shield and Rhea as an enforcement asset. Rhea respects competence in both men but despises Damon's willingness to classify personnel as disposable costs and distrusts Han when his abstractions ignore consequences for civilians and field teams. None of the three later defects from conscience.

Their alliance fractures during the final crisis because their established priorities become incompatible. Han seeks controlled withdrawal, public denial, and narrative containment. Damon tries to erase the operational archive while preserving private copies for a bargain. Rhea refuses to abandon personnel already committed under her command and remains to hold the line personally. This fracture creates a fair opening but does not defeat the Compact: the heroes must still protect civilians, preserve independent evidence, overcome Rhea, and secure the responsible leaders.

#### From Rovan's fragments to the Compact

No lieutenant, blood heir, or single successor inherits Rovan's organization. After his capture, unidentified transport brokers, fences, document forgers, and corrupt freight contractors continue operating separately. Over several decades, pieces of his route knowledge, shell carriers, coded accounting practices, and covert depot network change owners and become part of a wider gray-market logistics system.

Damon later identifies, purchases, coerces, or absorbs selected fragments into the Compact's acquisition and evidence-relocation architecture. Han redirects that infrastructure toward engineered dependency, while Rhea professionalizes the coercive capacity attached to it. The contemporary steering circle therefore remains responsible for knowingly transforming scattered criminal tools into a larger and more systematic organization; Rovan neither foresaw nor commanded it.

The final investigation proves organizational continuity through three independent chains rather than a matching emblem: Rovan's confiscated route documents and trial archive; freight registrations, depot transfers, and financial records demonstrating successive control of key assets; and Damon's acquisition files identifying the legacy brokers and facilities absorbed by the Compact. No one record is sufficient by itself, and ordinary employees or descendants are never treated as guilty merely because they appear in the historical chain.

#### Tomas's intermediary support chain

After Stage 2, Tomas returns to the independent commercial broker who previously helped him navigate the dubious dispute over the medicinal formula. The broker knows that Tomas possesses money, resentment, and valuable local intelligence but does not know that the Meridian Compact exists. Tomas requests equipment, transport, operational intelligence, and access to hired fighters for his revenge campaign.

The broker forwards those requirements to a legitimate-looking risk and logistics consultancy secretly controlled by Damon. Damon identifies Tomas as a valuable deniable instrument and authorizes limited assistance through a regional procurement cutout. That cutout purchases from legacy gray-market suppliers and arranges the material support without exposing the consultancy, Damon, or the Compact to Tomas, the broker, or the hired personnel.

Tomas pays most of the apparent cost. The shell consultancy secretly subsidizes selected resources so that the Compact can study the village's defenses, disrupt its research, and deepen Tomas's dependence. Tomas mistakes the unusually favorable terms for recognition of his influence and business ability rather than evidence that another party is investing in his campaign.

The chain can later be reconstructed without one convenient confession: Tomas's authenticated communications identify the original broker; the broker's invoices expose the shell consultancy; payment discrepancies reveal the concealed subsidy; and Damon's ledger proves deliberate Compact authorization. Each link can be discovered separately across the campaign while the sponsor's identity remains protected until the intended reveal.

#### Tomas realizes that he is disposable

Tomas discovers the exploitation late in the campaign, after Kael has rejected inherited revenge but before the Compact's identity is publicly revealed. He authorizes what he believes will be a decisive operation using sponsor-linked transport, intelligence, and hired personnel. Once the network has collected sufficient information about the village's defenses, the procurement cutout is ordered to recover surveillance records and specialized equipment, cancel Tomas's extraction route, abandon his remaining fighters and safehouses, seize pledged assets through the shell consultancy, and remove communications that could lead investigators upward.

Tomas initially assumes that the cutout has betrayed the larger network. By comparing the recovery order with concealed subsidies, reused route structures, and evidence from earlier attacks, he proves that the favorable assistance was always an investment in observing the village rather than recognition of him as a partner.

This knowledge does not produce immediate repentance. Tomas first preserves the evidence as personal insurance and searches for leverage. Only after the historical record is reconstructed and he confronts the damage that his revenge has caused Kael and other people does he deliberately surrender evidence he could have kept. His cooperation therefore develops from self-preservation into a genuine refusal to continue the revenge cycle without erasing his responsibility for earlier choices.

#### What Tomas learns and when

The abandoned operation proves to Tomas that a coordinated higher organization has used him, but it does not reveal the Meridian Compact's name, steering circle, or lawful public front. His preserved material exposes part of the intermediary chain rather than its ultimate leadership.

After Tomas surrenders that material, investigators authenticate it and combine it with the Sponsor Dossier, historical asset records, shell-company transactions, and independently recovered Compact evidence. The heroes and the Regional Civil Protection Council establish the organization's identity without relying on Tomas's interpretation. Tomas learns the result during a controlled debrief shortly before formal public exposure, only after the central conclusion is independently supported.

Tomas therefore cannot guide the heroes directly to the final enemy, privately confront the steering circle first, or become the sole source of the solution. His records remain a necessary contribution among several evidence chains, while his late discovery reinforces that the organization never regarded him as an informed partner.

Destroying one depot or defeating one commander cannot end the consortium. The final investigation must expose its records, financial links, leadership, coercive infrastructure, and intermediary protections. Its precise name, public fronts, motive, steering-circle members, and final combat force remain concealed until the appropriate later reveals.

### 10.3 Two-stage sponsor reveal

Late in the campaign, investigators discover that several shell companies and intermediary routes repeatedly converge on organizations participating in the legitimate Meridian Trade and Resilience Forum. This establishes the Forum as a necessary investigatory lead but not as proof of criminality. The heroes and the Regional Civil Protection Council explicitly reject treating ordinary Forum members or employees as guilty through association.

The decisive breakthrough requires three matching records: a Han strategy directive selecting the village as a dependency target; a Rhea operational authorization connecting coercive deployments to that strategy; and a Damon acquisition record connecting those deployments to shell payments and intended asset control. A concealed two-of-three approval identifier shared by the records reveals the name **Meridian Compact** and proves that it is a criminal steering agreement hidden within, but not synonymous with, the public Forum.

This evidence identifies Han, Rhea, and Damon before the final operation and provides a lawful, understandable basis for intervention. The final operation must still preserve the complete archives, protect innocent personnel, overcome Rhea's force, and secure the responsible leaders. Only afterward does the Council formally disclose the Compact while protecting uninvolved Forum participants and continuing individual investigations wherever evidence warrants them.

The reveal is therefore an earned conclusion assembled from strategy, operations, and finance rather than one confession, matching emblem, or villain speech. No production asset, dialogue line, costume emblem, Museum description, or enemy biography may disclose the Compact before the story formally completes this evidence chain.

### 10.4 Final campaign resolution

**Planned:** The sponsor remains hidden through Stage 3 and most of the later campaign. Each stage contributes one reliable fact about the intermediary system, its targets, resources, methods, or objectives without exposing the final answer prematurely.

The final campaign arc resolves the mystery. The accumulated evidence identifies the Meridian Compact's nature, central motive, operating network, and responsible steering circle. The heroes must understand what repeatedly targeted the village and bring the active campaign against it to a credible end.

This late resolution preserves Tomas as the central emotional antagonist for most of the story while preventing the Sponsor Dossier from becoming an unanswered promise. Tomas may discover only near the end that the network considered him disposable. The final outcome resolves both personal revenge and organized exploitation sufficiently for the campaign's lasting peace.

### 10.5 Accountability and institutional repair

Han, Rhea, and Damon all survive the final operation, receive any necessary medical assessment, and face separate documented proceedings based on their individual conduct. Their precise custodial terms belong to a later regional-law pass, but their canonical accountability is fixed.

- **Han Weiming** is held as the principal strategic organizer. The matched directives prove that he knowingly authorized engineered crises, coercive dependency, and deniable violence. He receives the most serious leadership judgment, loses all authority over infrastructure institutions, and cannot reduce his conduct to neutral economic planning.
- **Rhea Calder** is captured alive after the final boss battle. Her prohibition of uncontrolled cruelty and refusal to abandon personnel are acknowledged as facts but do not excuse the coercive raids she organized. She is permanently removed from security command and held accountable for the operations she authorized or directed.
- **Damon Kess** is captured while attempting to erase the operational archive. Investigators secure verified copies of both the main archive and his contingency records. His limited cooperation may locate assets and intermediaries but grants no immunity. His concealed holdings are traced for victim restitution, and he is barred from financial, fiduciary, and intelligence positions.

The Meridian Forum is placed under independent review rather than indiscriminately destroyed. Innocent employees and useful public services are protected, while compromised contracts, shell companies, and assets are separated and investigated individually. The peaceful resolution therefore rests on lawful accountability, restitution, and institutional repair rather than execution, revenge, or collective punishment.

## 11. Future village transformation

This direction belongs to the longer campaign and is not retroactively present in Stages 1 or 2.

### 11.1 Time and scale

**Approved timeline:** The transformation unfolds over approximately four years beginning after Stage 2. Stage 3 occurs about 18 months after Stage 2, the middle campaign spans roughly the following two years, and the final campaign occurs approximately four years after Stage 2 and seven years after Stage 1.

Development remains step-by-step and earned. Stage 3 shows improved contemporary clothing, research capacity, communication, roads, and early protective materials rather than the completed future village. Printed housing, photovoltaic roof tiles, the modern bridge, health-monitoring pilots, and structured transport expand during the middle campaign. Smart-home systems, civilian service robots, regulated civil-protection robots, and mature protective wearables become established only in the late campaign. Clothing, buildings, transport, public services, agriculture, robotics, and daily life evolve together.

### 11.2 Homes and energy

**Planned concept:** Modern 3D-printing methods help construct homes suited to the mountain environment. Traditional roof forms incorporate photovoltaic roof tiles that generate electricity. A distributed energy system supports homes, research facilities, greenhouses, the hospital, and civic services.

The river landscape, mountains, human scale, and recognizable village geography remain visible. Modernization does not turn the setting into a generic megacity.

### 11.3 Health-aware living

**Planned concept:** Beds automatically adjust for comfort and can monitor selected health signals, alerting the village hospital when a serious abnormality is detected. Refrigerators preserve food and can identify gathered medicinal plants and recommend safe storage conditions.

These systems support clinicians and residents; they do not diagnose every condition perfectly or replace human judgment.

### 11.4 Robots and civil protection

**Planned concept:** Civilian robots assist with agriculture, construction, maintenance, logistics, inspection, and care. The civil-protection program eventually includes robotic members or support units.

In later gameplay, heroes may summon a robot for temporary assistance. Robots remain accountable tools and partners rather than autonomous rulers or disposable weapons. Exact robot abilities, limits, ownership, personality, and combat rules remain unresolved.

### 11.5 Clothing and wearables

**Planned concept:** Clothing continues to become more modern, tailored, durable, and role-specific. Advanced materials can improve resistance to attack. Wearable devices may assist mobility, health monitoring, communication, defense, or offensive performance.

The elder adopts contemporary clothing and discreet wearable mobility assistance, allowing him to move comfortably without a cane. Technology should enhance his independence without making him superhuman.

Future Store clothing may include advanced-material combat styles and wearable equipment, but narrative progression must not imply that wealth is the only source of heroism. Gameplay balance, sidegrade rules, and accessibility remain governed by the main specification.

### 11.6 Transport and structured infrastructure

**Planned concept:** The historic stone bridge remains a pedestrian and cycling heritage crossing. A separate reinforced two-lane bridge develops beside it for electric cars, utility vehicles, and medium cargo trucks.

Roads, pedestrian routes, cycling space, greenhouse service lanes, loading areas, drainage, buried utilities, charging points, retaining structures, and public spaces become more organized over time. The system remains village-scaled, green, and resilient rather than becoming a freeway network.

## 12. Intended final direction

**Planned direction:** The campaign ends with peace rather than the permanent destruction of one side. The village survives and continues to develop. The sponsor mystery is resolved. The truth about Tomas's father, the unjust collective punishment, Tomas's adult choices, his betrayal of his wife, and his manipulation of their son becomes known.

"Everyone finds peace" does not require every character to avoid consequences. The final resolution may include lawful accountability, reconciliation, restitution, changed loyalties, or forgiveness where earned. It must not excuse kidnapping, armed raids, betrayal, or exploitation, and it must not celebrate inherited revenge.

Tomas's former wife survives, restores her voice to the historical record, and offers her son a personal meeting. He rejects Tomas's revenge campaign and parts ways with him, but may accept, decline, or defer the meeting without changing the campaign outcome. His ultimate relationship with his mother and the village remains open for later development.

After neutrality, the son establishes an independent protective identity with some of his martial-arts companions. He protects travelers and remote communities, later cooperates voluntarily with the village during the sponsor crisis, and may be playable as a guest hero without being required to join the village patrol.

Tomas survives, helps expose the sponsor's network, relinquishes revenge, and faces lawful accountability and restitution without an automatic pardon or guaranteed family reconciliation. The final physical confrontation is against the sponsor's combat force rather than Tomas.

### 12.1 Approved eight-stage campaign length

The main campaign contains eight stages. Stages 1 and 2 remain locked. The remaining six stages have the following approved narrative purposes, while their detailed routes, encounters, titles, and production requirements belong to the later campaign breakdown:

1. **Stage 1 — The Broken Water Pact:** restore the river and recover the first sponsor clue.
2. **Stage 2 — finalized greenhouse story:** defeat Marek's raid, expose Tomas, obtain the formula, and confirm the shared supply network.
3. **Stage 3 — Shadows over the Herb Mountain:** rescue the herb gatherers, confront Tomas's bandit alliance, and expose his renewed intermediary support.
4. **Stage 4 — The Weight of Inheritance:** Kael's confrontation and capture lead to authenticated evidence review, rejection of Tomas's account, and Kael's voluntary neutrality.
5. **Stage 5 — The Bell Before Dawn:** young Sheng Guo and Liang Zhen confront Oren's betrayal and Rovan's assault, revealing both the genuine crime and the injustice of collective exile.
6. **Stage 6 — The Price of Leverage:** the Compact uses Tomas's operation to study the village, abandons him, and begins seizing his assets. He learns that he was disposable but initially preserves evidence as leverage.
7. **Stage 7 — The Hidden Ledger:** Tomas relinquishes revenge, Kael cooperates voluntarily, and the heroes assemble the three-part evidence chain identifying the Compact and its leaders.
8. **Stage 8 — Fight for Peace:** the heroes protect civilians and archives, defeat Rhea's final force, secure the steering circle, begin restitution and institutional repair, and conclude the family and village arcs.

Eight stages provide space for Tomas, Kael, the historical truth, gradual modernization, and the sponsor investigation without delaying the resolution. The sponsor's identity and the historical stage's status as mandatory main-story content are resolved.

### 12.2 Final playable confrontation

Stage 8 is a multi-objective intervention at the Compact's regional coordination complex, conducted with the Regional Civil Protection Council after the three-part evidence chain establishes lawful grounds. Its four connected acts are:

1. **Protect and evacuate:** Rhea initiates a controlled lockdown. The heroes enter through the public Forum facilities, protect uninvolved employees, and open evacuation routes. Forum personnel are never treated as enemies merely because they work there.
2. **Preserve the evidence:** Damon begins deleting the operational archive and relocating contingency copies. The heroes secure physical records, backup devices, and transmission nodes while advancing through logistics and data-control areas. Damon is stopped and captured as a noncombatant rather than converted into an artificial boss.
3. **Break the coercive force:** Rhea commands disciplined squads in the operations yard and transport hub. The heroes defeat them under normal combat rules before facing her fair multi-phase Vector Command System encounter: empty-hand control, baton access, and breakable protective wearables.
4. **Secure the leadership:** Rhea is defeated and captured alive. Han's controlled withdrawal and public-denial attempt fail because the Council already holds authenticated evidence, and he is taken into custody without a token combat encounter.

In co-op, both heroes remain together rather than being forced into separate missions. Civilian protection and archive preservation affect rank, achievements, Museum records, and epilogue details, but the canonical ending always preserves sufficient evidence, captures all three leaders, and prevents civilian deaths.

The final epilogue returns to the village. It shows restitution beginning, the Forum undergoing independent repair, Tomas entering lawful proceedings, Kael retaining control over his family relationships, and the community choosing continued peace without surrendering its capacity to defend others.

## 14. Knowledge and formula continuity ledger

| Point in time | What is known |
|---|---|
| Tomas arrives | He claims the technique may improve recovery with fewer harmful effects. The claim is unverified. |
| End of Stage 2 | The formula and notes are genuine enough to justify controlled research. Provenance is incomplete. Tomas was a contributor but not sole inventor. No medicine is approved. |
| Planned post-Stage 2 research | Independent refinement produces a promising fictional preclinical effect in laboratory mice, including control of cancer-cell growth. This is not evidence of a human cure. |
| Approved Stage 3 catalyst | Continued preclinical work requires limited, responsibly gathered samples of the wholly fictional Silvervein Moonbell. Its invented MV-3 cofactor stabilizes the fictional candidate in mouse research but has no standalone curative role, human proof, actionable preparation, or gameplay power-up. The gathering expedition motivates the kidnapping plot. |
| Future unresolved state | Human trials, an approved therapy, exact efficacy, safety, ownership, credit, licensing, and access have not been decided. |

## 15. Continuity safeguards for future writing

Future stage work must observe the following rules:

1. Do not kill, free, forgive, or relocate a named captured or expelled character without explaining the transition.
2. Do not reveal the sponsor through background art, Store text, costume insignia, enemy metadata, or optional content before the mandatory story reveal.
3. Do not turn Tomas into a martial-arts boss without an explicit design decision that reconciles his established noncombatant identity.
4. Do not present the formula as a proven cure at the end of Stage 2. Stage 3 begins from promising preclinical research only.
5. Do not make Tomas the sole inventor. Preserve the cooperative origin, missing contributors, disputed control, and provenance investigation.
6. Do not excuse Tomas's adult crimes solely through his childhood. Preserve both the injustice done to him and his responsibility for later choices.
7. Do not portray the previous elder's actions as wholly correct or the father's betrayal as fabricated. The tension depends on both the real betrayal and the unjust collective punishment.
8. Do not make the Regional Civil Protection Council secretly corrupt without later evidence strong enough to justify changing its established legitimate role.
9. Do not retroactively place Stage 3 or future technology into Stages 1 or 2. Development must remain visible, gradual, and earned.
10. Do not let optional branches, hero selection, difficulty, cheats, or performance grades withhold mandatory story facts or produce incompatible campaign timelines.
11. Do not provide real-world medicinal formulas, ingredients, extraction instructions, dosing, or unsupported health claims through the fictional research plot.
12. Preserve the campaign philosophy: confront danger decisively, protect life, and pursue peace without equating mercy with the absence of accountability.
13. Do not reduce Tomas's former wife to a source of money or evidence. Preserve her genuine relationship, resistance, survival, agency, and right to offer contact without controlling her son's response.
14. Do not portray every member of the wife's family as equally prejudiced or use their unfairness to excuse Tomas's deliberate fraud, custody manipulation, and revenge.
15. Keep canonical Character Chronicle participants separate from non-canonical all-hero Mission Remix replays.
16. Treat the shared combat engine as a permanent campaign constitution. New stages, Curated Missions, heroes, weapons, mounts, environments, enemies, and bosses may add data-defined content and authored combinations, but may not fork, replace, or redesign the universal combat rules. Maintenance may correct defects, improve performance, security, tooling, compatibility, or accessibility only when validated gameplay behavior remains unchanged.
17. Do not treat the previous elder as either a blameless protector or a simple tyrant. Preserve the real emergency, the legitimate need to stop the father, and the injustice of permanent family punishment without individual review.
18. Treat the finalized Stage 1 and Stage 2 storylines as locked. Later refinements may reveal earlier off-screen history or consequences, but must not add encounters, characters, scenes, clues, or changed outcomes to those stages unless a separate critical continuity conflict is explicitly approved for correction.
19. Do not attach combat power, currency, achievements, superior ending ranks, or campaign divergence to the mother-son reunion choice. Meet Now, Reply and Meet Later, and Not Yet are equally valid personal states that converge on one canonical campaign timeline.
20. Do not equate Tomas's cooperation or abandonment of revenge with exoneration. He survives and may contribute to the truth, but still faces lawful accountability, restitution, and uncertain personal relationships; he also remains a noncombatant rather than the final physical boss.
21. Do not portray Rovan as the architect of a generations-long master plan or make his descendants inherit guilt. The modern sponsor inherits and transforms parts of an old criminal infrastructure under contemporary leadership and motives; the institutional connection is revealed late through evidence rather than an unchanged emblem.
22. Preserve the martial master's left-side injury consistently across ages and media. Mobility support may improve comfort and stability but cannot erase the injury or return him to unrestricted field combat.

## 16. Approved refinement roadmap and open decisions

The campaign storyline is completed before Stage 3 receives a detailed specification. This prevents early stage assumptions from forcing later character and mystery developments into unsuitable chapters.

### Phase 1 — Complete the campaign storyline — completed

Refine these topics in the following dependency order:

1. **Oren Rell and the historical betrayal — core event confirmed:** age, motive, Communal Supply and Trade Steward role, Seasonal Emergency and Trade Reserve, false emergency convoy, Rovan Kest and his institutional legacy, live capture, regional sentence, Qiao Lan's sacrifice and memorial, named supporting witnesses, fictional regional locations, and serious but contained harm. Remaining character-model and environmental-map details belong to later visual production.
2. **The exile and the current elder's involvement — core process confirmed:** one-month containment-to-exile chronology; rejected individual judgment and restitution plan; independent caravan matron; split-custody evidence chain; resettlement in Qingshi via the Changling Trade Road; and Tomas's later move to Yunzhou. Exact map geometry remains for the campaign-map pass.
3. **Historical Chronicle characters:** young elder, earlier Mountain Guardian and martial master, historical commander, personal names, relationships, Academy role, and visual identities.
4. **Tomas's former wife:** name, professional role, present life, return to the story, preserved evidence, meeting request, and the optional Meet Now, Reply and Meet Later, and Not Yet scenes of the Stage 8 epilogue.
5. **Tomas's son and companions:** name, age, fighting style, first appearance, companions, independent protection group, operating area, Chronicle missions, and guest-hero unlock point.
6. **The unknown sponsor:** identity, organizational nature, motive, intermediary structure, reason for exploiting Tomas, and final responsible leadership.
7. **Long-term village transformation:** placement of the later three-to-five-year development period and the narrative introduction of advanced infrastructure, clothing, wearables, robots, and civil-protection technology.
