8. **Complete campaign ending and required story length — confirmed:** major remaining plot beats, sponsor resolution, Tomas's family arc, peaceful conclusion, eight-stage length, and Stage 8's playable confrontation are approved.

Phase 1 is complete. Remaining visual identities, exact combat data, map geometry, legal term lengths, and stage-specific production details are intentionally deferred and do not constitute gaps in the campaign's core storyline.

**Current active topic:** Phase 4, Stages 4–5, and the canon-compatible Curated Mission *The Medicine Road* are approved. Stage 6, *The Price of Leverage*, is the active refinement, followed by *The Vanishing Survey*, Stage 7, and Stage 8; implementation planning remains separate.

#### Approved Stage 6 route architecture — Six-Link Infrastructure Defense Route

Stage 6 follows one continuous six-zone defense and recovery route. **Renewal District Briefing** establishes ordinary life in the transformed village before suspicious traffic and interrupted external support trigger the patrol response. **Modern Transport Bridge** places civilians and utility vehicles under coordinated attack and includes a bounded motorbike pursuit. **Solar Service Yard** makes protection of the expanding microgrid the central pressure while hosting the optional stabilization branch. **Charging and Logistics Concourse** introduces Hijack Technicians, armed retrieval units, electric utility vehicles, cargo and breakable service infrastructure. **Robot Maintenance Bay** combines technician evacuation with protection of civilian utility robots and one limited, supervised robot-support sequence that foreshadows rather than grants Stage 7's standard assistance system. **Recovery Terminal and Village Perimeter** reveals the abandonment: Tomas's promised transport and support have been withdrawn after the Compact collected the village's defensive data, while the Procurement Recovery Marshal attempts to extract surveillance equipment and records. The heroes capture the Marshal; Tomas escapes with part of the abandonment order.

The route escalates from protecting ordinary residents, through protecting interconnected infrastructure, to exposing the wider betrayal. It gives the bridge, solar power, electric vehicles, robots, firearms and motorbikes distinct readable gameplay spaces without changing the permanent combat engine. The previously approved solar-yard and maintenance-bay optional branches remain naturally embedded in Zones 3 and 5.

#### Approved Stage 6 duration and encounter budget

The first clear targets approximately **48–55 minutes** with normal exploration and unskipped story presentation; replay targets approximately **32–40 minutes** with scenes skipped. Completing both optional branches adds approximately **6–8 minutes**. The stage contains **nine mandatory encounters plus the boss**: three short transition encounters, four standard formation encounters, two major infrastructure-defense set pieces, and the multi-phase Procurement Recovery Marshal battle. The two optional branches each contain one optional encounter. One bounded motorbike pursuit lasts approximately two to three minutes, while the supervised robot-support sequence is integrated into an existing encounter rather than added as another wave. Higher difficulties change formations, coordination, objective pressure and specialists without adding repetitive mandatory waves. Co-op retains the encounter count and increases simultaneous responsibilities rather than merely increasing enemy quantity.

#### Approved Stage 6 opening — Renewal under watch

The stage opens on an ordinary morning in the transformed Renewal District. A short skippable cinematic shows civilian bridge traffic, electric utility vehicles entering the logistics district, solar-service teams, civilian utility robots assisting workers, and villagers wearing more modern clothing and early protective materials. Three connected warnings interrupt the routine: unauthorized utility vehicles approach the bridge, false maintenance credentials appear near the solar-service network, and Tomas's expected external transport support disappears from regional tracking. The player sees the third only as an unexplained communications anomaly; neither the Compact nor Tomas's abandonment is revealed.

Sheng Guo briefs the selected unique heroes in the civil-protection coordination room. The ordered priorities are **protect civilians and workers**, **keep essential infrastructure operational**, **capture attackers whenever possible**, and **never abandon people or critical systems merely to pursue retreating enemies**. The briefing contains the normal hero/loadout flow, spoiler-safe optional-branch indicators, accessibility controls and an updated infrastructure map. Solo and co-op receive identical facts. Control begins with a short interactive walk through the active Renewal District before the first bridge emergency, ensuring that the player experiences what is being defended before combat begins.

#### Approved Stage 6 route-transition contract

Stage 6 is one connected journey without a hub return or conventional inter-zone loading screen. Short twenty-to-forty-second traversal spaces carry dialogue, provisions, weapon management, visual storytelling, background streaming and checkpoint capture; cleared combat arenas never require backtracking. The first bridge emergency leads from the Renewal District to the Transport Bridge. A surviving courier then triggers the bounded pursuit toward the Solar Service Yard. Restored monitoring points to equipment theft at the Logistics Concourse; a stolen access device then triggers the Robot Maintenance Bay evacuation; protected robots identify the Recovery Terminal extraction route; and the Marshal's retreat opens directly into the Village Perimeter boss arena and abandonment reveal.

Optional branches are signposted before their exit boundary, reconnect to the main route, and never require a cleared encounter to be repeated. Crossing away from an available branch requires a clear confirmation. Branches may affect score, achievements, Museum content and presentation but never canon. Solo and co-op heroes remain together; safe regroup gates wait for both players without splitting them into separate maps. Hero resources, held equipment, ammunition, vehicles, objectives and infrastructure state persist continuously across every zone.

#### Approved Stage 6 objective and infrastructure-state contract

Objectives have three explicit levels. **Canonical mandatory objectives** require clearing evacuation routes, preventing permanent removal of essential surveillance records, reaching every critical zone, and defeating and capturing the Procurement Recovery Marshal. They cannot be lost off-screen; full-party defeat or an unrecoverable mandatory-objective failure restores the latest checkpoint. Tomas's escape with part of the abandonment order is fixed canon and is never presented as the player's fault.

The **performance-graded infrastructure ledger** independently tracks the Transport Bridge, Solar Microgrid, Charging and Logistics Network, Robot Maintenance Facility, and Recovery Terminal. Each ends **Protected** when no avoidable major disruption occurs, **Functional** after limited damage or interruption, or **Emergency Bypass** when avoidable local damage requires temporary redundant operation and later repair. Essential service never collapses permanently. A downgrade requires a visible warning and fair interruption opportunity; ordinary combat physics cannot change the ledger accidentally.

The two **optional preservation results** are **Microgrid Stabilized / Emergency Isolation Used** and **Technicians and Robots Protected / Technicians Evacuated with Robot Losses**. All people survive canonically. Missed protection may cause non-graphic injuries, delayed evacuation, equipment loss, repair needs and grading penalties, never civilian death. The result screen names every condition, downgrade cause and improvement opportunity. Conditions affect score, rank, achievements, Museum presentation and short ending details, but never Stage 7 access or the fixed capture/escape outcome.

#### Approved Stage 6 Modern Transport Bridge encounters

**Open the Crossing** is a short first encounter in which Mercenary Strikers and a Retrieval Shield hold remotely locked lanes. The heroes clear pedestrian, civilian-vehicle and emergency-service corridors; civilians move automatically after a route is safe rather than requiring individual escort. Solo threats remain sequential, while co-op permits bounded overlap without separating the heroes.

**Hold the Bridge Controls** is the infrastructure set piece. Attackers make three independently warned, visible and interruptible attempts: hijack an electric utility vehicle, overload the lane-control cabinet, and damage the local power relay. Solo permits one critical action at a time; co-op may present two only when both players have fair response time. Interrupting all three leaves the Bridge **Protected**, one success leaves it **Functional**, and two or more place it on **Emergency Bypass**. The bridge cannot collapse, become impassable or kill civilians, and ordinary attacks cannot alter its ledger. Lane dividers, cargo restraints, crates, vehicle doors and roadside fixtures remain valid tactical impact objects and throwable debris without affecting that result.

After the defense, a designated courier canonically escapes beyond the immediate protection perimeter on a motorbike with a stolen access device. The fixed escape neither blames the player nor downgrades the bridge. Civil-protection motorbikes lead directly into the Solar Service Yard pursuit.

#### Approved Stage 6 motorbike pursuit and dismount contract

The pursuit lasts approximately two to three minutes along an evacuated service road and uses the established mount controls and combat engine rather than a racing subsystem. The courier always reaches the Solar Service Yard. Performance instead determines how many mounted escorts are stopped and whether the civil-protection bikes survive. Stopped escorts are absent from the Yard; escaped escorts replace part of its opening formation rather than adding a wave. Pressure on the courier can deny a prepared Yard advantage but cannot defeat him or alter the route.

Courier pressure uses three authored gates at the bridge exit, service-road bend and Yard approach. Each grants at most one shared event through the first valid legal mounted hit on the guarded courier, interruption of that gate's designated escort screen, or on-foot fallback service control forcing an evasive route. Repetition, co-op duplication and delay cannot add events. The exact state stores a zero-to-three count, gate IDs, attribution and whether preparation was denied. Two or three deny the jammer; zero or one permits it. Pressure remains independent of escorts, bike condition, fixed arrival and achievement eligibility and commits at Solar Departure.

Each hero receives one persistent bike with independent Stability. A surviving bike is parked at the Solar Yard and becomes available again at the outdoor Logistics Concourse. Zero Stability causes a controlled dismount and permanently disables that bike for the run; no replacement or magical repair occurs. A parallel on-foot service walkway gives a dismounted hero a short interception and rejoins the same Yard state. In co-op, the mounted partner may continue, but both regroup before the next mandatory encounter and neither route adds a full wave. The evacuated road uses early-readable obstacles, non-instant enemy collisions, low-Stability audiovisual/controller warnings, bounded mounted pressure, assisted steering and reduced-camera-motion options. Losing both bikes never blocks later completion.

#### Approved Stage 6 Solar Service Yard and microgrid branch

**False Maintenance** is the Yard's mandatory formation encounter. The courier joins attackers carrying false credentials, while hostile silhouettes remain unambiguous beside real technicians. Three warned sabotage actions target an inverter-control bank, a battery-isolation switch and the load-routing terminal. Zero, one, or two-or-more successful actions leave the Solar Microgrid **Protected**, **Functional**, or on **Emergency Bypass** respectively. Automatic isolation always preserves critical village services, and ordinary attacks cannot affect the ledger. Successful pursuit pressure denies the courier preparation; otherwise he deploys a destructible portable jammer that suppresses only objective markers, never environmental, audio, shape, color or accessibility cues. The courier is captured, the bridge device recovered, and its log reveals a second intrusion already active at the Logistics Concourse.

The optional **Keep the Current** branch uses three deterministic steps: isolate a compromised circuit, route essential services through a safe inverter group, and confirm balanced load while defeating one interruption team. Solo serializes the steps; co-op may divide them within one connected arena. Multimodal cues never rely on color alone. Success records **Microgrid Stabilized**; skip or failure records **Emergency Isolation Used**. Stabilization prevents cascading risk but never repairs or upgrades the physical ledger. Technicians remain protected, while insulating barriers, empty reels, tool cabinets and pallets support combat without turning live electrical equipment, batteries or arrays into explosive weapons.

#### Approved Stage 6 Charging and Logistics Concourse

Outdoor **Break the Hijack** centers on portable override rigs at the charging hub, dispatch console and automated cargo lane. A Hijack Technician must stay connected within a marked control area to complete an override; players may interrupt the technician, disable the rig or force hostile control away. Zero, one, or two-or-more completed overrides leave the Logistics Network **Protected**, **Functional**, or on **Emergency Bypass**. Overrides cause temporary routing failure, never permanent destruction, and ordinary combat cannot alter the ledger. Surviving civil-protection motorbikes remain available in the forecourt while equivalent footpaths and service shortcuts reach every objective; bikes are parked permanently before the loading hall. Slow electric utility carts may be repositioned through normal interaction to open lanes or create cover, stop automatically near people and infrastructure, and are never lethal ramming weapons.

Indoor **Secure the Freight Spine** combines Retrieval Shields, finite-ammunition Gunners and Strikers contesting the dispatch platform. Players break protection, deny two temporary firing positions, and preserve marked medical/civil-protection cargo for grading. Cargo straps, empty pallets, movable crates and cart doors support environmental combat; charging batteries and live power equipment cannot explode. After security is restored, records show that a cloned credential issued a remote Robot Maintenance Bay evacuation command before the Concourse fight began, producing the next emergency without another fixed escape or player blame.

The marked cargo is exactly two groups: **Sealed Medical-Supply Pallet** and **Civil-Protection Equipment Cases**, each starting Secured and able to become Damaged once. Only establishment of its linked firing position followed by a finite Gunner's visible target-labelled **Cargo Breach** channel and one completed shot may downgrade it. Lost, Destroyed and contaminated states do not exist; all ordinary bullets, grenades, throws, physics, player attacks, carts and debris are condition-neutral. Solo permits one Breach channel and co-op two only on separate reachable lanes. Damaged cargo remains sealed for inspection and affects Discipline/commentary/Archive only. Exact position, channel, cause, interruption and once-only attribution persist.

#### Approved Stage 6 Robot Maintenance Bay and preservation branch

Mandatory **Clear the Evacuation Line** covers the calibration floor, charging aisle and emergency service gate. Technicians automatically advance after each connected area is safe and never require individual escort. Sustained hostile control after an explicit multimodal warning forces an emergency reroute; zero, one, or two-or-more reroutes leave the Maintenance Facility **Protected**, **Functional**, or on **Emergency Bypass**. Ordinary presence, stray attacks and incidental environmental damage cannot change that ledger.

Optional **Every Helping Hand** releases an inspection crawler, compact lifting robot and service carrier through normal interaction and protects their short autonomous paths to storage. Solo serializes releases; co-op permits two fairly spaced routes. Robots cannot be carried, shield the heroes or fight. Saving all three records **Technicians and Robots Protected**; skip or any unit loss records **Technicians Evacuated with Robot Losses**. Damage remains mechanical and non-graphic. Full preservation opens a history alcove containing a component from the village's first already-retired service robot, never a part taken from a newly damaged unit.

Mandatory **Hold the Service Gate** defends one connected rear-gate arena against Shields, Strikers and a Hijack Technician trying to reopen access. A rugged civil-protection support robot is released under technician supervision regardless of branch participation. After security, the robot and technicians trace the false command to the Recovery Terminal extraction route.

#### Approved Stage 6 supervised robot-support contract

The support robot appears only during **Hold the Service Gate**, remains under technician supervision, and never becomes a selectable or generally summonable hero. Marked support stations use the existing contextual interaction to request three single-use interventions in any order: **Deploy Cover** positions a temporary ranged-fire barrier that cannot crush or trap; **Restrict a Reinforcement Lane** visibly redirects and delays one group without deleting it; and **Support the Gate Lock** delivers emergency power that slows, but never completes, the Hijack Technician objective. Only one intervention is active at once, with technician confirmation and a visible route preview.

A telegraphed pre-activation disruption makes the robot safely retract and places the selected intervention on a fixed cooldown without consuming it. One retry is allowed; a second pre-activation disruption consumes that intervention without effect. Once cover is deployed, a barricade positioned, or emergency power connected, the intervention is consumed even if enemies later end its effect. Thus single-use means one successful activation with at most one failed approach/retry, preventing permanent first-failure loss and infinite stalling. Checkpoints store unused, approaching, disrupted, cooldown, retry-available, active and consumed states; retries add no score. The robot deals no damage, cannot affect combos, revive, collect, capture or complete objectives, and is independent of the optional preservation branch. Solo activation is quick; co-op shares one authoritative command state. Routes, timings, cooldowns and retry state restore deterministically. Difficulty may increase surrounding pressure but never weakens the support or hides its multimodal cues. This foreshadows Stage 7 assistance without changing the permanent combat engine.

#### Approved Stage 6 weapon and ammunition contract

All firearms and grenades are temporary stage pickups that cannot be pre-equipped, permanently purchased, carried between stages or replenished from containers. The fictional **revolver** has six visible precise shots, slow anti-loop recovery, a close directional grip strike, one committed downward aerial shot, no reload, and an empty throw. The fictional **compact automatic** uses one finite magazine, controlled bursts, recoil/recovery and directional angle or reposition variants without extra ammunition; it has no reload and may be thrown empty. Enemy versions obey the same finite-magazine rule.

A hero may carry at most one single-use fictional **concussion grenade**. Its landing area has audiovisual/vibration warning; it causes knockback, short stun and Defense damage without fragmentation, gore, permanent injury, friendly fire, civilian harm or objective/evidence damage. Boss control is reduced and retains Combo Breaker protection. One **restricted heavy machine gun** appears in the Recovery Terminal with one fixed supply, severe movement reduction, no jump/grapple/mount/item carry, controlled heat-limited bursts, anti-infinite protection and no effect on ledgers or indispensable objects. It discards automatically when empty and cannot enter the boss.

Ammo persists exactly through zones and checkpoints, with no random refills, reloads or duplication. Co-op pickup ownership is explicit and deliberate dropping permits transfer. Enemy fire always has readable aim preparation, directional warning and on-screen/edge cues; off-screen unreadable shots are forbidden, while empty states create punish windows. Player ammunition is not reduced by difficulty. Fictional designs use no real brands. Exact damage, recoil, frames and placements remain tuning data.

#### Approved Stage 6 vehicle and environmental-damage contract

Electric utility carts use normal interaction on marked low-speed lanes to open routes, create cover or move cargo. Automatic braking prevents collisions with people, robots, critical equipment and boundaries; carts cannot ram enemies. Only authored warned actions permit hostile hijacking. A damaged cart becomes stationary cover without explosion, trapping or sole-route blockage; its condition persists and cannot be repaired during the run.

Tagged lane dividers, lightweight barriers, empty maintenance crates/tool cabinets, pallets, restraint frames, service partitions, removable cart doors, empty cable reels, non-powered robot racks and already-secured terminal housings use the established **Intact → Damaged → Broken** schema. Approved breakage may create one marked lightweight throwable, never glass, live batteries, sharp electrical parts or hazardous chemicals. Tagged impacts add damage, stun, bounce or knockdown under collision cooldowns, diminishing control, elite resistance and Combo Breaker rules.

Bridge structure, solar arrays, live batteries/charging equipment, active robots, evidence storage, occupied vehicles and essential terminals are immune to ordinary attacks, firearms, grenades, throws and physics. Only defined warned objective actions alter the infrastructure ledger. Avoidable damage to nonessential property is separately recorded as **Collateral Care** for score/commentary, never canon; required combat, rescue, accessibility and route breakage is exempt. All states restore deterministically, while reduced flashes, shake, debris and effects preserve simulation.

#### Approved Stage 6 enemy and difficulty roster

The core roster is the **Mercenary Striker**, a readable close-range pressure fighter with punishable sweeps/throws and one eligible Defense-consuming Combo Breaker; the **Retrieval Shield**, whose finite non-regenerating Guard protects formations and is countered by Exact Blocks, throws, rear attacks, impacts and co-op flanks; the **Hijack Technician**, whose tagged-device overrides require visible local connection and warned channels and who cannot hack remotely, through walls, while down or off-screen; and the **Finite-Ammunition Gunner**, who uses warned finite fire, repositions, exposes an empty-state punish and cannot generate ammunition.

Extreme and Ultimate add the **Recovery Specialist**, who may reclaim one dropped firearm with only its remaining ammunition and make one long, interruptible relocation/restart of one disabled portable rig per encounter, but can never replenish, revive, touch mandatory evidence/civilians or repeat that restart. Ultimate additionally adds the **Systems Breacher**, whose visible interruptible connection may temporarily alter one tagged noncritical shutter, barrier or unused support station. A breach may change cover, redirect a lane or delay one robot intervention, but cannot damage ledger infrastructure, trap a hero, block the sole route, erase support, affect evidence or alter canon; defeat releases control.

Easy separates roles and lengthens warnings; Medium uses standard formations; Hard combines complementary core roles; Extreme adds selected Recovery Specialists; Ultimate retains them and adds selected later Systems Breachers. Higher difficulty changes coordination, positioning, objective timing, defense and punish discipline—not wave count, cue readability, infinite ammunition or primarily inflated Health. All roles retain anti-infinite, fair recovery and shared combat-resource rules.

#### Approved Stage 6 deterministic formation and anti-frustration contract

Authored formation tables replace randomness and hidden adjustment. Active-enemy caps for solo/co-op are **4/6 Easy**, **5/7 Medium**, **6/8 Hard**, **6/8 Extreme**, and **6/8 Ultimate**; reserve actors may reposition visibly but cannot attack, fire, channel or begin attacks. Approved patterns are Escort Wedge, Split Pressure, Objective Relay, Retreat Screen, Recovery Team and Systems Disruption.

Solo permits at most one active objective channel, one ranged aim and one advanced-specialist action; co-op permits at most two channels or aims on readable separate lanes. Two ranged enemies cannot form unavoidable crossfire on one hero. Ultimate specialists cannot channel against the same device or begin critical actions simultaneously. At most two melee enemies pressure one solo hero, or three pressure one co-op hero while ignoring the partner. Reinforcements use visible authored entrances and audiovisual warning, never spawn behind a hero/in an active combo/by an interaction, and cannot attack immediately. Critical warnings remain separated; objective timers pause for forced presentation/recovery, and bounded co-op extensions prevent a later channel becoming impossible after a down.

Shared wake-up protection, edge warning/directional audio, accessible shield counters, Defense-consuming Combo Breakers and remaining-ammo recovery prevent dogpiles and loops. Checkpoint restoration reproduces formation order, equipment, ammo and timings exactly. Higher difficulty produces tactical density without uncontrolled crowds, hidden reactions or performance-punishing reinforcements.

#### Approved Stage 6 Recovery Terminal encounters

Mandatory encounter eight, **Break the Extraction Line**, places Shields, finite Gunners and Strikers around three surveillance-equipment loads on the exterior freight apron. A load reaching the vehicle remains recoverable after the arena is cleared and never disappears off-screen; preventing all loading improves grading without gating canon. Extreme/Ultimate may add the approved Recovery Specialist. The one restricted heavy machine gun is an optional anti-formation tool, never required. Before the protected interior corridor, any remaining gun must be placed in a visible secured disposal clamp after an explicit confirmation showing surrendered ammunition; it cannot enter the interior or boss.

Each load follows Secured on Apron, Carried, Dropped Unsecured, Secured in Extraction Vehicle and Recovered after Combat. A designated carrier requires a visible pickup channel and authored path; cannot throw, damage, duplicate or hand off; and drops at a fixed nearby marker when interrupted. Another eligible carrier may resume while hostiles remain, but players never carry the load. The extraction vehicle cannot depart, explode or be driven. On final-hostile defeat it is automatically secured and every non-apron load becomes Recovered after Combat through a short custody confirmation, with no extra interaction/wave. Apron security grants full credit; post-combat recovery records one response incident. All three are recovered before interior entry, and complete route histories persist.

Mandatory encounter nine, **Preserve the Record**, warns three local operations: disconnect a defensive-sensor archive module, export local route telemetry, and sever the authenticated recovery-log connection. Zero, one, or two-or-more completed operations leave the Recovery Terminal **Protected**, **Functional**, or on **Emergency Bypass**. Immutable backups preserve the mandatory order, evidence trail and story progression; success affects local equipment, redundant copies, grading and repairs, never indispensable evidence. Ultimate permits one Systems Breacher under the existing no-shared-target restriction. After the formation falls, the Procurement Recovery Marshal seals the perimeter and enters alone. No ordinary reinforcements join the boss, and all earned equipment/terminal conditions remain exact.

#### Approved Stage 6 Procurement Recovery Marshal boss

The Marshal's three-phase **Field Extraction System** uses the unchanged standing, aerial, ground, weapon, Defense, Exact Block, counter and Combo Breaker systems. **Control the Perimeter** combines a finite-Guard retrieval shield and control baton with standing strings, joint-control throws, warned low sweep, committed aerial strike and escapable ground restraint. It ends only after both the authored Resolve threshold and shield break, ensuring the shield lesson without indefinite delay. **Reclaim the Assets** abandons the shield for faster baton, throws, aerial interception and two warned finite-weapon recovery opportunities; recovered equipment retains exact ammo/durability, a warned disarm counter only drops rather than deletes hero weapons, interruption is punishable, and one standard fictional concussion grenade is allowed. The heavy gun and replacement weapons are forbidden.

**Cut the Line** begins when a withdrawal order confirms revoked support. Firearms are discarded for empty-hand control, compact baton, low/aerial/grounded attacks and counter throws. One warned extraction attempt at low Resolve either yields a large interrupt punish or merely repositions the Marshal behind a short visible defense; escape and Resolve restoration are impossible. One visible Defense Bar governs breakers without hidden armor/refills. Shared anti-infinite, wake-up, throw-rescue and co-op targeting rules apply; difficulty adds legal feints/choices, never hidden attacks, extra life bars or phases. Terminal condition changes presentation/cover only, not boss difficulty. At zero Resolve, an in-engine non-QTE disarm captures the living Marshal.

#### Approved Stage 6 abandonment revelation and evidence split

During the Marshal's final phase, Tomas monitors from a separate temporary command vehicle and intercepts a recovery packet intended for the Marshal. It proves that withdrawal was authorized before his decisive phase, his accounts/contracts/equipment/intermediary network are already being seized, and retrieval forces were ordered to take surveillance material and abandon his remaining personnel after enough village-defense data was gathered. Tomas turns against the network but neither repents nor helps the village.

The heroes recover the authenticated recovery-order header, procurement-cutout identifiers, concealed subsidies, asset-seizure notices and extraction instructions. These prove a larger organization financed, studied and discarded Tomas; a recurring authorization marker points toward a shell consultancy without yet exposing the Compact or its leaders. Tomas secretly retains the authorization annex, deliberate-abandonment timestamp and partial internal routing record later attributable to Damon's consultancy. This is genuine leverage but insufficient alone for exposure or prosecution.

While heroes lawfully secure the living Marshal, people and records, Tomas uses a prearranged off-arena service route in his own vehicle. He takes only his portable copy, never village/player-protected evidence. The fixed escape is independent of rank, conditions, branches and delay, and the heroes' protection priority is affirmed rather than blamed. Tomas keeps the annex as protection against both sides, remaining vengeful and calculating while severed from sponsor loyalty. The Marshal confirms the order/cutout but knows neither the Compact's name nor steering circle, preserving Stage 7's investigation.

#### Approved Stage 6 solo/co-op equivalence contract

Solo selects one of the four village heroes; co-op selects two unique heroes, with duplicate selection prohibited. Every hero can operate every objective, vehicle and support station, so traits create preferences without mandatory identities. Solo serializes critical channels and interaction steps with travel-aware timers; co-op may divide at most two reachable responsibilities without duplicating completion or rewards. The approved active caps and shared anti-infinite systems apply. Co-op adds formation coverage and coordination rather than doubled Health or extra waves, distributes pressure fairly, and keeps friendly fire/collision damage disabled.

Existing revive rules apply. A later simultaneous channel receives the approved bounded extension after a down, progress does not reset for one downed hero, enemies cannot camp a down/revival without response, and full-party defeat uses the standard checkpoint path. One connected map, soft camera tether, partner indicator and safe regroup gates prevent forced separation. Bikes, weapons, ammo, food and hero combat resources are individual; infrastructure, robot commands, branches, evidence and score events are shared authoritative state. Solo/co-op leaderboards remain comparable but separate and player-count normalized. No rank, achievement, Museum record, canon or unlock requires co-op. Hot-seat and local-Wi-Fi share deterministic host-authoritative objective rules.

#### Approved Stage 6 checkpoint and restoration contract

Seven authored anchors are **Bridge Response** (post-briefing), **Pursuit Launch** (committed bridge), **Solar Departure** (Yard/branch committed), **Logistics Secured**, **Maintenance Secured**, **Recovery Interior Entry** (exterior and heavy-gun surrender committed), and **Marshal Confrontation** (terminal committed, pre-boss). No boss-phase checkpoint exists; defeat restores exact pre-boss resources and restarts Phase 1.

Each anchor stores hero identity/resources/status/loadout/move assignments; all weapon/ammo/dropped ownership/food; bike Stability/loss; five infrastructure ledgers and causes; branches/robots; utility vehicles/breakables/cargo/Collateral Care; enemies/formations/specialist permissions/timers; support interventions/cooldowns; evidence/equipment/Marshal; score, difficulty, player count and integrity. Restore never repairs, refills, rerolls, duplicates, reverses a branch or improves a result.

Validated technical recovery and one consumed-on-resume transactional **Suspend Stage** preserve eligibility. Defeat or voluntary **Continue from Checkpoint** preserves story and Stage 7 access but marks **Continued**, removing competitive score, rank, Credits, achievements, titles and records; **Restart Stage** is fresh. A disconnected client enters nonparticipating protected standby. Co-op eligibility persists only during standby and validated same-player reconnect before any continued play. Confirming solo continuation marks **Party Changed**: future formations/timers/concurrency switch to solo at the next safe boundary, committed state remains, and the absent hero/inventory withdraw without transfer, duplication or conversion. Completion retains canon, Stage 7, difficulties, mandatory records and catalogue visibility but no score, rank, Credits, achievements, emblem, title, personal best or leaderboard. A later anchor rejoin restores exact personal state but not eligibility. Archive records the initial pair, disconnect, confirmation, rejoin, time by player count and Party Changed class in noncompetitive history, comparing with neither solo nor co-op. Migration/reconnect before continuation or a paused interruption with no continued play preserves eligibility. Pursuit and boss restart only from their named anchors; no state can be rerolled.

#### Approved Stage 6 score and rank model

An eligible run has a **100,000-point core** plus up to **5,000 optional bonus**: Combat Mastery 35,000; five infrastructure assets 30,000; Emergency Response 15,000; Tactical Discipline 10,000; and Hero/Team Resilience 10,000. Each asset grants 6,000 Protected, 4,000 Functional or 2,000 Emergency Bypass. Combat rewards varied character-specific standing/aerial/ground/freestyle routes, defense, counters, throws, weapon and co-op mastery under repetition decay and anti-farming. Response scores each warned/corridor/equipment action once. Discipline covers finite tools, marked cargo and Collateral Care with required destruction exempt. Resilience covers resource judgment, downs and fair co-op revival/contribution without score stealing.

**Keep the Current** and **Every Helping Hand** grant up to 2,500 each; skipping is never penalized and S remains possible without either. Thresholds are **S 90,000**, **A 78,000**, **B 65,000**, **C 50,000**, and **D below 50,000**. Clear time is only a leaderboard tiebreaker, with menus/presentation/accessibility not punished. Boards separate difficulty, solo/co-op, hero/pair and integrity class, and normalize team scoring. Cheat-Assisted/Continued runs show diagnostics but submit no score/rank/economy/prestige record. The result screen attributes every score, downgrade and branch outcome once without double penalty.

#### Approved Stage 6 achievements, rewards and unlocks

Eight eligible achievements are: clear **The Price of Leverage / 筹码的代价 / Der Preis des Druckmittels**; Protected bridge **Every Lane Open / 全路畅通 / Alle Fahrspuren frei**; branch clears **Keep the Current / 电流不息 / Der Strom bleibt** and **Every Helping Hand / 众力皆护 / Jede helfende Hand**; both branches **Systems in Harmony / 系统协同 / Systeme im Einklang**; all five assets Protected **Renewal Defended / 守护新生 / Erneuerung verteidigt**; every escort stopped and every assigned solo/co-op bike preserved **Close Pursuit / 紧追不舍 / Dicht auf den Fersen**; and interrupting the final extraction then capturing the Marshal after Exact Block/Counter **Leverage Reversed / 反制筹码 / Das Druckmittel gewendet**. None requires co-op.

Leverage Reversed requires the Marshal's designated extraction-setup attack to be met by either Exact Block or legal Counter, arming a Reversal Chain. The team must interrupt the extraction before the Marshal returns neutral or within four seconds, whichever is first, then complete live capture in the same eligible attempt. Both defenses are not required; capture need not be immediate. In co-op one hero may arm and the partner interrupt, with both attributed. Plain interruption, expired chain, restored pre-boss state, Cheat-Assisted, Continued or Party Changed cannot award it.

Any canonical clear, including Continued/Cheat-Assisted, unlocks Stage 7, *The Vanishing Survey*, Stage 6 Chapter Challenge, mandatory Chronicle/Museum entries and difficulty progression (Hard→Extreme, Extreme→Ultimate) without score/rank gates. First eligible clear grants balance-defined Credits and permanent Store visibility for **Infrastructure Emergency-Response Outfit** and **Village Renewal Festival Attire** for every playable hero unlocked through Stage 6, including noncanonical Mission heroes. The four present village heroes receive their canonical Stage 6 default evolution free. First eligible A grants a text-free bridge-and-sun emblem; first eligible S grants **Guardian of the Renewal / 新生守护者 / Hüter der Erneuerung**. Higher eligible difficulties add Chronicle marks, not power. Continued/Cheat-Assisted retains access/catalogue/mandatory records but no Credits, achievements, rank rewards, emblem, title or leaderboard. Locked entries expose localized spoiler-safe multimodal clues.

#### Approved Stage 6 Museum and Campaign Chronicle structure

Canonical clear unlocks galleries for the Renewal District, bridge, microgrid, logistics/charging, civilian robots, heroes/enemies/boss, weapons/vehicles/environment, costumes/food, production art, music/SFX/ambience, cinematics/briefings/endings and the Marshal's equipment. Fictional weapons receive safety/context notes and no exhibit grants power. The **Abandoned Instrument** evidence exhibit uses controlled localized UI—not generated text in art—for the recovery header, cutout IDs, subsidy trail, seizure notices, extraction instructions and testimony.

The source matrix marks pre-authorized withdrawal, asset seizure and surveillance recovery **Confirmed**; larger-organization financing/use of Tomas **Corroborated**; and sponsor identity, consultancy ownership, leadership, routing chain and Tomas's retained contents **Unresolved**. The Chronicle may state the player witnessed Tomas take a fragment while explicitly noting that the patrol cannot authenticate its contents. Optional **Power in Balance** follows the microgrid branch with a non-instructional illustrated flow presentation. **Every Helping Hand** adds the already-retired robot component, history art and technician commentary. Both add **Renewal through Cooperation**, combining people, infrastructure and utility support. Replay recovers missed optional content.

The Museum cannot name the Compact/leaders before Stage 7, treats Damon's link as unresolved, never inflates the Marshal into leadership, and cannot reveal or collect Tomas's annex. No optional contains mandatory clues. Locked content has localized spoiler-safe multimodal hints; Cheat-Assisted clears receive mandatory story exhibits/clues without prestige records.

#### Approved Stage 6 provisions, placement and costumes

**Greenhouse Vegetable Wrap** restores Health, **Aquaponic Fish Broth** restores Defense, **Fruit-Energy Gel** restores Attack, and the once-per-run **Balanced Village Meal** moderately restores all three. The herb, formula and experimental medicine are never power-ups. Deterministic placement is: Wrap at Bridge; Broth plus a Fruit-Energy Gel awarded only after successful Keep the Current at Solar; one Gel plus an always-present post-hall Wrap at Logistics; Wrap and Broth in Maintenance staff storage; Gel on the Terminal approach; and the balanced meal in the pre-boss service room. The mandatory baseline is three Wraps, two Broths, two Gels and one meal; the branch adds the third Gel. Solo receives one item; co-op uses two smaller same-type portions with equivalent combined value and exact ownership/consumption. Difficulty may tune values but never identity. The branch Gel is excluded from `FULLPANTRY`; the ordinary post-hall Wrap follows eligible-container behavior. Existing `J` interactions and exact consumption persist.

Marked staff cabinets, coolers, meal boxes and baskets contain food; hostile cases/hands/racks contain weapons; evidence/Museum/objective silhouettes remain distinct. Containers never randomize categories. Revolvers appear through authored Gunners from the Concourse, automatic weapons in loading-hall/Terminal formations, concussion grenades only in selected Logistics/Terminal encounters, and the single heavy gun at the Terminal exterior; safe debris supplies supplemental temporary pickups. Nothing respawns or rerolls.

The four village heroes receive free Stage 6 default evolution with fitted early protective materials, improved boots/gloves/joint protection/communications/visibility, individual silhouettes and nonmilitary civil-protection identity. Purchasable visual-only **Infrastructure Emergency-Response Outfit** uses character-specific modern rescue/workwear and restrained visibility marks; **Village Renewal Festival Attire** uses contemporary tailored cultural designs with combat-safe garments. Both are distinct rather than palette swaps, permanently purchasable after visibility unlock, and usable wherever costume selection is permitted for every hero unlocked through Stage 6; canonical first play retains story defaults.

Approved recovery-item localization is **Greenhouse Vegetable Wrap / 温室蔬菜卷 / Gewächshaus-Gemüsewrap** with “Restores Health. / 恢复生命值。 / Stellt Gesundheit wieder her.”; **Aquaponic Fish Broth / 鱼菜共生鲜鱼汤 / Aquaponik-Fischbrühe** with “Restores the Defense Bar. / 恢复防御槽。 / Stellt die Verteidigungsleiste wieder her.”; **Fruit-Energy Gel / 水果能量胶 / Frucht-Energiegel** with “Restores the Attack Bar. / 恢复攻击槽。 / Stellt die Angriffsleiste wieder her.”; and **Balanced Village Meal / 村庄均衡餐 / Ausgewogene Dorfmahlzeit** with “Moderately restores Health, Defense Bar, and Attack Bar. / 适量恢复生命值、防御槽和攻击槽。 / Stellt Gesundheit, Verteidigungsleiste und Angriffsleiste teilweise wieder her.”

Pickup feedback is **Health restored / 生命值已恢复 / Gesundheit wiederhergestellt**, **Defense restored / 防御槽已恢复 / Verteidigung wiederhergestellt**, **Attack restored / 攻击槽已恢复 / Angriff wiederhergestellt**, and **All resources restored / 所有资源已恢复 / Alle Ressourcen wiederhergestellt**. If the balanced meal does not completely fill all bars, the last becomes **All resources partially restored / 所有资源已部分恢复 / Alle Ressourcen teilweise wiederhergestellt**.

#### Approved Stage 6 Academy curriculum

Four spoiler-safe lessons unlock with Stage 6: **Priorities under Pressure** teaches objective levels/warnings/conditions; **Ride, Dismount, Regroup** teaches bike Stability, defense, fallback and co-op regroup; **Finite Arms Discipline** teaches firearms, grenade, ammo, empty throws and transfer; **Break the Retrieval Formation** teaches Shield flanks, Technician interruption, empty-Gunner punishment and Recovery Specialist response. After first clear, **Direct the Helper** teaches all three support interventions/disruption recovery, and **Capture the Extraction Marshal** isolates each boss phase, shield/low/weapon/throw/final-extraction/live-capture counters.

Every lesson uses the unchanged engine with demonstration, guided practice, independent test and Free Practice. Deterministic training actors replace story people/evidence; solo receives configurable partner support for co-op concepts, while co-op is optional and unique-hero. Training-only weapon/ammo reset cannot transfer to scored modes. My Combos records legal Stage 6 weapon, aerial, ground, counter and handoff routes. Lessons/medals grant no power, access, Credits or rank. EN/zh-Hans/DE text, narration, captions, transcripts and diagrams support remapped German physical keys and active gamepads.

#### Approved Stage 6 shared-cheat interpretation profile

Stage 6 adds no code. `SOFTERBLOWS` and `SAFEHEART` affect ordinary Health only; `STEADYFEET` eligible on-foot knockdowns only; `IRONGUARD` hero Defense only; `BRIGHTSPIRIT` the legal Attack Bar; and `STRONGHANDS` eligible Health/Resolve damage—not shield Guard, bikes, ledgers, objectives or environmental force. `TRUSTYTOOLS` protects eligible melee durability only, never firearm ammo, grenades, heavy-gun heat/ammo or debris. `STEADYRIDE` preserves player-bike Stability while retaining reactions, controls, obstacles, boundaries and authored dismount/parking; enemy bikes remain unchanged.

`QUIETCROWD` lowers ordinary commitment/concurrency but retains the selected difficulty's Specialists, Breachers, operators, boss kit and discoveries. `FULLPANTRY` adds deterministic ordinary recovery only, never the balanced meal, branch provision, weapons, evidence or Museum items. `HOMECOMING` returns defeat to the latest exact anchor without improvement. `SAFEPASSAGE` displays **Not Applicable in This Stage** because mandatory people/evidence already cannot reach completion-blocking loss; it cannot preserve infrastructure, optional robots, cargo, bikes or collateral. Presets mark only that component unavailable.

Any code marks the run Cheat-Assisted. It retains Stage 7, Vanishing Survey, Chapter Challenge, difficulties, canon, mandatory records, discoveries and catalogue visibility but no competitive/economy/performance/prestige result. ASCII typing remains optional; localized cards support all inputs, and co-op requires mutual acceptance.

#### Approved Stage 6 Automatic Highlight exclusion

Automatic Highlight detection and screenshots remain exclusive to **Curated DLC Missions** as a pilot. Stage 6 and all eight main-story stages expose no Highlight toggle, run no real-time detector or automatic screenshot capture, reserve no performance/memory budget for it, and show only their normal score, rank, objectives, ledgers, achievements and records. Players retain ordinary manual platform screenshots. Lightweight authored replay bookmarks may identify major events such as a Protected bridge, robot preservation or Marshal capture, but contain no automatic image or localized highlight judgment. The existing Curated Mission pilot and later Curated DLC eligibility remain unchanged; any future campaign expansion requires a new explicit decision.

#### Approved Stage 6 cinematic, localization and accessibility plan

The hybrid presentation contains **Morning of Renewal**, a 24–32-second montage of several short silent Flow clips showing bridge traffic, solar work, electric utility vehicles, robots and evolved clothing; an in-engine/portrait/map **civil-protection briefing** with controlled localized anomaly UI; **The Line Is Cut**, using one or two small-motion Tomas-alone Flow clips plus localized evidence inserts and an in-engine Marshal notice; and **Leverage Retained**, an in-engine/illustrated ending showing live custody, secured terminal, earned infrastructure conditions, Tomas's departure/annex and Sheng Guo's unresolved-sponsor conclusion without blaming the heroes.

Flow uses approved start/end frames, one axis, small existing-frame motion, explicit position/gaze/hand/prop ownership and no generated speech, readable screens, subtitles, logos, signatures or watermarks. Complex movement, crowds, handovers and changes stay in engine/editing; after two failures, simplify the action rather than rewriting indefinitely. Audio/localization are added afterward and every clip has a still fallback.

English defaults with complete zh-Hans/DE subtitles, speaker labels, speech/narration, briefings/evidence, warnings/results, transcripts/Museum replay, SFX captions and directional descriptions. Optional Windows narration is a separate track and never replaces subtitles. Size/background, speaker color-plus-shape, narration/music/SFX/ambience volumes and reduced motion/flash/shake/particles/video are independent. Every sequence is separately skippable with a localized recap, and presentation/readiness time never affects scoring.

#### Approved Stage 6 music, ambience and SFX suite

The **A Village Tested Again** family contains **Renewal in Motion** (warm village modernization), **Across the New Bridge** (defense/pursuit), **Current under Strain** (stable/pressured/isolation Solar layers), **Freight Lines** (Logistics formations), **Hands of Tomorrow** (human-centered Maintenance/robot support), **The Recovery Order** (Terminal/evidence), three-phase beat-aligned **No One's Partner** (Marshal control, retrieval and withdrawal fracture), and unresolved ending **Leverage Retained**. Authored exploration/combat/objective/critical/victory stems transition on beat; optional branches receive confirmations rather than exclusive tracks.

Music ducks under alarms, firearm cues, Stability warnings, robot confirmations, extraction warning, dialogue and narration; it is never the sole gameplay cue. Zone-specific ambience covers humane district activity, bridge wind/traffic, safe energy systems, logistics machinery, maintenance servos and secure terminal equipment without masking direction. Fictional firearms, grenade, heavy gun, empty states, shield, channels, overrides, robots and bikes have distinct captioned/directional families at restrained loudness. Generated candidates require human editing, looping, provenance and creation-date commercial-use verification; no lyrics or language-dependent vocals enter the final score.

#### Approved Stage 6 Chapter Challenge and Run Archive contract

Any canonical Story clear, including Cheat-Assisted for access, permanently unlocks Stage 6 Chapter Challenge. Players choose unlocked difficulty, solo/co-op, unique hero/pair, Standard/Open Loadout, costume/presentation and declared assist/cheat eligibility. Every run starts fresh; full stage, nine encounters, boss, branches, specialists and ledgers remain, while the briefing may shorten and previously seen scenes default skippable. Canon cannot change, Automatic Highlights remain absent, and authored bookmarks remain allowed.

Every competitive completion archives date/active time; mode/difficulty/player/hero/loadout/moves; category score/total/rank/tiebreak; five ledgers/causes; branches/robots/cargo/bikes/weapons/ammo/provisions/collateral; specialists; boss statistics; checkpoint/suspend/recovery/reconnect/migration; integrity/assists/cheats; version and validation hashes. Cheat-Assisted, Continued, failed and practice history is separately noncompetitive. Personal-best comparison requires compatible mode/version, difficulty, player/hero configuration, loadout and integrity and shows category deltas; incompatible history is visibly **Legacy**, never recalculated.

Eligible compact replay capsules store inputs, authored bookmarks, transitions, authority and hashes rather than video. Compatible versions replay deterministically; incompatible versions show localized timeline/results. Replay cannot reward, alter records, recover Museum content or become a checkpoint. Profile export/import preserves records, capsules, labels and integrity history.

#### Approved Stage 6 release acceptance matrix

Release coverage includes all four solo heroes and six unique co-op pairs; five difficulties; Standard/Open/Assisted/Cheat-Assisted/Continued; keyboard/remapping including German physical placement, supported gamepads, same-device and local-Wi-Fi; supported browsers at 60-FPS target and stable 30-FPS minimum without simulation drift. It covers all six zones, nine encounters, branches, pursuit, support, Marshal phases, enemy/specialist roles, every reachable five-ledger result, warned-action boundary, bikes/fallbacks, robots, all weapon/ammo/grenade/heavy-gun/transfer/disarm/disposal states, provisions/containers/environment/vehicles/collateral, evidence split, fixed escape, custody and Stage 7 transition.

Persistence coverage includes all seven anchors, technical recovery, suspension, Continue/restart, reconnect/rejoin/migration/solo continuation, exact co-op authority, replay/Legacy, backup/import and migration with no improvement, duplication, loss or eligibility corruption. Result coverage includes every score/rank boundary, normalization/personal best, eight achievements/clues, all access/rewards/costumes, integrity exclusions, Museum/Chronicle/Archive. Presentation covers EN/zh-Hans/DE, Flow/still/skip/reduced video, screen readers/focus/remapping/non-color cues/subtitles/directional warnings/effect reductions, and complete audio/ducking at both performance targets.

Negative proof forbids civilian death; completion-blocking evidence loss; bridge/critical-service collapse; incidental ledger downgrade; impossible warnings; off-screen/infinite gunfire; infinite combos; lost/duplicated equipment; mount softlock; autonomous robot completion or premature Stage 7 summon; boss heavy gun; Marshal death/escape/skipped phase/missing capture; blamed Tomas escape or early leader disclosure; branch/co-op gates on S/canon/progression; Cheat rewards; Story Automatic Highlights; persistence duplication; or accessibility/presentation simulation changes. Release requires zero unresolved Critical/High defects. Exact frames, damage, thresholds, final assets and tuning remain production validation unless a fundamental conflict emerges.

**Stage 6 status:** Feature refinement and the full product/content/technical specification at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-02-fight-for-peace-stage-6-specification.md` were approved on 2026-11-02. It integrates all seven approved builder clarifications, and fresh follow-up review returned **nothing important left**. Implementation planning remains separate.

### Phase 2 — Create the complete stage breakdown — completed

After Phase 1, distribute the completed storyline across the required number of stages. Each stage receives a self-contained conflict and ending, one meaningful contribution to the larger campaign, an appropriate sponsor clue, and a controlled introduction of new heroes, enemies, locations, technology, and gameplay systems. Historical Chronicles are placed at the revelations that make them narratively necessary.

#### Approved sponsor-evidence progression

- **Stage 3:** A procurement batch reference and damaged invoice prove that Tomas's bandits received equipment through the same route family seen in Stages 1 and 2. The evidence identifies only the regional procurement cutout.
- **Stage 4:** Authenticated records from Tomas, Kael, and the Xu investigation connect that cutout to the commercial broker involved in the old formula dispute. This proves that Tomas deliberately reactivated an established intermediary relationship.
- **Stage 5:** Rovan's trial archive, freight records, and historical depot evidence reveal the old logistics infrastructure from which the modern intermediary system evolved. No contemporary Compact identity appears in the historical material.
- **Stage 6:** The abandoned-operation recovery order, concealed subsidies, and asset seizures connect Tomas's broker and procurement cutout to Damon's shell consultancy. They prove that a higher organization financed Tomas to study the village and always considered him disposable.
- **Stage 7:** Matching Han strategy, Rhea operations, and Damon acquisition records reveal the Meridian Compact, its two-of-three approval system, and its three leaders.
- **Stage 8:** The complete operational and contingency archives establish the Compact's full scope, identify victims and assets for restitution, and support individual accountability.

Each stage contributes one indispensable discovery. Later evidence expands or authenticates earlier clues rather than invalidating them, and the Compact's identity remains concealed until Stage 7.

#### Approved modern-stage chronology

- **Stage 3:** 18 months after Stage 2.
- **Stage 4:** Approximately 19.5 months after Stage 2, six weeks after Stage 3.
- **Stage 5:** Its present-day framing begins immediately after Stage 4's evidence review and adds no substantial modern time. Its playable events occur approximately forty years before Stage 1.
- **Stage 6:** 38 months after Stage 2, approximately eighteen months after Stage 4. This interval supports the next visible infrastructure and institutional-development step.
- **Stage 7:** 47 months after Stage 2, nine months after Stage 6.
- **Stage 8:** Approximately 48 months after Stage 2 and only days or weeks after Stage 7 identifies the Compact.

This spacing gives Kael about twenty-seven months between leaving Tomas in Stage 4 and voluntarily joining the village in Stage 7, allowing the Wayfarer Watch and its independent protection record to become credible. The short Stage 7–8 interval preserves urgency while the Compact's archives remain at risk. At the finale, Wind Sentinel and Fire Warden are approximately 22, Kael approximately 28, and Sheng Guo approximately 73.

#### Stage 4 opening and inciting evidence chain

**Confirmed stage identity:** **Stage 4 — The Weight of Inheritance / 传承之重 / Die Last des Erbes** is the campaign's evidence-road interception chapter. “Inheritance” refers to the grievance, manipulated history, family responsibility, and moral choice passed from Tomas to Kael rather than merely to blood descent. The chapter connects the authenticated Stage 3 invoice evidence to Stage 5's historical reconstruction without revealing Kael's later decision before the evidence review.

**Confirmed:** Stage 4 begins approximately six weeks after Stage 3. This interval allows Garran's damaged invoice and procurement batch reference to receive initial authentication while preserving the urgency of Tomas's escape and renewed intermediary contact.

The Regional Civil Protection Council orders a documented transfer to its evidence facility. The convoy carries Garran's damaged invoice and batch reference; Tomas's formula-dispute and broker records recovered through the Stage 2 investigation; independently preserved financial and legal records submitted under seal through Xu Lian's representative; and a neutral archivist's chain-of-custody report. Digital hashes and working copies already exist, but the sealed originals require physical transfer for formal evidentiary authentication. Their loss would delay lawful action rather than unrealistically erase every copy.

A neutral archive examiner accompanies the transport. Tomas falsely tells Kael that this examiner is a coerced witness and that the convoy carries fabricated evidence designed to condemn the Rell family. Before revealing that connection, a short opening cinematic shows Kael, Soren Beck, and Chen Yue protecting unrelated travelers from ordinary road bandits, treating injuries, and taking nothing from the defeated attackers. Tomas's courier then delivers the false account. Kael accepts it because it matches the history he was taught, but imposes strict nonlethal orders.

Mountain Guardian, River Scout, Wind Sentinel, and Fire Warden receive the lawful escort assignment along Changling Trade Road. After Kael's eventual capture, his courier instructions and communication records become the Kael portion of the approved Stage 4 evidence chain. Xu Lian's identity is disclosed to Kael only during the post-capture evidence review, preserving the emotional weight of her independent record and his later personal choice about meeting her.

**Confirmed Five-Act Chain-of-Custody Route:** Stage 4 opens at the **Council Transfer Yard**, where the evidence seals, neutral archivist, convoy-protection rules, and lawful assignment are established. The first combat section crosses **Changling Trade Road**, where a coordinated roadblock threatens travelers and attempts to interfere with the evidence. The convoy then reaches the **Damaged Waystation**, where the heroes open the main evacuation corridor while an optional service-annex branch permits direct evacuation of an additional trapped group; the evidence vehicle remains at a secured authored anchor rather than becoming persistent escort AI. **Broken-Road Pursuit** follows with motorbike pressure and an optional opportunity to recover a separated convoy recorder before environmental damage can compromise its condition. The final **Evidence-Facility Approach** reveals Soren and Chen Yue's growing doubt; Kael protects their withdrawal and nearby civilians, then remains for a full final confrontation and live capture.

The convoy moves only through authored transitions between secure anchors. This keeps chain of custody central without forcing continuous vehicle babysitting, gives each location a distinct protective objective, supports solo and co-op equally, and allows Kael's doubt to emerge through conduct before the post-capture evidence review.

**Confirmed Three-Part Chain-of-Custody Ledger:** The **Sealed Originals** resolve as **Secure**, **Disturbed**, or **Recovered**. They cannot be permanently destroyed or removed from the story; a temporary breach or hostile carrier moves the case into an authored recovery encounter, affects rank and bounded ending detail, and never erases the investigation. The **Archivist and Civilian Safety** track uses **Protected**, **Evacuating**, and **Cleared** short authored states without ordinary Health bars or death; a clearly marked interference may cause one bounded disruption penalty before evacuation completes. The supporting **Convoy Recorder** resolves as **Intact**, **Damaged**, or **Lost**. Intact recovery grants the best score, achievement progress, and Museum material; damaged recovery grants partial credit; environmental destruction remains completable because the sealed originals, authenticated hashes, and working copies contain all mandatory truth.

The Ledger creates protection stakes without a hidden failure timer, plot-erasing mistake, persistent escort AI, or mandatory restart. Essential evidence and the canonical review always survive, while player performance still changes rank, records, optional discoveries, and presentation.

**Confirmed Resolve-Limited Rival Appearances:** Soren Beck and Chen Yue participate as named rivals under shared combat rules but are not bosses, lieutenants, loot sources, or custody targets. On **Changling Trade Road**, Soren leads a controlled roadblock through defensive savate, precision kicks, and evasive positioning; when damaged roadside equipment endangers unrelated travelers, he disengages to warn and protect them rather than being conventionally defeated. At the **Damaged Waystation**, Chen attempts a non-injurious restraint of the archivist through judo and joint control; when civilians are injured by environmental damage, she abandons the interception, provides emergency care, and refuses to leave them unprotected.

Both appearances use readable, bounded Resolve exits and cannot be farmed. At the Evidence-Facility Approach, Soren and Chen openly urge withdrawal. Kael orders them to escort civilians away and remains to cover their retreat. His subsequent boss battle is complete and must be genuinely won; their moral decisions neither convert them into additional bosses nor make Kael's capture voluntary or trivial.

**Confirmed Kael boss structure:** Kael is a three-phase, empty-hand boss whose complete kit previews his later playable identity without granting Story access early. **Measured Pursuit** uses pressure boxing, compact combinations, body counters, low kicks, lane cutting, and disciplined forward movement. **Chain Control** adds clinch entries, linked takedowns, controlled ground transitions, short joint controls, and throws; failed entries have visible recovery and co-op holds permit partner rescue. **Burdened Resolve** begins after Soren and Chen leave with the civilians and tightens Kael's defense through Exact Blocks, Counter conversions, and one defense-consuming Combo Breaker without adding unexplained armour, rage immunity, or reinforcements.

Kael remains empty-handed, obeys shared Defense, scaling, anti-infinite, throw, breaker, ground, and co-op rules, and cannot trap both co-op heroes in one sequence. Evidence condition never changes his statistics. Victory requires genuine combat resolution followed by controlled incapacitation, nonlethal restraint, medical assessment, and live custody; it is not a scripted surrender.

**Confirmed Stage 4 enemy roster:** **Road Blocker** is a heavy positioning role that deploys or moves one declared roadside barrier and uses broad strikes and shoves to create space for evidence interference. **Baton Interceptor** is a mobile close-range role using quick checks, guard pressure, low attacks, and punishable counters. **Bike Pursuer** provides warned mounted lane pressure with separate Stability and safely dismounts into ordinary close combat at zero. **Restraint Controller** uses straps and joint-control tools for temporary hero holds; solo always has a breakout, co-op permits partner rescue, and protected actors cannot be restrained.

Extreme adds **Convoy Breaker**, whose clearly marked designated actions alone may worsen evidence-vehicle or supporting-equipment condition. Ultimate adds **Counter Grappler**, an advanced defensive throw and counter-bait role with short ground conversions but without Kael's full chain complexity or an inescapable loop. Solo permits one major objective action at a time; co-op permits at most two staggered actions against different targets. The faction follows Kael's nonlethal order, wears modern travel/work clothing, and uses ordinary road equipment without firearms, military kit, sponsor insignia, executions, or lethal crash behavior.

**Confirmed bounded motorbike pursuit:** Broken-Road Pursuit is a short authored mount section rather than a race, traffic simulation, or open-road mode. Solo receives one evidence-convoy utility bike; co-op provides one bike per hero so neither player becomes a passenger. Riders use readable depth lanes, capped acceleration, braking, hazard dodge, one limited mounted strike, and voluntary dismount through the inherited abstract mount controls.

Player and enemy bikes use separate Stability. Zero Stability always causes a safe nonlethal dismount. A dismounted hero continues through an authored on-foot roadside path, and the convoy slows at the next safe anchor so a lost bike never fails the stage or abandons a co-op partner. Mounted success improves pursuit score and the recorder's likely condition but cannot determine mandatory evidence survival. Bikes are stage-local, non-Store, non-persistent mounts; the section adds no firearm use, civilian collision, free roaming, or explosive crash.

**Confirmed two-tool road arsenal:** **Folding Road Baton** is a fast short-to-medium tool emphasizing quick recovery, interruption, guard pressure, and Resolve damage with unique standing, aerial, low, and ground routes but lower raw damage. **Tire Iron** is slower and heavier, emphasizing guard damage, knockback, low sweeps, and interactions with tagged road barriers, crates, and damaged vehicle panels; contextual interactions cannot damage sealed evidence or protected safety equipment.

Both tools use the shared Attack/directional Attack graph and `J` pickup, throw, and discard lifecycle, never auto-restrain or bypass Resolve, and appear only through Stage 4 racks, emergency kits, and eligible drops. A held tool is secured to the hero's bike or convoy rack during a mounted transition and restored at the safe dismount; mounted combat uses the universal mounted strike rather than separate tool-on-bike routes. Neither tool is pre-equipped, sold in the Store, or persisted between stages. Their later reuse in *The Medicine Road* is intentional shared-world continuity rather than a new weapon definition.

**Confirmed Deterministic Roadside Impact Network:** Council Transfer Yard uses handcarts, empty transport cases, and collapsible safety barriers for controlled wall reactions and light fragments while sealed evidence remains protected and non-interactive. Changling Trade Road uses road barriers, cargo crates, guardrails, and maintenance frames for throws, rebounds, and tagged Tire Iron interactions without opening lethal falls. Damaged Waystation uses benches, tables, shutters, luggage carts, shelving, and breakable cover while civilian shelter areas remain protected. Broken-Road Pursuit uses authored loose cargo, stones, and damaged barriers as clearly warned lane hazards with identical solo/co-op schedules. Evidence-Facility Approach uses security dividers, document carts, and one retractable access barrier without exposing authenticated evidence to damage.

Every environmental action follows shared damage scaling, Resolve, bounce, stun, and anti-infinite rules. It cannot crush civilians, strike protected custody actors, destroy mandatory evidence, block the only route, or cause lethal vehicle impacts. Enemy-caused and mandatory transition damage never becomes player collateral. Reduced-effects settings simplify debris, reflections, and camera motion while preserving warnings, collision, and timing.

**Confirmed Roadside Provision Set:** **Waystation Noodle Box / 驿站面餐盒 / Nudelbox der Raststation** restores Life; **Citrus Electrolyte Drink / 柑橘电解质饮料 / Zitrus-Elektrolytgetränk** restores Defense; **Roasted Nut Bar / 烤坚果棒 / Gerösteter Nussriegel** restores Attack Bar; and rare balanced **Convoy Lunchbox / 护送餐盒 / Proviantbox des Geleits** moderately restores all three. Effects are gameplay abstractions rather than medical claims.

The Medium solo baseline is three Life, two Defense, and two Attack-Bar items across safe anchors; co-op adds one common item in each category. Immediately before Kael, every active hero receives one ownership-assigned Convoy Lunchbox. Easy adds one of each common category; Hard, Extreme, and Ultimate remove one, two, and three common items without eliminating a category or the pre-boss lunchboxes. Supplies appear only at cleared convoy anchors and safe waystation areas, never during evacuation, mounted pursuit, restraint, or custody, and retain shared `J`, ownership, handoff, checkpoint, and anti-duplication rules.

**Confirmed Stage 4 optional branches:** **No One Left at the Waystation / 驿站不留一人 / Niemand bleibt an der Raststation** enters a damaged service annex after the mandatory main evacuation corridor opens. The heroes directly evacuate an additional trapped group before the Council team arrives; if skipped, the Council rescues them canonically. Completion prevents that branch's disruption penalty and reveals a text-free road-guardian training token left by Kael's group. **The Record Survives / 记录留存 / Die Aufzeichnung bleibt erhalten** uses visible, warned recorder-deterioration opportunities during Broken-Road Pursuit; Intact or Damaged recovery completes it at different score levels, while Lost remains a valid non-blocking outcome.

Completing both in one eligible run grants **People and Proof Preserved / 人证物证皆保全 / Menschen und Beweise bewahrt** and upgrades the Museum's early road-guardian exhibit. After the Wayfarer Watch is founded, the same exhibit gains commentary identifying the token's later significance. Neither branch gates S, Kael's capture, evidence review, Stage 5, or base Museum completion. Cheat-Assisted play may reveal Museum material but grants no branch achievement.

**Confirmed Chain-of-Custody Guardian Grade:** Stage 4 uses 100 points: Evidence and Chain-of-Custody Protection 40; Archivist, Traveler, and Civilian Safety 25; Combat and Nonlethal Control 25; Resource and Active-Time Efficiency 10. Candidate thresholds are C for a valid clear, B at 60, A at 75, and S at 90, with final validation by difficulty and solo/co-op profile.

A valid clear requires Kael's live custody, delivery of the authenticated originals, and safe arrival of the archivist and mandatory civilian groups. S requires the originals to finish Secure or Recovered, the archivist and mandatory evacuation corridor to finish without unresolved disruption, and collateral within the S band. One recovered temporary evidence breach or one resolved civilian disruption may coexist with S, but not both. Optional annex rescue and recorder recovery are not required for S; a Lost recorder reduces points without an automatic cap. Repeated avoidable evidence breaches cap A, and major avoidable protected-property damage caps B.

Optional-branch time is compensated. Pause, cinematics, loading, protected evacuation, custody, and validated network recovery do not count as active time. Restrained enemies, protected actors, repeated breakables, and scripted transitions cannot be farmed. Cheat-Assisted completion preserves campaign progress and canonical results but grants no competitive score, rank, Credits, or prestige reward.

**Confirmed Stage 4 achievement and reward catalogue:** Eight achievements are: **The Weight of Inheritance / 传承之重 / Die Last des Erbes** for an eligible clear; the two localized branch titles; **People and Proof Preserved** for both branches in one eligible run; **Unbroken Chain / 证据链无损 / Lückenlose Beweiskette** for keeping the sealed originals Secure; **Safe Testimony / 证人安然 / Sichere Aussage** for protecting the archivist and mandatory civilians without disruption or major protected-property damage; **Broken Road, Unbroken Ride / 断路疾行 / Ungebrochene Fahrt** for completing the pursuit without forced dismount and recovering the recorder Intact, with both riders qualifying in co-op; and optional co-op **Together on the Road / 同路并肩 / Gemeinsam auf der Straße** for both heroes performing a tagged partner rescue and clearing the stage.

Any canonical clear grants Stage 5 access, mandatory Stage 4 Chronicle/Museum records, and Store visibility for each of the four heroes' **Road and Convoy Guardian Outfit** and **Traveler's Homecoming Attire**; first eligible clear additionally grants itemized Credits. First A grants the text-free **Verified Passage** profile emblem; first S grants **Keeper of the Chain / 证据链守护者 / Hüter der Beweiskette**. Kael is not unlocked as playable here; his default and two Stage 4 alternatives remain hidden until the approved Wayfarer Watch unlock. Co-op is never required for S, progress, Museum completion, or solo rewards. Cheat-Assisted completion receives the approved progression, mandatory records, and catalogue visibility but no achievement, Credits, rank, emblem, title, or prestige reward.

**Confirmed Stage 4 presentation — Truth Under Seal / 封存之下的真相 / Wahrheit unter Siegel:** Stage 4 uses a hybrid cinematic and in-engine presentation. A short skippable opening shows Kael, Soren Beck, and Chen Yue protecting unrelated travelers and taking no reward before Tomas's courier delivers the manipulated convoy instructions. This establishes them as principled but misled opponents. Character-selected convoy briefing, evidence explanation, and all detailed localized information remain in-engine rather than depending on generated dialogue footage.

After Kael's genuine defeat and live capture, he is restrained, medically assessed, and treated with dignity. A neutral examiner connects four authenticated evidence groups: Garran's invoice and procurement batch; Tomas's pharmaceutical-formula dispute and broker trail; Xu Lian's sealed financial and legal records; and Tomas's instructions and communications to Kael. Sheng Guo states the moral distinction without simplification: Tomas's father committed a genuine betrayal, yet collective punishment and exile of the whole family were unjust; that injustice explains Tomas's pain but does not excuse his later fraud, manipulation, or violence. Kael accepts responsibility for the interception, suspends allegiance to Tomas, and chooses neutrality without instantly joining the village or receiving absolution. Xu Lian's identity is disclosed, but any later meeting remains Kael's voluntary choice. Conflicts in the historical record trigger the mandatory Stage 5 reconstruction.

Google Flow footage is limited to text-free, low-motion material already represented in the start and end frames: protected travelers at rest, courier arrival, evidence observation, and restrained emotional reactions. Large actions, evidence comparison, and factual exposition use authored editing and in-engine presentation. English, Simplified Chinese, and German subtitles and transcripts, still-image fallback, and full skip/review support are mandatory. Recorder and optional-branch states may add bounded inserts but never alter the canonical truth or Kael's decision.

**Confirmed Stage 4 music and SFX direction:** The **Burden of Blood** family retains seven adaptive cues: *Records in Transit*, *Doubt at the Waystation*, *Wheels under Pressure*, *Three Who Stand Together*, *Kael — Inherited Weight*, *Truth Under Seal*, and *A Road Chosen Freely*. Traveling strings, wheel-like percussion, restrained modern rhythm, unresolved harmony, and the gradually clarifying Wayfarer motif express responsibility and doubt; the sponsor motif remains a faint evidence-review color.
